SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/348168  2026-11-13 (submitted 2018-05-07, disclosed 2018-12-27; HackerOne (own program), "Timing attack towards endpoints on the web without CSRF"; reporter `b258ea62bf297b02afa9854` — credited as `mslavco` in the H1 team summary; weakness "Information Disclosure"; severity Low; body `visibility: full`)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the page (first route worked) + the live H1 `.json` via `curl` (`vulnerability_information` 7,145 chars read IN FULL). H1 team summary, verbatim: "In our testing the method was not reliable enough to exploit this in the wild, which is why no fix was prioritized."
STATUS: VERIFIED (adapted mechanism reproduced in a local lab this pass; the 2018 status-presence leg did NOT reproduce in modern Chromium)
CLASS: cross-origin information disclosure (XS-Leak) via the browser RESOURCE TIMING API against CSRF-less GET endpoints — the victim's browser loads attacker-chosen subresources with the victim's cookies and exposes each load's `duration` to the attacker page across origins (`duration` is NOT gated by `Timing-Allow-Origin`), turning data-dependent response time/size into a cross-origin read (CWE-203 / CWE-359).
CORE METHOD:
  1. TARGET CLASS (verbatim): "Every endpoint that lists data without CSRF token, but holds interesting conclusion like ( does one program have triaged reports atm )" — e.g. `https://hackerone.com/bugs.json?text_query=999999&subject=&sort_type=pg_search_rank&substates%5B%5D=triaged` ("always constant output for any logged in user ~750b").
  2. THE ORACLE (2018 shape): attacker page loads the endpoint as `<img src="…bugs.json?…">`, reads `performance.getEntriesByType("resource")`, computes `responseEnd - responseStart`; presence differential, verbatim: "200 response code will cause the script to measure the load time, if not load time won't be shown ( http 400, 500 status codes)".
  3. CALIBRATION + INFERENCE (verbatim): burst 30 cache-busted copies (`&rnd=`), "we can get conclusion even for the number of reports / records in the response if we divide the content size with average size of one (json) record"; existence ("if victim have created report in the last few days"). Reporter: "PoC calculations based on 30 requests per endpoint from my localhost and works like a charm".
  4. LAB (this pass; attacker page 127.0.0.1:8092 -> target 127.0.0.1:8091, no `Timing-Allow-Origin`; 1 KB / 200 KB / 200 KB + 400 ms server delay / 500 / 404 as `<img>` AND `<script>`; server-side hit log confirmed all 30 requests): (a) entries appear for 200 AND 404/500, `responseStatus` = 0 (TAO-gated) — the presence differential is DEAD in modern Chromium; (b) `responseStart` zeroed cross-origin -> use `entry.duration` (not TAO-restricted); (c) timing/size signal SURVIVES: `<script>` 1 KB = 27 ms vs 200 KB = 62-64 ms, 400 ms server delay = 489-492 ms (img same paths: 45-64 / 46-78 / 437-462 ms).
DETECTION SIGNAL: a GET endpoint with no validated CSRF token returning user-specific data whose processing time or response size varies with that data; no `SameSite` needed to observe; `Timing-Allow-Origin` NOT required.
CHAIN: victim logged in + visits attacker page (cross-origin subresource GETs carry ambient cookies) -> 30 cache-busted loads, `entry.duration` averaged, calibrated against known-size endpoints -> limited cross-user data read (demonstrated terminal: logged-in state, victim's report counts, per-program triage state — P3 floor). Chain-first-link per the chain rule: aim the oracle at existence/count inferences over sensitive objects (order counts, balance buckets). Program's own verdict kept honest: "not reliable enough to exploit this in the wild".
TRANSFERABLE TEST: (1) from a second origin load the endpoint 30x as `<script>`/`<img>` with `&rnd=`; (2) use `entry.duration` — never `responseEnd - responseStart`; (3) two query variants differing in size/DB work, compare BOTTOM QUARTILES; (4) calibrate duration deltas against two known-size endpoints -> byte deltas -> record counts. Rule: **if a data-dependent GET can be loaded as a subresource, `duration` survives SOP — size and server-side work become a cross-origin read.**
WHY IT IS A NEW GENERAL METHOD (§17.714): 0 dedupe hits. Nearest recorded: §17.174 (H2 response-ORDER oracle — different channel), §17.254 (DNS-cache/63-octet oracles + bottom-quartile rule). None records the Resource Timing API channel, the TAO zeroing pitfall, or size-calibrated record-count inference.
FRESHNESS: BACKFILL (2018); the `duration`-survives-SOP mechanism re-measured against current Chromium this pass.
HONESTY: local lab reproduced (no target touched); all quotes from the fetched report body/team summary; lab numbers are this pass's own.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/167489  2026-11-13 (submitted 2016-09-11, disclosed 2016-10-21; Yelp, "Bybass The Closing of the account and logged again to your account"; reporter `need_new_username_103`; weakness "Improper Authentication - Generic"; body `visibility: full`)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` + the live H1 `.json` via `curl` (`vulnerability_information` 1,061 chars read IN FULL).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced)
CLASS: AUTH-BYPASS / account-lifecycle credential gap — closing an account invalidates LOGIN but NOT outstanding password-reset tokens, and the reset-completion flow auto-issues a session without re-checking account state (CWE-613 / CWE-287).
CORE METHOD:
  1. PRE-POSITION (verbatim): "go to forget password page and get new password reset token and dnot use it".
  2. TRIGGER (verbatim): "go and make anything against the rules lead to close your account".
  3. BYPASS (verbatim): "using the reset password email you will go to the change password page / Enter the new password two times you will get in in your profile" — completion restores access AND issues a session ("after you reset your password the web app redirect you directly to your profile").
  4. RESIDUAL STATE (verbatim): "You can edit your privacy and password ,info but when you try to enter your email page the server will respond with 500 internal error" (+ 500 on write-review / edit-profile) — the closure flag is read per-branch.
  5. FIX (verbatim): "when you close the account make sure you expire all reset token associted with it".
DETECTION SIGNAL: account state transition enforced at exactly ONE gate (login); credentials minted before the transition (reset tokens, magic links, pending email-change tokens, API keys, OAuth grants) consumed later without re-reading account status; post-reset auto-login as the tell; 500s on some post-bypass actions = per-branch enforcement.
CHAIN: pre-position unused reset token -> closure/ban (expires login, not the token) -> complete the reset -> fresh session without state re-check -> closed account accessible again (demonstrated: edit privacy/password/info; per-account self-access only). Deletion-flows variant = deletion resurrection (the high-value chain).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: before ANY close/ban/suspend/delete, mint and HOLD one of each outstanding credential, apply the state change, then consume each artifact — any completion (reset -> auto-login counts) = one-gate enforcement; re-run against deletion flows. Rule: **an account state change must expire every credential minted before it, and every exchange flow must re-read account state before minting a session.**
WHY IT IS A DELTA (no §17 slot): the general method is recorded at ledger 10069-10077 (Reddit `offerId` — token not invalidated on state revocation) + credential-lifecycle family (ledger 8671, 635, 2885-2886). NEW atoms: auth-token/account-closure carrier, pre-positioning BEFORE the transition, auto-session completion gate, per-branch residual state as the tell.
FRESHNESS: BACKFILL (2016); mechanism version-independent.
HONESTY: not reproduced; all quotes from the fetched report body.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3874004  2026-11-13 (submitted 2026-07-19, disclosed 2026-09-15; Weblate; reporter `mask0ff`; weakness "OS Command Injection"; High (8.5); body `visibility: full`; CVE-2026-86035 / GHSA-327h-qqgm-qv55)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` (FIRST route worked — full body incl. code, preconditions, controls, remediation).
STATUS: VERIFIED (reproduced in a local Mercurial lab this pass — hg 6.7.2, no target touched)
CLASS: RCE / argument injection into a VCS CLI — a repository-controlled FILENAME placed BEFORE the `--` option terminator becomes a Mercurial option; `--config=alias.<subcommand>=!<shell>` replaces the invoked subcommand with a shell command (CWE-88 -> CWE-78)
CORE METHOD:
  1. SINK (report code, verbatim): `return self.execute(["cat", "--rev", revision, path], …)` — "Weblate passes the configured component template filename to `hg cat` without an option terminator"; "Component.template accepts relative filenames beginning with -" and Mercurial "interprets an option-shaped template filename as command-line configuration instead of a repository path".
  2. CALLER (verbatim): the "Update RESX files add-on passes component.template directly into this sink after a repository update": `content = component.repository.get_file(component.template, previous_head,)`.
  3. GADGET (verbatim): "Using Mercurial's `--config=alias.cat=!<command>` syntax, an attacker can replace `hg cat` with a shell alias and execute commands as the Weblate service account."
  4. PRECONDITIONS (verbatim): Mercurial + RESX + option-shaped template filename + Update RESX files add-on + a later repository update; a "non-superuser, non-staff account with project-scoped component.edit can set the template and install the compatible add-on through the real API even though management.addons is false" (403 before; `api_template_patch=200`, `api_addon_install=201` after).
  5. LAB (this pass): `hg cat --rev tip "--config=alias.cat=!touch <marker>"` (malicious string AS the path, no caller `--config`, no `--`) -> marker created (option became `--config`, alias hijacked `cat`); with the `--` terminator -> "`…: no such file in rev <id>`", no marker (path literal = the report's fix `["cat","--rev",revision,"--",path]`); benign control returned file content.
  6. RESIDUAL-SINK DISCIPLINE (verbatim): "The Weblate 4.11.1 fix hardened several Mercurial argument paths but did not fix HgRepository.get_file()"; "audit every Git and Mercurial call containing repository-derived path arguments and add[] a regression test using an option-shaped tracked filename."
DETECTION SIGNAL: a user-editable NAME (template filename, ref, branch, artifact name) reaching a VCS/CLI argv with NO `--` before it; the A/B: make the name option-shaped and read the tool's error text (literal-path error = safe; config/hook/alias behaviour = injected); argv-leaking errors name every injectable position.
CHAIN: project-scoped `component.edit` (low-privilege API capability) -> set `component.template` to an option-shaped filename + install the RESX add-on (value reaches `hg cat` argv before `--`) -> `--config=alias.cat=!<cmd>` hijacks the subcommand (argument injection -> command execution) -> next repository update runs the alias as the service account (impact: RCE as `uid=1000(weblate)` — repos, app data, env credentials, signing material; P1 per severity doctrine §6; program rated High 8.5).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every feature feeding a NAME/PATH into a VCS/CLI: set the name to `--config=alias.<subcommand>=!id` (hg) / `--config=hooks.<event>=<cmd>` (hg) / `--upload-pack=id` (git) / `-oProxyCommand=id` (ssh) / `--output=/tmp/marker` (tar/cp) and trigger the operation; if the request layer filters it, deliver it via an app FIELD or a tracked filename in a repo you control (raw/single/double-encoded rungs); when a VCS-argument-injection fix exists in the target, DIFF the patch and regression-test EVERY sibling call site with an option-shaped tracked filename; with `--` before the path the string must be a literal filename. Rule: **a repo-controlled name placed before `--` is a FLAG — one `--config=alias.<subcmd>=!<shell>` token turns any hg invocation into shell execution; after any argument-injection fix, sweep the SIBLING sinks.**
WHY IT IS A DELTA (no §17 slot): the general method is recorded (§13.4 branch name -> command execution; §13.7 "Test `-o`, `--upload-pack`, `--config`"; batch 230 #288704 = hg branch name `--config=hooks.pre-log=wget` -> RCE, its gadget `--config=hooks.<event>=<cmd>` noted "so no later pass re-opens it"; ledger 2640/2648 name-as-flag -> `--output=` -> RCE; ledger 3287 + 6041-6043 = the incomplete-fix residual-sink shape). NEW atoms: the `alias.<subcommand>=!<shell>` gadget (hijacks the EXACT subcommand, no hook event needed — verified first-hand), the missing `--` terminator as root cause + one-token fix (verified both directions), the app-TEXT-FIELD carrier (`component.template`), the residual-sink audit as a standing test.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2026-07-19, disclosed 2026-09-15 — INSIDE the window.
HONESTY: reproduced in a local lab this pass (hg 6.7.2, `/tmp/hglab_*`); all quotes from the fetched report body.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3761342  2026-11-13 (submitted 2026-05-25, disclosed 2026-07-29; Node.js; reporter `sy2n0`; weakness "Improper Access Control - Generic"; severity High; body `visibility: no-content`; CVE-2026-58043)
ROUTE: live `.json` (VI 0 B) — `summaries[0].content` (367 B) = the route that worked; corroborated via nodejs.org's July 29 2026 security-release advisory (read in full), the Red Hat CVE record, and — for the sibling root cause's lookup semantics + repro preconditions — nodejs.org's October 21 2026 advisory (CVE-2026-106071, "a different root cause from CVE-2026-58043") + nodejs/node issue #66943 (both read this pass).
STATUS: VERIFIED (honest split: the sibling wildcard short-path over-grant REPRODUCED FIRST-HAND on local Node v22.23.4; the assigned CVE-2026-58043 plain-prefix variant = MEASURED NEGATIVE on this post-fix build)
CLASS: sandbox/permission-model bypass -> file read/write outside the filesystem allowlist — a radix-tree path allowlist over-grants across node boundaries (CWE-551; sandbox escape / internal file read)
CORE METHOD:
  1. DEFECT (summary, verbatim): "A flaw in Node.js Permission Model enforcement can over-grant filesystem access across radix-tree prefix boundaries. Under `--permission`, an attacker who is granted access to one path can abuse boundary handling to read from or write to paths outside the intended filesystem allowlist." (affects main/22.x/24.x/26.x; fixed 2026-07-29.)
  2. LOOKUP MECHANISM (sibling CVE-2026-106071 advisory, verbatim — different root cause, same tree): "When the requested path is shorter than the prefix of a wildcard grant, the radix tree lookup stops comparing early and treats the path as a match. For example, `--allow-fs-read=/etc/passwd.*` also allows reading `/etc/passwd`" — "the only grant for that scope".
  3. REPRO PRECONDITIONS (nodejs/node #66943, verbatim): "the over-grant only reproduces with a single-entry tree … any second insertion into the same RadixTree … makes the `tes`-style boundary unreachable"; "Running the equivalent check via a script file instead of `-e` did not reproduce" (the script is a second insertion).
  4. LAB (host Node v22.23.4, measured this pass): single wildcard grant `--allow-fs-read=/…/secret.txt.*` + `node -e` -> reading `/…/secret.txt` GRANTED ("SECRET") = REPRODUCED; + second grant -> DENIED; script-file -> DENIED; stdin -> DENIED; divergent-prefix (`secret.ty.*` -> `secret.txt`) -> DENIED; past-boundary (`secret.txtX`) -> DENIED; exact literal prefix (`secret.txt.`) -> permission-granted, FS ENOENT (correct). Plain-grant boundary sweep (the 58043 class): control GRANTED, `gr` extensions/siblings/traversal all DENIED = measured negative on the patched build.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a sandbox/path-ACL matching paths via a tree or prefix matcher (Node `--permission`/`--allow-fs-read`/`--allow-fs-write`, Deno/Bun, path-ACL middleware); the oracle = a successful read/write at a strict truncation of the grant's literal prefix, one char past its boundary, or a shared-prefix sibling; record the tree SHAPE (over-grants so far only with a SINGLE grant).
CHAIN: a process under `--permission` with one granted path (the allowlist IS the sandbox) -> request a boundary truncation/extension of the grant (lookup stops comparing early -> over-grant) -> read/write outside the allowlist (impact: sandbox escape, arbitrary file read/write as the process user; C:H/I:H, S:C).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every prefix/tree path allowlist: sweep EVERY prefix length of one granted path (`/a`, `/a/`, `/a/b`, …) plus one-char-past-boundary extensions (`/a/b/cdefX`, `/a/b/cdef2/`, `/a/b/cdef/../out`); for WILDCARD grants probe paths SHORTER than the literal prefix (`/etc/passwd.*` -> `/etc/passwd`); repeat with a second grant added and via script-file vs `-e`; the oracle is the read/write attempt. Rule: **a prefix/tree allowlist must verify the BOUNDARY, not just the shared bytes — one granted path becomes a whole neighbourhood when the lookup stops comparing early.**
WHY IT IS NEW (§17.715): `radix`, `prefix boundary`, `over-grant`, `permission tree`, `58043`, `106071`, `sy2n0` = 0 technique hits. Nearest recorded: ledger 4601-4606 (Node permission model TYPE-coverage gap — string/`Buffer` vs `Uint8Array`) and ledger 5949-5960 (#2434811 monkey-patched `Buffer.from`/`utf8Write` -> §17.170) — mechanisms TYPE and INTERCEPTOR, not the matcher's boundary. NEW: prefix/tree allowlist boundary over-grant + its sweep, the wildcard short-path form (first-hand), the single-entry-tree precondition.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2026-05-25, disclosed 2026-07-29 (sibling CVE October 2026) — INSIDE the window.
HONESTY: wildcard short-path over-grant reproduced FIRST-HAND (local Node v22.23.4, no target touched); the assigned variant is a measured negative on that build (version-gated to 22.23.1/24.18.0/26.5.0); honest anomaly recorded: NVD lists CVE-2026-106071 as 26.x-only yet its probe shape reproduced on v22.23.4 here — version list may be incomplete; recorded as measured. All quotes from the fetched sources (H1 `summaries[0].content`, nodejs.org advisories, #66943, Red Hat record).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3611837  2026-11-13 (submitted 2026-03-17, disclosed 2026-06-16; Rocket.Chat; reporter `eldudareeno`; weakness "Improper Authentication - Generic"; severity High; body `visibility: no-content`)
ROUTE: live `.json` (VI 0 B) — `summaries[0].content` (477 B) = the route that worked.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Rocket.Chat stood up; mechanism quoted verbatim from the summary; DDP frame shape = standard Meteor protocol context, not a report quote)
CLASS: AUTH-BYPASS / BAC on a real-time RPC method plane — the authorization check is conditioned on the caller's IDENTITY BEING PRESENT, so an UNAUTHENTICATED connection (`Meteor.userId()` -> null) never reaches the check and execution falls through to the destructive sink (CWE-862 / CWE-287)
CORE METHOD (verbatim from the summary):
  1. "The deleteFileMessage Meteor method permanently deletes any uploaded file by ID without requiring authentication."
  2. "When called via an unauthenticated DDP WebSocket connection, Meteor.userId() returns null, causing the authorization check to be skipped."
  3. "Execution falls through to `FileUpload.getStore('Uploads').deleteById(fileID)`, which removes the file from storage and database unconditionally."
  4. "File IDs are discoverable from public channel message payloads and download URLs."
  5. Transport (Meteor DDP, protocol context): cookie-less WebSocket to `/websocket`, frame `{"msg":"method","id":"<n>","method":"deleteFileMessage","params":[<fileID>]}`.
DETECTION SIGNAL: RPC methods callable WITHOUT a session (DDP/WebSocket/GraphQL/JSON-RPC/gRPC); a guard shaped `if (userId && !allowed) throw` (identity-presence envelope); the tell = a mutating method that SUCCEEDS on a cookie-less connection; destructive siblings (delete/remove/revoke) + object ids in public payloads/download URLs.
CHAIN: unauthenticated caller (DDP socket needs no credential) -> `deleteFileMessage` with a public-harvested file id -> null userId skips the check (guard fails OPEN for anonymous) -> `deleteById(fileID)` removes file from storage AND database (impact: permanent unauthenticated cross-tenant destruction of any uploaded file — integrity loss at scale; program rated High).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: enumerate the RPC method handlers; call EVERY mutating method from a completely UNAUTHENTICATED transport (no cookie/token — not merely with a foreign id); the oracle is success where 401/403 is expected (`if (userId && …)` never fires for null); aim destructive methods at ids from public payloads/URLs; then repeat with a FOREIGN id from an authenticated session (the §17.345 leg — different bug). Rule: **an authorization check conditioned on identity PRESENCE fails open for anonymous callers — the unauthenticated call is the "no-check" case, not the "no-permission" case; exercise every mutating RPC method from a cookie-less socket.**
WHY IT IS NEW (§17.716): `3611837|eldudareeno|deleteFileMessage|deleteById|Meteor.userId() returns null` = 0 hits. Nearest recorded: §17.345 (Rocket.Chat `autoTranslate.translateMessage` — AUTHENTICATED + foreign id, REST fix missed on DDP; terminal READ), ledger 14718 (Rocket.Chat `ufsImportURL` — AUTHENTICATED foreign `userId`, carrier delta), §17.299 (Magentrix unauth REST delete-by-id — the guest ROLE holds the grant), §17.652 (Roxy-WI — auth check nested in `if <param> is None:`, bypassed by SUPPLYING the param), ledger 6695 (expiry-branch fail-open). None records "guard conditioned on identity presence -> anonymous falls through". NEW: null-identity fall-through, the unauthenticated RPC leg distinct from foreign-id replay, destructive permanent-delete terminal + public-id harvesting.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2026-03-17, disclosed 2026-06-16 — INSIDE the window.
HONESTY: not reproduced; all quotes from the fetched summary (H1 `summaries[0].content`).

NOTE — reader pass 2026-11-13 (batch 270 — §17 numbering: highest label in /root/Bugbountyskills.md at read time = §17.714; this pass appended 2 §17 slots — §17.715 from source 5, §17.716 from source 6, both genuinely NEW general methods). 6 assigned reports read in assigned order (1 BAC-label + 1 XSS-label + 1 RCE-label + 1 MEMORY-CORRUPTION-label + 1 BAC-label + 1 AUTH-BYPASS-label). 3 entries (this file + the harvest ledger) + 2 §17 slots. Per-source disposition: (1) #130460 (bitaccess "BYASSING OTP Verification", `geekboy`, submitted 2016-04-13, disclosed 2016-06-22, `visibility: no-content`; read via the live `.json` where `summaries[0].content` 143 B is the only public text) -> VALID, NOT APPENDED (EXACT DUPLICATE of V-07: "OTP was not properly checked on the session so it could have been bypassed by intercepting the server response and changing its value to valid" = V-07 "response manipulation" verbatim, Bugbountyskills 2859/2876; same standard as batch 154 Enjin #2312320). (2) #321 (HackerOne own program, "CSP not consistently applied", `janpaul123`, 2013-11-08/2013-11-30, VI 279 B read in full) -> VALID, NOT APPENDED (doctrine §2(c) CSP opinion excluded standalone + CHAIN-ONLY + carrier of a recorded class): verbatim "sometimes the CSP response headers served are missing for browsers that don't support them, but then the page without these headers can be cached by Cloudflare. This makes it easier to mount a XSS attack" — no XSS demonstrated (chain-only), and the mechanism (UA-capability-sniffed security header + shared cache not keying on it -> weakened variant served to all) is a carrier of ledger 9415's web-cache-poisoning class ("UNKEYED or OVERRIDDEN request header / cache-key differential … escalation stored XSS"). NOTE atom kept: capability-sniffed security headers + shared cache = mitigation-less variant cached for everyone — test every security header's UA/Vary dependence against the cache. (3) #3874004 -> ENTRY (DELTA over the recorded VCS argument-injection family). (4) #3709605 (Tor "Conflux-queued zero-length RELAY_END triggers heap out-of-bounds read", `geeknik`, 2026-05-02/2026-09-10, Medium 5.9, body + ASan trace read IN FULL) -> VALID, NOT APPENDED (CLASS OUTSIDE the recordable list + crash-only terminal `C:N/I:N/A:H` = doctrine §2(d) DoS): technique residue noted — zero-length `RELAY_END` is legal (`length==0` -> `END_STREAM_REASON_MISC`) but `connection_ap_process_end_not_open()` reads `get_uint8(msg->body)` BEFORE the length check while Conflux's `relay_msg_copy()` allocates `sizeof(relay_msg_t) + msg->length`, so for length 0 the body pointer lands past the allocation -> heap OOB read; fix = `msg->length > 0 ? get_uint8(msg->body) : END_STREAM_REASON_MISC`; the reusable shape (first field read before the length check + an exact-size copy path masking it) is a memory-corruption pattern outside this pipeline's classes. (5) #3761342 -> ENTRY + §17.715. (6) #3611837 -> ENTRY + §17.716. ROUTES: live `web_extract` x6 (full bodies for #3874004/#3709605/#321-sibling; keenable timeout on #321/#3761342/#3611837), live H1 `.json` via curl x6 (200 x6; `summaries[0].content` = the route that worked for #130460/#3761342/#3611837), parquet mirror (#130460 row), 1 web search (Node CVE landing), nodejs.org July + October advisories + nodejs/node #66943. TWO LOCAL LABS (no target touched): (a) hg 6.7.2 alias-gadget lab (#3874004: option-shaped path -> `--config=alias.cat=!…` -> marker created; `--`-terminated control -> literal path, no marker); (b) Node v22.23.4 permission-model boundary lab (#3761342: wildcard short-path over-grant reproduced on single grant + `-e`; second grant / script-file / stdin / divergent-prefix / past-boundary all DENIED; plain-prefix sweep all DENIED on the patched build). Dedupe greps scoped to the three write targets before every decision (tokens: `130460|geekboy|OTP|response manipulation|value to valid`; `321|janpaul123|CSP|cach.{0,40}CSP`; `3874004|mask0ff|Weblate|hg cat|mercurial|alias\.(cat)|--config|argument injection|option.?shaped|incomplete fix`; `3761342|sy2n0|allow-fs-read|radix|permission model|over-grant|prefix.?boundary`; `3611837|eldudareeno|deleteFileMessage|deleteById|DDP method|rocket\.chat|Meteor.userId`; `unauthenticated.{0,40}delet|fail.?open|fall.?through`) with context review of every hit (V-07 = source 1; ledger 9415 = source 2; batch-230 #288704 + §13.4/§13.7 + ledger 2640/2648/3287/6041-6043 = source 3; ledger 4601-4606 + 5949-5960 = source 5; §17.345 + ledger 14718 + §17.299 + §17.652 + ledger 6695 = source 6). SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3738654  2026-11-13 (submitted 2026-05-15, disclosed 2026-06-11; AWS VDP / Firecracker; reporter `terrynini38514`; weakness "Out-of-bounds Read"; "Firecracker Out-of-bounds Read/Write Local Privilege Escalation Vulnerability", a variant of CVE-2026-5747; severity None (0.0), no bounty per the live page)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the page — FIRST route worked (title, the vulnerable code blocks, the cached-pointer mechanism, the summary, the fix quote and the impact read in full; some PoC/log sections elided by the extractor). No fallback needed.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Firecracker/KVM lab this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the fetched report body)
CLASS: sandbox/container escape (VM guest -> host) via STATE-DESYNC ON SNAPSHOT RESTORE — a fix that gates privileged state mutation on runtime flags is bypassed by restoring a serialized state whose flags are individually valid but JOINTLY UNREACHABLE (device_activated=true while driver_status rolled back), reviving stale cached host pointers -> out-of-bounds read/write (stale-pointer / state-confusion class)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE PRE-FIX DEFECT (report, verbatim): in `common_config.rs` "queue configuration registers were written directly into the live `Queue` object without checking whether the device had already been activated"; queue activation "validates queue geometry only once and then caches raw host pointers derived from that geometry" (`initialize()` caches `desc_table_ptr`, `avail_ring_ptr`, `used_ring_ptr`); "After activation, later queue operations trust `self.size` for indexing, but still dereference the cached pointers. If the guest changes queue fields after activation, Firecracker can perform out-of-bounds virtqueue reads and writes through stale pointers." (the original CVE-2026-5747).
  2. THE FIX gates queue-field writes on activation state ("the queue configuration write gate in `update_queue_field()`").
  3. THE BYPASS (report summary, verbatim): "under hostile snapshot restore conditions, a guest can bypass the post-activation virtio PCI queue-write fix by restoring an active device with rolled-back PCI driver_status, reviving stale virtqueue pointer use and enabling out-of-bounds virtqueue access, including a host-crashing out-of-bounds used-ring write."
  4. ROOT CAUSE + FIX (the merged PR quoted in the report, verbatim): the restored combination "is unreachable during normal operation but could be crafted in a modified snapshot to bypass the queue configuration write gate in update_queue_field(). This could potentially allow post-activation queue mutations that lead to stale-pointer accesses." — the enhancement "rejects snapshots where device_activated is true but driver_status is not DRIVER_OK".
  5. THE GENERAL SHAPE: the live state machine enforces a cross-field invariant IMPLICITLY (activated => DRIVER_OK); the restore path validates fields individually, so a serialized snapshot can carry a flag combination the live machine forbids. Every gate keyed on the deserialized flag is bypassed, and derived data (pointers/sizes/handles) computed once at "activation" is dereferenced against attacker-mutated geometry. Impact (report, verbatim): "out-of-bounds memory reads and writes that cause host process crashes and potential access to host memory regions beyond intended guest boundaries."
DETECTION SIGNAL: any snapshot/checkpoint/restore, import, or persisted-state-load path that reconstructs a live object from serialized fields; a fix/gate keyed on a serialized FLAG (activated/started/ready/status/phase) rather than on revalidated state; an API/PR/comment asserting a combination is "unreachable during normal operation"; cached derived pointers/handles/sizes computed once and trusted afterwards.
CHAIN: guest supplies a snapshot file (primitive: restore accepts guest-controlled serialized state) -> restore an "active" device with rolled-back driver_status (primitive: cross-field invariant not re-checked on restore) -> the queue-write gate is bypassed and queue fields are mutated against cached host pointers (escalation: stale-pointer revival defeats the CVE-2026-5747 fix) -> OOB virtqueue read/write incl. a host-crashing used-ring write (impact: guest -> host memory access beyond the guest boundary, LPE in the VMM process per the report title; program's own impact quote above; the program merged the invariant check as defense-in-depth).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every restore/import/load-state feature (VM snapshots, container checkpoints, session/state import, job/workflow persistence): (1) enumerate the runtime flags the live code path gates on (activated/started/status/phase); (2) craft serialized state with flag combinations that are individually valid but jointly unreachable LIVE (active=true + pre-init status; started=true + empty/rolled-back geometry; two mutually exclusive phase flags); (3) restore it, then re-run the operations the gate was supposed to block (mutate the geometry/config after "activation"); (4) the oracle: an error/behaviour proving the gate was skipped, stale derived data was dereferenced, or an "unreachable" state was accepted. Rule: **a state machine reconstructed from serialized state must re-validate its cross-field invariants — the restore path is a second writer, and any fix that gates on a deserialized flag is bypassable by restoring a flag combination the live machine can never reach.**
WHY IT IS A NEW GENERAL METHOD (§17.717 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md): `snapshot restore|cross-field|invariant|unreachable state|state machine` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets (only unrelated entries: disk-image PARSER imports at ledger 2517-2524, business-logic state-machine WORKFLOW flaws at 2869/3680/6099/9986, and response-diffing "invariant attribute" at 7596-7602). The recorded deserialization material covers type-confusion/gadget chains, not invariant re-validation on a state-restore path. NEW axes: (a) the restore path as a SECOND WRITER bypassing activation-gated fixes; (b) the jointly-unreachable flag-combination craft; (c) stale derived pointers cached at "activation" as the memory-corruption consequence.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2026-05-15, disclosed 2026-06-11 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no Firecracker/KVM lab this pass); every quote above appears in the fetched report body (live `web_extract`, first route).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3608558  2026-11-13 (submitted 2026-03-16, disclosed 2026-05-30; phpBB; reporter `misop00p`; weakness SSRF; "Blind POST SSRF via Web Push Notification Endpoint"; Medium 5.9 `AV:N/AC:L/PR:L/UI:N/S:C/C:L/I:N/A:N`; fixed phpBB 4.0.0-a2 on 2026-04-27; no CVE — alpha release, per the program)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the page — FIRST route worked (summary, steps, code, the live PoC, impact, and the full staff/researcher triage discussion read in full).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no phpBB instance stood up this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the report)
CLASS: SSRF (blind POST) via a USER-SUPPLIED PUSH-SUBSCRIPTION ENDPOINT URL stored without validation — carried by a FEATURE-REPLACEMENT REGRESSION: the sink class phpBB removed (Jabber URL SSRF #1018568, admin-configured) returned in its replacement feature with a LOWER privilege floor
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE CARRIER (verbatim): "A registered user can register an arbitrary URL as their Web Push notification endpoint. phpBB stores this URL without validation and later uses the Minishlink WebPush library to send HTTP POST requests to it." — intercept the browser's subscription POST to `/user/push/subscribe`, replace the `endpoint` field with an internal URL (valid P-256 ECDH keys required — "trivially generated"); stored at `webpush.php:321-327` (`'endpoint' => $data['endpoint'], // no URL validation`).
  2. THE TRIGGER: any notification event for the attacker (a reply, quote or PM) — `notification/method/webpush.php:248-255` reads the endpoint from the DB and queues the push via Guzzle.
  3. THE OUTBOUND SHAPE (live PoC, verbatim): `POST /ssrf-poc HTTP/1.1` / `Host: attacker-server:9999` / `User-Agent: GuzzleHttp/7` / `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` / `Content-Encoding: aesgcm` / `Authorization: WebPush TOKEN...` / `Content-Length: 3070` — "The phpBB server sent a full Web Push protocol request (encrypted payload, VAPID JWT, ECDH headers) to the attacker-controlled URL."
  4. THE HONEST IMPACT BOUNDARY (program triage + the reporter's retraction, verbatim): the response body is not visible ("you'd need a separate response-reading primitive to actually exfiltrate anything from the metadata service"); the body is "a fixed 3KB WebPush-encrypted blob" so "the attacker can't meaningfully craft the content to target specific internal APIs"; the residual impact is "internal network probing: by varying the IP and port in the endpoint URL across multiple subscriptions, a registered user can infer which hosts and ports are reachable from the phpBB server, which is the standard utility of blind SSRF."
  5. THE REGRESSION-ON-REPLACEMENT (verbatim): "This is the same class of vulnerability as HackerOne #1018568 (SSRF in Jabber settings), but requires only a registered user rather than admin access. The Jabber feature was removed in phpBB 4.0 via migration; Web Push was added as its replacement."
  6. PRECONDITIONS (verbatim): webpush enabled by admin (`webpush_enable=1` + VAPID keys; `webpush_method_default_enable` defaults to `true`, so "once an admin enables it, all users have it active") + a registered account.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a push-subscription / webhook / notification-endpoint registration whose `endpoint` field accepts an absolute URL (the family's recorded detection signal at ledger ~13020); second signal — a NEW feature that re-implements a REMOVED one (changelog/migration: "removed X", "replaced by Y"); third signal — the attacker's listener receiving the service's own headers (`Authorization: WebPush <VAPID JWT>`, `Content-Encoding: aesgcm`) and a fixed-size body.
CHAIN: registered user (primitive) -> subscribe with `endpoint` = attacker/internal URL (primitive: no URL validation) -> any notification event makes the server POST a 3KB WebPush request carrying the service's VAPID JWT to that URL (escalation: authenticated server-side request to a caller-chosen destination) -> blind POST SSRF: internal host/port reachability probing from the server's vantage + the VAPID JWT lands on the attacker's listener (impact: internal network mapping; per the program's own triage NOT response read and NOT crafted internal API calls — the cloud-metadata/IAM leg requires a separate response-read primitive).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: the push-subscription register-and-listen test is already recorded (ledger ~13036). NEW standing test from this source: (1) diff the target's changelog/migration notes for REMOVED features ("removed Jabber/XMPP/legacy webhook for security") and locate the REPLACEMENT feature; (2) re-run the removed feature's sink test (user-supplied URL -> server fetch/invoke) against the replacement; (3) expect a LOWER privilege floor (admin -> registered user) and record who can now set the URL; (4) rate the result by the honest impact boundary: response read back = full-read SSRF (P1/P2); listener-only + fixed body = blind reach/probing only. Rule: **when a feature is removed for security, its replacement is the first place to re-test the same sink — the sink class comes back with a lower privilege floor; and rate blind SSRF by what the request actually carries, not by what the URL could theoretically reach.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over the recorded push-subscription SSRF family — NO §17 slot. Recorded neighbour REVIEWED: the Chaturbate #411865 entry (ledger ~13020-13055: `POST /notifications/update_push/` with an absolute `endpoint` URL, server fetch forwards Crypto-Key/Encryption/Authorization; transferable test "on ANY push-subscription / webhook-registration / notification-endpoint feature"; its own disposition: "NO §17 slot: the general method ... is already recorded; this is a concrete carrier + header set") — the carrier and the register-endpoint test are that entry's verbatim subject. NEW atoms kept: (a) the FEATURE-REPLACEMENT REGRESSION rule (a removed sink class returns in the replacement with a lower privilege floor) — grep-confirmed new (no feature-replacement/reintroduce technique hit in the three write targets; the `regression` hits at ledger 8475/16654/19866 are fix-incompleteness atoms, different mechanism); (b) the exact blind-POST WebPush outbound shape (fixed 3KB `aesgcm` body + `Authorization: WebPush <VAPID JWT>`) with the honest impact boundary recorded by the program's own triage; (c) the precondition nuance `webpush_method_default_enable` default true = one admin enable activates it for every user.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2026-03-16, disclosed 2026-05-30 — INSIDE the preferred window.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no phpBB stood up this pass); every quote above appears in the fetched report body (live `web_extract`, first route), including the triage exchange where the reporter retracts the metadata/internal-action claims.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2829063  2026-11-13 (submitted 2024-11-08, disclosed 2024-12-11; curl; credited advisory reporter Harry Sintonen; weakness Information Disclosure; "CVE-2024-11053: netrc + redirect credential leak"; body `visibility: full`, VI 1,121 B)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` -> SPA chrome only; live H1 `.json` via `curl` -> HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, body read IN FULL — THE ROUTE THAT WORKED.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no curl <= 8.11.0 stepped back this pass; the repro is verbatim from the report)
CLASS: sensitive info disclosure / credential leak — a per-host credential store matched on a PARTIAL match inherits the PREVIOUS host's secret across a redirect boundary (CWE-200 / CWE-522)
CORE METHOD (verbatim):
  1. Setup: two sites `https://a` (301 -> `https://b`); netrc: `machine a / login alice / password alicespassword` + `default / login bob`.
  2. `curl -L --netrc-file netrc -v https://a` -> "Credentials `bob:alicespassword` will be sent to `https://b`." — the LOGIN comes from the `default` entry for host b while the PASSWORD is carried over from host a's entry (a mixed pair).
  3. THE TRIGGER CONDITION (verbatim): "This flaw manifests itself if the netrc file matches the redirect target, but the entry omits password or both login & password."
  4. THE LEAK GRADES (verbatim): "If the login is specified for the redirect target host in netrc, only the password is leaked, if neither login or password is specified full credentials are leaked."
  5. THE FIX SHAPE (verbatim): "When parsing netrc file during redirect, if a match is found clear the previous username and password."
DETECTION SIGNAL: any client/tool/SDK with a per-host credential store (netrc, git credential helper, .npmrc/.pypirc, docker config, curl config `--user`) combined with redirect-following (`-L`, HTTP client redirects, webhook delivery follows); the tell is a PARTIAL entry for the redirect target (`default` line; `machine b` with login only; `machine b` with neither) + a cross-host redirect; the oracle is the credential pair arriving at the second host.
CHAIN: host a holds netrc credentials (primitive: stored secret bound to a host) -> a redirect to host b whose netrc entry matches but omits the password (primitive: partial match + no credential reset on host change) -> curl assembles `bob:alicespassword` — login from the target's entry, password leaked from the source host (escalation: credentials cross a trust boundary on the SAME scheme, no user gesture) -> the pair is delivered to whatever host the redirect names (impact: credential theft to an attacker-controlled redirect target; the recorded family's terminal).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every redirect-following client with a per-host credential store: (1) give it host-A credentials and a credential entry for host B that is PARTIAL (`default` line; `machine b` with login only; `machine b` with neither); (2) have host A 301 to host B and watch B's listener for the credential pair; (3) test SAME-SCHEME (http->http / https->https) cross-host FIRST — the leak is on HOST change, scheme change is NOT required; (4) grade the leak: password-only vs full pair; (5) the fix test: on a host change the client must clear the previous username/password before the new lookup. Rule: **a per-host credential lookup that falls back on a PARTIAL match inherits the previous host's secret — credentials must be dropped on a new HOST (not just a new scheme), and a password-less entry is enough to trigger the carry-over.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA materialising the lead held at the #2912277 disposition (ledger ~14483-14495), which read the sibling curl report for the SAME CVE and declined re-appending with: "DELTA noted for the next pass: the .netrc variant leaks on a SAME-SCHEME (http->http) cross-host redirect, so the recorded test's 'cross-scheme' framing is narrower than the real leak condition — the rule that matters is 'drop credentials on a new HOST'. Held as a lead; no entry, no §17 slot." Base family recorded at ledger ~5990 (#1551586 / CVE-2022-27774: "point a redirect-following client at your own host and have it 301 to a DIFFERENT host ... watch your listener for Authorization/USER/PASS/cookies"). NEW atoms kept from #2829063: (a) the PARTIAL-MATCH trigger — a `default` line or a password-less (or wholly empty) machine entry is the carry-over condition; (b) the MIXED-PAIR output (`bob:alicespassword` — login from one source, password from another) as the exact detection shape; (c) the two leak grades (password-only vs full pair); (d) the same-scheme host-change rule correction — this pass consummates the held lead. NO §17 slot (new anchor for the recorded credential-on-redirect general method; batch-268 precedent).
FRESHNESS: submitted 2024-11-08, disclosed 2024-12-11 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (about 2 years); recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned and because the previous pass explicitly held this delta; the rule is version-independent (curl 8.11.1 fixes it, but every credential-store + redirect-following client is in scope of the test).
HONESTY: not reproduced (no vulnerable curl build exercised this pass); every quote above appears in the fetched report body (live `.json`, VI 1,121 B).

NOTE — reader pass 2026-11-13 (batch 271 — numbered after the batch 270 NOTE in this file's tail; §17 numbering: highest label in /root/Bugbountyskills.md at read time = §17.716, and this pass appended 1 §17 slot — §17.717, from source 1, a genuinely NEW general method). Knowledge intake only. 6 assigned HackerOne reports read ONE BY ONE in the given order (1 MEMORY-CORRUPTION-label + 1 SSRF-label + 1 INTERNAL-FILE-READ-label + 1 BAC-label + 1 XSS-label + 1 INTERNAL-FILE-READ-label). 3 entries appended (this ledger + methodologies-2026-11-13.md) + 1 §17 slot. Per-source disposition, in assigned order: (1) #3738654 -> ENTRY (first block above; sandbox-escape class; NEW general method -> §17.717). (2) #3608558 -> ENTRY (second block above; DELTA over the recorded push-subscription SSRF family — no §17 slot). (3) #3290630 (GitHub, "Sample report: Denial of service", reporter `ghbountyocto` = hubot, submitted/disclosed 2025-08-07, weakness mislabeled "LLM06: Sensitive Information Disclosure", `visibility: full` but the body is "Sample test report." / "Sample report" placeholders) — SKIPPED/INVALID (it is GitHub's own sample-report template, teaches no technique; noted so no later pass re-opens #3290630). (4) #2610478 (MTN Group, "Unauthenticated phpinfo()files could lead to ability file read at h2f54.n1.ips.mtn.co.ug [/dashboard/]", created 2024-07-18, disclosed 2025-02-20, weakness "Violation of Secure Design Principles"; `visibility: full`, VI 1,321 B read IN FULL via the live `.json` after `web_extract` returned SPA chrome) — VALID but NOT APPENDED (family duplicate + chain-only with NO documented chain): the method, verbatim, is "Visit the target scope is https://h2f54.n1.ips.mtn.co.ug / You can used `burp-suite-intruder` for finding sensitive directory / And now we found a directory is `info.php` / Let's see opened in our browser is directory https://h2f54.n1.ips.mtn.co.ug/dashboard/phpinfo.php / You can see this page can be view without authenticate" with the demonstrated impact "The remote web server contains a PHP script that is prone to an information disclosure attack" (PoC banner: "Windows NT SUNSVR 6.3 build 9600 (Windows Server 2012 R2 Datacenter Edition) AMD64") — the directory-bruteforce-finds-phpinfo technique is recorded (Bugbountyskills 4893 wordlists `/phpinfo.php`/`/info.php`, 6642/7626 phpinfo as chain material for `session.save_path`, §17.535), and the title's "could lead to ability file read" chain is nowhere documented in the body = doctrine chain-only rule (same standard as the batch-182 decline of the sibling MTN report #2764952 and the batch-223 decline of #761790). Noted so no later pass re-opens #2610478. (5) #2389565 (Ruby on Rails / IBB, "Action Text XSS (Rails 7.1.x)", reporter `ooooooo_q`, submitted 2024-02-25, disclosed 2025-02-04; `visibility: full`, VI 1,612 B read IN FULL) — VALID but NOT APPENDED (EXACT DUPLICATE — this exact report is ALREADY recorded verbatim): ledger 5357-5376 is the #2542806 entry whose technique lives "in the PARENT report it links, https://hackerone.com/reports/2389565" — same lab setup (Rails 7.1.3.2 scaffold + `rich_text`), same payload `<action-text-attachment content-type="text/html" content="' + escapeHTML(html) + '"></action-text-attachment>`, same show-vs-edit asymmetry, same rails/rails#45739 attribution ("not reproduced in Rails 7.0") — plus §17.164 ("the editor's EDIT route re-hydrates stored markup and sanitizes less than the READ route — deliver the framework's own attachment ELEMENT"). Noted so no later pass re-opens #2389565. (6) #2829063 -> ENTRY (third block above; DELTA materialising the lead held at the #2912277 disposition — no §17 slot). ROUTES this pass: `web_extract` on all six live report URLs in assigned order (first route worked for #3738654/#3608558/#3290630; #2610478/#2389565/#2829063 returned SPA chrome), the live H1 `.json` via `curl` (HTTP 200 x3 — `visibility: full`, VI read IN FULL for #2610478 1,321 B / #2389565 1,612 B / #2829063 1,121 B — THE ROUTE THAT WORKED for those three). No lab this pass (source 1 would need a Firecracker/KVM snapshot environment, source 6 a pre-8.11.1 curl build — both marked UNVERIFIED rather than half-verified). Local dedupe greps scoped to the three write targets before every decision (tokens: `3738654|Firecracker|snapshot.?restore|driver_status|device_activated|virtqueue|stale.?pointer`; `3608558|phpbb|web.?push|push.?subscription|Minishlink` + `webhook.{0,60}SSRF|callback.{0,60}SSRF`; `2610478|phpinfo`; `2389565|action.?text|action-text-attachment|Trix`; `2829063|netrc|11053`; plus `state.?machine|cross-field|invariant|restore.{0,40}(state|snapshot)|unreachable state` and `feature.?replacement|reintroduc|regression`) with context review of every hit (ledger ~13020-13055 = source 2's decisive family; ledger ~14483-14495 + ~5990 = source 6's delta base; ledger 5357-5376 + §17.164 = source 5's decisive duplicate; Bugbountyskills 4893/6642/7626/§17.535 = source 4's family; ledger 2517-2524/2869/3680/6099/9986/7596-7602 + Bugbountyskills 21499 = source 1's reviewed neighbours). NEW: 3 entries (sources 1, 2, 6), 1 §17 slot (§17.717 — a state machine reconstructed from serialized state must re-validate its cross-field invariants; the restore path is a second writer). Doctrine framing: source 1's demonstrated terminal is guest->host OOB read/write with host crash and "potential access to host memory regions beyond intended guest boundaries" (sandbox escape; report title claims LPE; severity doctrine P1 class, program rated None — recorded as the report states it, not inflated); source 2's terminal is blind POST SSRF = internal reachability probing only (P2 floor with demonstrated internal reach; the metadata/credential leg explicitly retracted in triage); source 6's terminal is credential theft to a redirect target (sensitive info disclosure with PoC). SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2401359  2026-11-13 (submitted 2024-03-04, disclosed 2024-04-28; Internet Bug Bounty / Apache Airflow; reporter `parantheses`; weakness "Improper Authentication - Generic"; "CVE-2024-25128: Apache Airflow: Authentication Bypass when Legacy OpenID(2.0) is in use as AUTH_TYPE"; team rating medium — report verbatim: "The Impact is `Critical` but as OpenID(2.0) is a legacy mechanism, the severity was lowered to `Medium` on the basis of low usage probability"; Airflow < 2.8.2, Flask-AppBuilder GHSA-j2pw-vp55-fqqj)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the report page -> SPA chrome / keenable timeout; live H1 `.json` via `curl` -> HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI 2,433 B read IN FULL + team summary 796 B — THE ROUTE THAT WORKED.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Airflow/Flask-AppBuilder OpenID-2.0 lab this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the fetched report body or team summary)
CLASS: authentication bypass / ATO via a ROGUE IDENTITY PROVIDER in a legacy federation login — the login form takes the identity-provider URL as a CLIENT-SUPPLIED body parameter, and the configured "allowed IDPs" list is never enforced at request time, so the backend can be deceived into trusting an arbitrary OpenID 2.0 IdP that asserts any existing account's identity (CWE-287)
CORE METHOD:
  1. PRECONDITION (verbatim): "`AUTH_TYPE = AUTH_OID` should be defined" in the Airflow/Flask-AppBuilder config plus the allowed-providers lines uncommented — the deployment config SHIPS "a predefined list of allowed IDPs" (a trusted-IdP allowlist).
  2. THE DEFECT (verbatim): "normally the backend should have checked for provided values(idp urls) from client with the allowed idp list in backend, but didn't" — the allowlist is decorative.
  3. THE ENTRY POINT (verbatim): "The one body parameter of this `POST` request to `/login/` page, `openid` is used to define IDP provider Url. An attacker could change this url to their malicious IDP and can make a `fake` authentication and deceive the backend to trust it(as the `allowed providers` check wasn't properly done)."
  4. THE EXPLOIT (report, verbatim): "it is possible for an attacker to forge authentication to any existing account in the Target Airflow installation. This was possible via deceiving the backend of app to trust arbitrary OpenID 2.0 Identity Provider(even if the provider is not in the trusted IDP list in config). In conclusion, an attacker could deploy their own IDP and could alter the target app's authentication fully and gain unauthorized access." The report's quick-PoC route points `openid` at the PUBLIC IdP `https://openstackid.org`; "After successful auth with provider, the attacker will be redirected to Airflow and logged in as target existing account" — "Leading to Full Account Hijacking".
  5. TEAM ADVISORY (verbatim): "When Flask-AppBuilder configuration is set to `AUTH_TYPE` set to `AUTH_OID`, it allows an attacker to forge an HTTP request that could deceive the backend into using any requested OpenID service. This vulnerability could grant an attacker unauthorised privilege access if a custom OpenID service is deployed by the attacker and accessible by the backend." (note: OpenID 2.0 legacy mechanism, NOT OIDC.)
DETECTION SIGNAL: a federation/SSO login whose provider or issuer is chosen by the CLIENT (a body/query parameter naming an IdP URL/identifier — here `openid`; family shapes `provider=`, `idp=`, `iss=`, `realm=`, `domain=`) while the deployment config ships a TRUSTED-IdP ALLOWLIST; the tell is that the allowlist is enforced nowhere at request time — an UNLISTED provider URL is accepted. Cheap oracle: the login completes against an IdP absent from the configured list.
CHAIN: legacy federation login enabled (precondition: `AUTH_TYPE = AUTH_OID`, allowlist configured) -> tamper `POST /login/` `openid` to an attacker-deployed (or any public) OpenID 2.0 IdP (primitive: provider URL is client-supplied) -> the allowlist check is never performed ("the `allowed providers` check wasn't properly done") so the backend runs the auth flow against the attacker's IdP (primitive: decorative trust list) -> the attacker's IdP asserts the target existing account's identity and the backend mints a session for it (escalation: forged authentication to ANY existing account) -> "Full Account Hijacking" of any account in the installation (impact: full ATO incl. admin, report's own words; team rated moderate only because OpenID 2.0 is legacy/low-usage — the method class is not).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every federation/SSO login where the provider/issuer is client-selectable: (1) map which parameter carries the IdP URL/identifier and what allowlist the config claims to enforce; (2) send an IdP value NOT in the allowlist (your own `https://<collab>/` endpoint first, then any real public IdP); (3) complete the flow and observe WHICH session you land in; (4) re-run asserting a victim/admin identity from your own IdP. Oracle: a completed login against an unlisted provider = the allowlist is decorative. Rule: **a trust list is only a control if the request-time check consults it — always test the NEGATIVE case (an unlisted provider/value) against every allowlist-gated auth flow, and a rogue-IdP login that lands as an existing account is a full ATO, not a config finding.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over the recorded rogue-issuer federation family — NO §17 slot. Recorded neighbour REVIEWED: ledger 5707-5714 (Nextcloud `user_oidc` ID4me — the RP resolves a user-supplied IDENTIFIER to its AUTHORITY and trusts its discovery/JWKS; transferable test "on any federated login where you can influence the issuer (self-service IdP config, an ID4me/email-domain identifier, an OIDC 'custom provider' field, a mobile/BFF login), point it at YOUR OWN `.well-known/openid-configuration` + your own JWKS; mint an `id_token`... asserting the victim's `sub`/email, then walk the callback and see WHICH session you land in") — the general method (point the federated login at your own IdP, assert a victim identity, land in their session) is that entry's verbatim subject, so no §17 slot; also REVIEWED: ledger 6659's neighbour note ("IDENTITY-PROVIDER allowlist gap", ledger ~2509-2511 — a social login accepting ANY Google account: an account-DOMAIN allowlist gap), the SAML entityId-normalization entry (ledger 2743), §17.210 (org-claim) — different mechanisms. NEW atoms kept: (a) the DECORATIVE ALLOWLIST — the config SHIPS a trusted-IdP list and the request-time check never consults it ("the `allowed providers` check wasn't properly done"), so the test is the NEGATIVE case (unlisted provider accepted), a different observation from "no allowlist exists"; (b) the exact carrier: legacy OpenID **2.0** (`AUTH_OID`, NOT OIDC) with `POST /login/` body param `openid` = the IdP URL (Flask-AppBuilder; Airflow < 2.8.2; CVE-2024-25128 / GHSA-j2pw-vp55-fqqj); (c) the quick-PoC nuance that a REAL PUBLIC IdP (`https://openstackid.org`) suffices to demonstrate the deceptive login — a self-hosted IdP is only needed for arbitrary-identity assertion.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2024-03-04, disclosed 2024-04-28 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~2.5 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned; the method is version-independent (class: client-chosen IdP + unenforced allowlist in any legacy/OIDC federation login).
HONESTY: not reproduced (no Airflow stood up this pass); every quote above appears in the fetched report body (live `.json`, VI 2,433 B) or the team summary in the same payload.
SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2032778  2026-11-13 (submitted 2023-06-20, disclosed 2023-07-05; HackerOne — internal machine learning API; reporter `jobert`; weakness Path Traversal; "Internal machine learning API endpoint for CWE classification is vulnerable to path traversal")
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the report page -> SPA chrome only; live H1 `.json` via `curl` -> HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI 2,395 B read IN FULL — THE ROUTE THAT WORKED.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no ML API/lab stood up this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the fetched report body)
CLASS: LFI/path traversal in JSON body parameters that SELECT THE MODEL DIRECTORY for a deserializing loader -> arbitrary-directory load; paired with any file-write primitive -> RCE through the app's own trusted loader (CWE-22 -> CWE-502)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE ENTRY POINT (verbatim): the inference endpoint `POST /predict/report_weakness_id` ("An endpoint to suggest report's weakness id") on the internal ML API — "The `request.version` and `request.trained_at` parameters are both vulnerable to path traversal."
  2. THE SINK (report code, verbatim): `model_dirpath = pathlib.Path(f"{os.path.dirname(__file__)}/../models/report_weakness_id/{request.version}/{request.trained_at}/")` then `tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_dirpath, use_fast=True)` — "The `version` and `trained_at` inputs are interpolated directly into the path, as can be seen on line 29."
  3. THE PROBE (report's verbatim curl, run "inside the local Docker container"): `curl -X POST http://localhost:8082/predict/report_weakness_id -H 'content-type: application/json' -d'{"version":"v1", "trained_at": "2023-01-01T00:00:00Z/../../..", "input": [{"title": "test xss", "num_of_top_predictions": 3}]}'` — and the twin `"version":"v1/../../../.."` with a fixed `trained_at` (the f-string's trailing `/` means a `trained_at`-rooted traversal needs one `..` fewer than a `version`-rooted one).
  4. THE ESCALATION (report's Impact, verbatim): "An attacker would be able to execute arbitrary python code if they were able to get a joblib file onto the ML API (i.e. as a temporary file)." — the loader deserializes (joblib/pickle family) whatever the traversed directory contains; the RCE leg is conditional on a file-write primitive (the report names "a temporary file"), stated honestly here as the report's own precondition.
DETECTION SIGNAL: inference / model-load / tokenizer-load / template / plugin LOAD endpoints whose body/query params (`version`, `trained_at`, `model`, `revision`, `checkpoint`, `dataset`, `template`) are path SEGMENTS interpolated into a directory handed to a deserializing loader (`AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained`, `joblib.load`, `torch.load`, `pickle.load`, `load_model`); the tell is a date/version-shaped param accepting an embedded `/../` as one segment (no segment validation); the oracle is the loader's error naming the escaped directory.
CHAIN: caller of the inference API -> `trained_at`/`version` = `<valid segment>/../../..` (primitive: body params interpolated into the model dir path, no segment validation) -> `AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained()` opens a directory OUTSIDE the models root, anywhere the service user can read (escalation: the app's own trusted loader is redirected off-root) -> any joblib/pickle file planted in that directory (a temp-file/upload primitive — the report's "as a temporary file") is deserialized on the next call -> arbitrary python code execution in the ML API process (impact: RCE; the file-write leg is the documented precondition, not assumed).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every model/tokenizer/template/plugin LOAD endpoint: (1) fuzz each path-shaped body param with `<segment>/../` x1..N (JSON body needs no URL encoding) and read the loader's error as a path oracle (escaped dir named in the error / different error for existing vs non-existing dir); (2) aim the traversal at every directory a file-write primitive on the same service can reach (upload dir, /tmp via any temp-file feature, workspace/attachment dirs); (3) plant the loader's expected filename containing a pickle/joblib `__reduce__` payload; (4) re-trigger the load — code execution is the oracle. Rule: **a parameter that selects a directory for a deserializing loader is one file-write away from RCE — traversal turns the app's own trusted loader into an arbitrary-directory unpickler, so pair every such traversal probe with the target's file-write surfaces.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW. `2032778|report_weakness_id|trained_at|from_pretrained|joblib|AutoTokenizer|model.?load` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the generic insecure-deserialization entry (Bugbountyskills ~8354-8374 — the attacker SUPPLIES the serialized bytes through a param/cookie/blob; here only the LOCATION is client-shaped and the loader is a trusted code path); §17.219 (pickle.loads fed by a forged client cookie — direct client-supplied blob); §17.501 (LFR -> source audit -> `unserialize` + gadget -> XXE -> unpickle — a read-then-gadget chain); the upload->LFI-include chains (ledger 81/928 — webshell include, no deserializer involved). NEW axes: (a) the DIRECTORY-selection parameter as the injection point of a deserializing loader; (b) the traversal + file-write composition reaching RCE with NO code-execution sink in the app; (c) the honest conditional terminal (file-drop leg required). NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.718 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2023-06-20, disclosed 2023-07-05 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~3.4 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned; the method class is version-independent (any loader fed a client-shaped directory).
HONESTY: not reproduced; every quote above appears in the fetched report body (live `.json`, VI 2,395 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1085042  2026-11-13 (submitted 2021-01-23, disclosed 2022-07-11; Shopify — shopify.plus; reporter `ramsexy`; weakness "Improper Access Control - Generic"; "[h1-2102] Improper Access Control at https://shopify.plus/[id]/users/api in operation UpdateOrganizationUserTfaEnforcement"; program severity Medium 5.4 after triage downgrades critical 9.6 -> high 8.1 -> medium 5.4)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the report page — FIRST route worked (Stripe-style summary + timeline); the researcher body additionally read IN FULL from the live `.json` (VI 1,597 B).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Shopify Plus tenant this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the fetched report body)
CLASS: BAC/IDOR — a GraphQL mutation keyed by a cross-organisation global object id performs NO ownership check, and the mutation REPORTS an error while its NOTIFICATION side effect still fires and mails the victim's record to the caller (CWE-284/639)
CORE METHOD:
  1. ENTRY POINT (verbatim request): `POST /34808573/users/api` · `Host: shopify.plus` (the numeric path segment is the caller's own org id), reached from `Administration` -> `Users` -> a user page -> `Security` -> edit the 2FA setting; body `{"operationName":"UpdateOrganizationUserTfaEnforcement","variables":{"id":"Z2lkOi8vb3JnYW5pemF0aW9uL09yZ2FuaXphdGlvblVzZXIvMzQwNTc5Mzg=","enforced":false},"query":"mutation UpdateOrganizationUserTfaEnforcement($id: OrganizationUserID!, $enforced: Boolean!) { updateOrganizationUserTfaEnforcement(id: $id, enforced: $enforced) { organizationUser { id tfaEnforced __typename } userErrors { field message __typename } operationStatus message __typename } }"}`.
  2. THE ID: `Z2lkOi8vb3JnYW5pemF0aW9uL09yZ2FuaXphdGlvblVzZXIvMzQwNTc5Mzg=` is base64 of `gid://organization/OrganizationUser/34057938` — a relay/global id whose TYPE PREFIX names the tenant namespace; step 5, verbatim: "In Burp Repeater, edit the `id` with `Z2lkOi8vb3JnYW5pemF0aW9uL09yZ2FuaXphdGlvblVzZXIvMzQwNzE2MzI=`".
  3. THE ASYMMETRY (verbatim): "While the response shows an error message, an email is sent to the user with the 2FA status, first name, last name, email address, and shop id from the victim." Step 6, verbatim: "You will receive an email containing Anatoly information". The authorization fails IN THE RESPONSE while the notification pipeline still merges the VICTIM's record into an email addressed to the CALLER.
  4. THE IMPACT (verbatim): "A Shopify Plus user can retrieve information (2FA status, first name, last name, email address, shop ip) from a user in another organisation."
DETECTION SIGNAL: any mutation/update action whose response carries `userErrors`/an error while a side channel (email to the caller, notification feed, webhook, audit log) still fires; the notification body merging the TARGET's record (security/2FA status, name, email, tenant id) while being delivered to the actor; GraphQL global ids with type-prefixed namespaces (`gid://organization/OrganizationUser/<n>`) used as cross-tenant selectors.
CHAIN: any Shopify Plus user (member of their own org only) -> `UpdateOrganizationUserTfaEnforcement` with a cross-ORGANISATION `OrganizationUser` global id (primitive: the id is the only selector, session != ownership) -> the mutation errors (`userErrors`) but the email notification still sends (escalation: the failure response hides a committed side effect) -> the caller's inbox receives the victim's 2FA status, first/last name, email and shop id (impact: cross-organisation security-posture + PII disclosure — which org users lack 2FA, feeding targeted ATO; program rated Medium 5.4, recorded as the program states it).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every mutation that reports `userErrors`/an exception: (1) send it with a FOREIGN global id (decode the base64 relay id first — `gid://<Type>/<n>` — re-encode for the victim) and treat the error response as UNINFORMATIVE; (2) check EVERY out-of-band channel — YOUR (the caller's) email inbox, notification feeds, webhooks, audit/activity logs — for the side effect that committed anyway; (3) diff what the notification CONTAINS: a message addressed to the caller but merging the target's record IS the leak; (4) repeat across id namespaces (the type prefix maps the object family). Rule: **an error response does not mean the side effect did not run — a failed mutation may still fire its notification pipeline, and the notification can merge the victim's record into the caller's inbox; always diff out-of-band channels against the response's verdict.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over two recorded families — NO §17 slot. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED: ledger 3660-3670 (#2207248, Shopify admin GraphQL `node(id:)` global-ID IDOR — "the id is the only selector (session != ownership)"; its transferable test already covers replaying with a second account's global id and fetching the generated artifact URL) and ledger 3851-3864 (#152067 — "Treat the error text and the resulting SESSION STATE as two independent signals; the error message is a lie"). NEW atoms kept: (a) the NOTIFICATION/EMAIL side channel as the committed side effect of a FAILED mutation — addressed to the CALLER but carrying the VICTIM's record (a leak shape neither neighbour records; both stop at session state / generated artifact); (b) the security-POSTURE datum (2FA enforcement status of a cross-org user) as the high-value field; (c) the `gid://organization/OrganizationUser/<n>` type-prefixed global id as the cross-tenant selector namespace.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2021-01-23, disclosed 2022-07-11 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~4.3 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced; every quote above appears in the fetched report body (live `web_extract` first route + live `.json` VI 1,597 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1296366  2026-11-13 (submitted 2021-08-09, disclosed 2022-02-14; U.S. Dept Of Defense VDP; reporter `dreyand_`; "EC2 subdomain takeover at http://████████/"; weakness "Privilege Escalation"; severity Critical (9 ~ 10) — DoD triage moved it low -> critical, agent-l8 verbatim: "Sorry this got dropped- still an issue, severity is adjusted"; `visibility: full`, VI 966 B read IN FULL)
ROUTE: live `web_extract` on the report page — FIRST route worked (researcher body + timeline + disclosure thread); the live H1 `.json` via `curl` (HTTP 200) confirmed the VI (966 B) and the dates; both routes read. The claim MECHANICS the report does not spell out are grounded in three secondary sources opened this pass (HackTricks Cloud, can-i-take-over-xyz README + issue #137 comments via the GitHub API, blogs.jsmon.sh 2026-04-08) — each named where used.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no AWS allocation was attempted this pass; every report quote below is verbatim from the fetched body; per the secondary sources the modern EIP loop is probabilistic, throttled and cost-bearing)
CLASS: privilege escalation / subdomain takeover via a DANGLING DNS **A record** pointing at a decommissioned EC2 public IP — the record outlives the instance and the raw IP is reclaimable from the provider's regional pool, so a first-party hostname serves attacker content (misconfiguration with impact; the program's own class is "Privilege Escalation", rated Critical 9-10)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE DEFECT (verbatim): "There is a dangling DNS A record that points to an EC2 instance that no longer exists" — the record survives the resource; the owner-side remediation, verbatim: "Remove the A record pointing to the current ec2 instance."
  2. THE CLAIM (report, verbatim): "I was able to claim the EC2 instance and host content on http://████████/." — the report does NOT spell out the claim steps; the carrier's documented claim path (HackTricks Cloud "AWS - Subdomain Takeover", opened this pass): "allocate and release Elastic IPs in the same region in a loop until AWS hands you the target address; then stand up a listener on it. IP pools are regional and finite, so a determined attacker can re-acquire a specific IP" (blog-grade automation, jsmon.sh: `boto3` `allocate_address(Domain=\"vpc\")` in a loop + `release_address` on miss, region inferred from AWS `ip-ranges.json` + passive DNS).
  3. THE PROOF (report, verbatim): "Visit http://█████████/██████████.html and view the PoC" — a canary at a hidden path of the reclaimed host (the family's discreet-proof shape; the can-i-take-over-xyz README: "Do not serve content on the index page").
  4. THE IMPACT (report, verbatim): "Hosting content on http://█████/ and potentionally fully bypassing web protections like CORS (in cases of `████████`) or redirecting users to malicious pages" — attacker bytes on the victim's origin inherit the name's trust (the family terminal, HackTricks framing: "phishing on a trusted domain, cookie/session theft, OAuth/redirect abuse, CORS bypass" — trust follows the NAME, not the owner).
DETECTION SIGNAL: an in-scope hostname whose DNS is a bare **A record** (or an `ec2-*.compute.amazonaws.com` CNAME) inside a cloud provider's public IP ranges where NO application answers (connection refused / provider default page / stale content) — DNS alive + service dead; crucially the family's provider-fingerprint sweep does NOT fire here (there is no "unknown domain"/`NoSuchBucket` page — the resource is an unowned IP, not an unclaimed service NAME). Secondary tell: CT/passive-DNS history tying the host to the brand while the runtime service is gone.
CHAIN: dangling A record survives EC2 instance termination (primitive: DNS outlives the resource) -> the released public/Elastic IP returns to the provider's regional pool and the attacker obtains that exact address (primitive: reclaimable raw IP — the report's "claim the EC2 instance") -> serve attacker content at the victim's first-party hostname (escalation: attacker-controlled bytes on the victim's origin) -> "fully bypassing web protections like CORS ... or redirecting users to malicious pages" (impact, the report's own words — origin-trust inheritance; DoD rated Critical 9-10).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every in-scope hostname resolving to a bare A record in a cloud IP range: (1) request it and note that NO provider fingerprint exists — the family's unclaimed-service strings never fire; (2) confirm staleness (CT/passive-DNS history vs the current site; the owner-side tell is literally "Remove the A record pointing to the current ec2 instance"); (3) attempt the claim in the record's region (infer region from AWS `ip-ranges.json` + passive DNS): loop `allocate_address`/`release_address` (or launch/terminate instances) until the target IP is issued to you, and serve a canary at a RANDOM hidden path, never the index; (4) grade feasibility BEFORE burning budget — "AWS does not guarantee FIFO reallocation. This can take minutes or hours. AWS throttles aggressive allocation loops, and running this at scale risks account review" (jsmon.sh 2026-04-08), and field reports in can-i-take-over-xyz #137 found the loop saturating: "You will only get a set of 4 to 5 IPs when you associate and release the IPs. Before you could iterate until receiving the wanted IP" (`pdelteil`) and "eventually it started giving me the same IPs again without giving me any that were available for possible takeover" (`AnotherWayIn`); (5) if the claim fails, the DANGLING RECORD itself may still be accepted — "some bug bounty programs may accept dangling DNS record reports without requiring proof of compromise" (can-i-take-over-xyz README). Rule: **a bare A record into a cloud IP range is a takeover candidate the CNAME-fingerprint family sweep misses — the claim is IP reallocation in the record's region (probabilistic, throttled, cost-bearing), and the terminal is origin-trust inheritance on the victim's hostname.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over the recorded dangling-DNS takeover family — NO §17 slot. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED: ledger 4849-4862 (Snapchat `fastly.sc-cdn.net` — dangling CNAME to a dead third-party SERVICE; claim = register the SERVICE; detection = provider unclaimed-service fingerprint); ledger 9427-9440 (Basecamp — dangling NS DELEGATION, claim = register the cloud DNS ZONE; the family's carrier-delta precedent, appended the same way); ledger 9221-9235 (#2523654 -> §17.310, GitLabPages pre-verification window); Bugbountyskills 15741 ("DNS Zone Takeover" checklist line). None records the A-RECORD/EC2-IP carrier. NEW atoms kept: (a) the bare-A-record/cloud-IP-range detection shape (DNS alive + service dead + NO provider fingerprint — the exact gap in the family's fingerprint sweep); (b) IP REALLOCATION as the claim path (no hostname registration, no zone registration at all) with its feasibility matrix (probabilistic, non-FIFO, throttled — grounded in the named secondary sources); (c) the report's impact framing kept verbatim — origin-trust inheritance (CORS bypass / malicious-page redirection) as the terminal.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2021-08-09, disclosed 2022-02-14 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The MECHANICS leg carries fresh grounding (jsmon.sh, 2026-04-08): that source also records the CloudFront sibling claim as DEAD since 2022 (ACM-certificate requirement makes the claim circular — `CNAMEAlreadyExists`/`InvalidViewerCertificate`) — do not attempt the CloudFront variant.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no AWS account touched; per the sources above the modern EIP loop is probabilistic and cost-bearing — jsmon.sh's own verdict: "impractical as a first-pass technique"). Every quote attributed to the report appears in the fetched body (live `web_extract` first route + live `.json` VI 966 B). The claim MECHANICS are explicitly NOT in the report body; they are cited to sources opened this pass: HackTricks Cloud "AWS - Subdomain Takeover" (cloud.hacktricks.wiki), EdOverflow/can-i-take-over-xyz issue #137 comments (GitHub API) + its README, and blogs.jsmon.sh "Hunting Dangling DNS in AWS: Updated Techniques That Actually Work in 2026" (2026-04-08).


SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3871816  2026-11-13 (disclosed 2026-11-05; WordPress — WordCamp.org; "Unauthenticated private CSV export of Meetup organizer PII via broken authz check in WordCamp Reports"; VI 5,720 B)
ROUTE: live H1 `.json` via `curl` -> HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI read IN FULL — THE ROUTE THAT WORKED.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no WordCamp lab this pass; quotes verbatim from the fetched report body)
CLASS: BAC / authorization bypass — an INVERTED BOOLEAN OPERATOR in a compound authz guard: `if ( ! wp_verify_nonce(...) && current_user_can(...) ) return;` fires for exactly the ONE caller combination that is least dangerous (privileged user with a bad nonce) and admits everyone else, including anonymous (CWE-863)
CORE METHOD (verbatim):
  1. THE BROKEN GATE, verbatim: `if ( ! wp_verify_nonce( $nonce, 'run-report' ) && current_user_can( CAPABILITY ) ) { return; }` in `Meetup_Details::export_to_file()` (wordcamp-reports plugin, hooked to `admin_init`; intended capability `view_wordcamp_reports`).
  2. THE CORRECT SIBLING, verbatim (class-meetup-sponsors.php): `if ( ! wp_verify_nonce( $nonce, 'run-report' ) || ! current_user_can( CAPABILITY ) ) { return; }`.
  3. THE TRUTH TABLE (report's): anon + invalid nonce -> EXPORT PROCEEDS (vulnerable); privileged + invalid nonce -> blocked; anon + valid nonce -> proceeds; privileged + valid -> proceeds (intended). "Only privileged users with a bad nonce are blocked."
  4.
