SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3679660  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3679660.json` — HTTP 200, `vulnerability_information` 7,163 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Shopify (shopify-scripts/mruby-engine), "mruby-engine: UAF in MRubyEngine#initialize enables local RCE", reporter `0xd0ff9`, created 2026-04-17, disclosed 2026-04-24, weakness Memory Corruption - Generic, 30 votes; H1 severity field `none` (report's own severity section: "Full chain ... RCE").
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no engine was built and no request was sent to any target)
CLASS: sandbox escape -> local RCE — double invocation of a C-extension initializer frees the engine and unmaps its memory pool BEFORE validating arguments, a raise-between-free-and-reinit path leaves the Ruby object's `DATA_PTR` dangling (UAF), the freed VA is reclaimed deterministically with `mmap(MAP_FIXED)`, and the forged engine's `mrb_state->allocf` is pointed at `libc.system` (CWE-416 -> CWE-822 -> arbitrary code execution)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE FREE-THEN-RAISE (report verbatim): `ext_mruby_engine_initialize` begins `ext_mruby_engine_free(DATA_PTR(rself));   // frees engine + munmap mspace`, then validates: `long capacity = NUM2LONG(rcapacity); if (capacity <= 0) { rb_raise(rb_eArgError, "memory quota cannot be negative"); }  // raise AFTER free`. Two defects (report): "`initialize` is re-callable via `obj.send(:initialize, ...)` — bypasses Ruby's private-visibility convention" and "`DATA_PTR(rself)` never zeroed after free ... Any `rb_raise` between free and successful re-init leaves `self` holding dangling pointer"; pre-success raise paths: negative capacity, negative instruction quota, negative time quota, `me_memory_pool_new` failure, `me_mruby_engine_new` failure.
  2. THE VA RECLAIM (report): the freed mspace is `MAP_PRIVATE|MAP_ANONYMOUS`, so "Kernel reuses freed VA via `mmap(MAP_FIXED)`" — the attacker mmaps the exact freed range and writes the bytes of a forged `me_mruby_engine` (`+0 struct mrb_state *state`, `+8 struct me_memory_pool *allocator`) and `mrb_state` (`+0 jmp` reused as the cmd string, `+16 mrb_allocf allocf`).
  3. THE HIJACK (report): "points `mrb_state->allocf` at `libc.system`, arranges bytes of `mrb_state` to also spell a shell command. Next engine method calls `mrb_malloc` → indirect call through `allocf` → `system("id>/tmp/pwned")`" — `(mrb->allocf)(mrb, NULL, sz, mrb->allocf_ud)` fires with `mrb` = the attacker buffer whose FIRST bytes are the command.
  4. THE REPRO (report): `poc_rce_final.rb` — `dead = MRubyEngine.new(CAP, 1_000_000, 60)`, `dead.send(:initialize, -1, 1, 1)` (rescue the raise), find the 8 MiB hole in `/proc/self/maps`, `mmap(hole_start, CAP, PROT_RW, MAP_FIXED_ANON, -1, 0)` + write the forged layout at the observed `DATA_PTR` offset (`0x3e0`), then `dead.sandbox_eval("rce.rb", "noop")` triggers. Proof (report): "`/tmp/pwned` written with `uid=0(root) gid=0(root) groups=0(root)` inside test container"; reliability "VA reclaim: 60/60". Fix (report): "zero `DATA_PTR` before any raise, and do validation before free", plus raise on a second `initialize` call.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a native-extension sandbox engine whose initializer frees the existing native state before validating arguments and re-assigns the wrapper's data pointer only on the success path; any exception between free and re-init; a native backing store obtained via `mmap` (VA reuse makes reclaim deterministic); host-side code that can reach `send(:initialize, ...)` on an existing engine. Tell: the first instance-method call after the failed re-init crashes or ASan-fires heap-use-after-free on the engine object.
CHAIN: a host-Ruby caller who can invoke `MRubyEngine#send(:initialize, ...)` on a live engine (precondition per report: "Host integration code that reuses or exposes engine objects, or any `eval`/deserialization of untrusted host-side Ruby" — the report states it is "Not reachable from guest mruby") -> `send(:initialize, -1, 1, 1)` raises after free (primitive: UAF on `DATA_PTR`, the object stays live) -> `mmap(MAP_FIXED)` reclaims the freed VA with attacker bytes (escalation: full engine + `mrb_state` forgery, 60/60) -> `allocf` -> `system` (terminal: arbitrary OS command execution as the host process — root in the report's test container; per doctrine §8: precondition = host-side re-init reachability, gain = OS command execution).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any native-extension sandbox engine (mruby-engine, Lua bridges, JS/WASM runtimes with native state): (1) call the initializer twice via reflection/`send` with INVALID args so it raises between free and re-init, then call any instance method — a crash/UAF error = the dangling-pointer defect; (2) source-audit the initializer: free-before-validate + data pointer assigned only on the success path + never zeroed = the bug shape; (3) where the native store is `mmap`'d, reclaim the freed VA with `mmap(MAP_FIXED)` and forge the struct to turn the UAF into the RCE claim (lab only). Rule: **an initializer that frees before it validates and raises after the free leaves a live object pointing at freed native state — double-init is the trigger and MAP_FIXED reclaim turns the UAF into code execution.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3679660`, `0xd0ff9`, `MRubyEngine`, `ext_mruby_engine`, `poc_rce_final`, `allocf`, `mmap(MAP_FIXED`, `send(:initialize` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the recorded mruby-engine entries (#181910/#185051 runtime constant-lookup type confusion -> attacker IP control; §17.49 builtin-exception overwrite crash; §17.55 core-method deletion crash; #184661/#184857 `initialize_copy` on never-initialized objects -> NULL-`DATA_PTR` struct write crash; §17.107 C-stack recursion crash) — every one of those is a GUEST-SCRIPT-reachable primitive in the hosted sandbox; this entry is a HOST-API free-before-validate UAF (explicitly not guest-reachable) with a full VA-reclaim -> function-pointer-hijack RCE chain — a defect class none of the recorded entries covers. NEW: double-init free-before-validate UAF + `mmap(MAP_FIXED)` VA reclaim + `allocf`->`system`. New general method -> §17.748.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-04-17, disclosed 2026-04-24 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; no binary fetched, no request sent to any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (7,163 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3856148  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3856148.json` — HTTP 200, `vulnerability_information` 15,985 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: MariaDB, "Low-privilege RCE in MariaDB: SYS_REFCURSOR cursor-array use-after-free chained with an ST_Area heap over-read", reporter `rdjgr`, created 2026-07-10, disclosed 2026-10-07, weakness Use After Free, severity Critical; report's own chain CVSS 3.1 `AV:N/AC:H/PR:L/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:H/A:H` = 8.5.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no MariaDB instance stood up, no SQL sent to any target)
CLASS: RCE escalation of a LOW-PRIVILEGE SQL session — two chained memory-safety bugs: (1) a reallocating `Dynamic_array` moves the cursor-array buffer while an open `SYS_REFCURSOR` keeps a raw `select_result *` into the old buffer -> use-after-free with virtual-dispatch through a freed vtable on `FETCH`; (2) `Gis_multi_polygon::area` (`ST_Area`) advances its read cursor by `WKB_HEADER_SIZE` with no bounds check and folds out-of-bounds heap bytes into the returned `DOUBLE` -> an in-process memory-disclosure oracle that defeats ASLR/PIE (CWE-416 + CWE-125 chained)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE UAF (report verbatim): "A reallocating `Dynamic_array` moves its backing buffer while an open cursor keeps a raw pointer into the old buffer; a later `FETCH` dereferences it and reaches a virtual call, giving control of the instruction pointer." Trigger (report): `sp_cursor_array` stores elements INLINE (`prealloc=0`, growth 0,16,32,48); "The 33rd `OPEN` grows capacity from 32 to 48 and frees the 32-slot buffer. Cursors opened while it was live (e.g. `c16`) still point into it, so `FETCH c16` reads freed memory" — one anonymous block, no stored routine: `BEGIN NOT ATOMIC DECLARE c0..c32 SYS_REFCURSOR; ... OPEN c0 FOR SELECT 1; ... OPEN c32 FOR SELECT 1; FETCH c16 INTO a; END` (ASan: heap-use-after-free at `Materialized_cursor::fetch` `sql/sql_cursor.cc:294`, freed by `my_realloc` in `sp_cursor_array::append`).
  2. THE ORACLE (report verbatim): "`Gis_multi_polygon::area()` loops over its declared sub-polygon count, advancing the read cursor by `WKB_HEADER_SIZE` each iteration ... with no bounds check before the advance. If the declared count exceeds the polygons actually present, the cursor moves past `m_data_end`, and the length passed to the child, `(uint32)(m_data_end - data)`, is a negative value truncated to a ~4 GiB `uint32`, defeating the child's bounds checks." Trigger: `SELECT ST_Area(0x...0106000000020000000103...0000)` — "a MULTIPOLYGON declaring 2 polygons but supplying 1"; on a release build "the out-of-bounds bytes are returned in the `DOUBLE` result (leak)". The sibling `geometry_n()` HAS the missing guard — the differential identifies the defect.
  3. THE CHAIN (report): "Bug 2 leaks the heap and code base to defeat ASLR/PIE, and Bug 1 uses that to write a privilege flag and run code. Fixing either bug breaks the chain." Step 1: "`ST_Area` folds out-of-bounds heap qwords into its `DOUBLE` result, recovered exactly via a controlled multiplier" -> vtable leak -> heap arena base + `mariadbd` PIE base. Step 2: the 33 OPENs free the 32-slot buffer; `SET @s1 = 0x<3584 bytes>` reclaims it with a crafted layout. Step 3: `FETCH c16`'s virtual dispatch runs a write-what-where gadget (`mov 0x8(%rdi),%rdx ; mov %rax,(%rdx) ; ret`) writing into the session's `Security_context::master_access` — sets an ACL including `FILE`. Step 4: UDF via `LOAD_FILE`+`INTO DUMPFILE` or a call-oriented chain to `execlp("/bin/sh","sh","-c","id>/tmp/maria_proof")`.
  4. THE PROOF (report): end-to-end on `mariadb:13.0.1-rc` Docker as the built-in unprivileged app account ("per-database `SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,CREATE,DROP,CREATE TEMPORARY TABLES,EXECUTE`, with no FILE, SUPER, admin, or CREATE ROUTINE") — "`id` output written to disk by the `mariadbd` process user"; full weaponized `docker-demo/exploit.py`.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a SQL-reachable engine surface where (a) a by-value/growing array holds objects whose addresses are aliased by longer-lived handles (cursors, statements, result objects) — signal: `realloc`-backed growth while open handles point into the buffer; (b) a binary-parsed structure (WKB geometry, BLOB formats) whose declared count drives a cursor advance without a bounds check and whose computed lengths come from unchecked subtraction. Cheap probes: open more cursors than the growth increment (33rd `OPEN`) then `FETCH` an older one; `SELECT ST_Area(<MULTIPOLYGON with n_polygons> actual polys>)` and diff the `DOUBLE` against a known-good value.
CHAIN: a low-privilege authenticated DB user with only ordinary per-schema DML/DDL (precondition per report: "The account needs only per-database SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE,CREATE,DROP,CREATE TEMPORARY TABLES,EXECUTE") -> `ST_Area` OOB read folded into the `DOUBLE` result (primitive: in-band memory-disclosure oracle, the only low-priv channel that returns a raw pointer to the client) -> leak heap + PIE (escalation: ASLR/PIE/Full-RELRO defeated) -> cursor-array UAF reclaimed with `SET @s = 0x...` (primitive: attacker bytes under a live cursor's dangling `result`) -> `FETCH` virtual dispatch -> write-what-where flips `master_access` to add `FILE` -> UDF/`execlp` shell as the `mariadbd` user (terminal: arbitrary OS commands as the DB process — "reading/modifying all data across all databases ... pivoting to other systems"; report rate: roughly 1/2 reliability).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any DB engine a SQLi/low-priv session can reach: (1) run the cursor-growth probe — declare/open N+1 cursors where N exceeds the engine's array growth increment, then `FETCH` a cursor opened before the last growth (ASan build or a crash = UAF); (2) run the parser probe — feed binary-parsed values (geospatial WKB, BLOBs) whose DECLARED element count exceeds the supplied payload and diff numeric results against a legit value (a shifted `DOUBLE`/int = OOB-read oracle); (3) chain them: use the oracle's leaked pointers to aim the UAF reclaim (`SET @s = 0x<hex layout>` / heap grooming) — an in-band leak plus a reclaimable UAF is the complete RCE ladder. Rule: **an OOB read that folds bytes into a numeric result is a pointer oracle, and a realloc that moves an array under live handles is an IP-control primitive — low-priv SQL to RCE is those two bugs chained, each useless alone.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3856148`, `rdjgr`, `SYS_REFCURSOR`, `sp_cursor_array`, `Gis_multi_polygon`, `ST_Area`, `Dynamic_array`, `master_access` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the recorded MariaDB entry (batch 212, #3897914 — FILE+RELOAD privilege -> crafted `.frm` metadata file -> out-of-range `fieldnr` index onto `comment_pos` bytes -> fake `Field*` -> virtual call) — same terminal class (SQL-reachable memory corruption -> code exec in `mariadbd`) but a different mechanism (metadata-file parsing vs in-session UAF+oracle), a different privilege precondition (FILE+RELOAD vs plain DML/DDL — THIS is the escalation delta: no FILE needed) and different atoms (the in-band `DOUBLE`-folded pointer oracle is entirely new); the client-side `unpack_fields` OOB-read entry (malicious SERVER -> client crash) is the wrong direction of trust. NEW: low-priv two-bug chain (OOB-read-into-scalar oracle + realloc UAF reclaim) -> RCE. New general method -> §17.749.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-07-10, disclosed 2026-10-07 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; no database stood up, no SQL sent to any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (15,985 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3687543  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3687543.json` — HTTP 200, `vulnerability_information` 12,083 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Monero, "`relay_tx` wallet-rpc skips `--restricted-rpc` guard and lets any caller corrupt wallet state via attacker-controlled `pending_tx`", reporter `benisprlh`, created 2026-04-21, disclosed 2026-08-05, weakness Improper Access Control - Generic, H1 severity LOW (program's own rating recorded honestly), 37 votes. Target: `monero-project/monero` `src/wallet/wallet_rpc_server.cpp`, `master @ 230de3794` (2026-04-20).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no monerod build stood up, no request sent to any target)
CLASS: authorization bypass (restricted-mode) + unsafe acceptance of a caller-supplied SERIALIZED "prepared operation" — `on_relay_tx` deserializes a hex blob into `wallet2::pending_tx` and hands it to `commit_tx`, which mutates wallet state from the blob's SELF-DECLARED indices/keys with only a bounds check and no provenance/ownership check that the transaction was built by this wallet (CWE-863 + CWE-502-shape without gadget execution)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE MISSING GUARD (report verbatim): commit `604bb05d8` (PR #10378, "fix: return early in sensitive methods for --restricted-rpc", merged 2026-03-23) added `if (m_restricted) { er.code = WALLET_RPC_ERROR_CODE_DENIED; ... }` to `transfer`, `sweep_all`, `transfer_split`, `sign_transfer`, `submit_transfer`, `sweep_dust` — "Every other transfer-class method ... gained one ... `on_relay_tx` was not touched. So a caller holding only restricted credentials can invoke `relay_tx` freely."
  2. THE FORGED BLOB (report verbatim): `on_relay_tx` does `binary_archive<false> ar{...}; ::serialization::serialize(ar, ptx)` on `req.hex` then `m_wallet->commit_tx(ptx)` — "nothing in the handler checks that the `pending_tx` was ever produced by this wallet. `commit_tx` trusts every field verbatim: `selected_transfers` (which outputs to mark spent), `tx_key` (written into `m_tx_keys[txid]`), `dests` / `change_dts` / `construction_data` (written into `m_unconfirmed_txs[txid]`), and the per-transfer multisig nonce (`m_multisig_k`)". The report quotes `commit_tx`: `for (size_t idx: ptx.selected_transfers) THROW_WALLET_EXCEPTION_IF(idx >= m_transfers.size(), ...);  // bounds only, no key-image match` — "There is no check that `m_transfers[idx].m_key_image` appears anywhere in `ptx.tx.vin`."
  3. THE EXPLOIT (report PoC): attacker wallet runs `transfer` with `do_not_relay:true, get_tx_key:true, get_tx_metadata:true` (a real signed tx spending the ATTACKER's own outputs, unbroadcast) and submits the returned `tx_metadata` hex to the RESTRICTED victim wallet: `relay_tx {"hex":"<TX_META>"}` -> `{"result":{"tx_hash":"..."}}` ("succeeds despite restricted mode" while `transfer` returns `-7 Command unavailable in restricted mode.`). "The attacker ships their own valid transaction (spending their own outputs) but tells commit_tx that the victim's outputs were selected."
  4. THE IMPACT (report verbatim): `commit_tx` runs `set_spent()` on the blob's indices — "AFTER: 200 outputs — 2 SPENT (frozen), 198 unspent" ("permanently marked spent in the wallet cache ... On a hot wallet processing payments, this freezes funds and causes sends to fail"); `m_tx_keys[txid] = ptx.tx_key` — "Any call to `get_tx_key` or `get_tx_proof` for that txid returns attacker-controlled key material ... enough to forge payment confirmation in any system that trusts wallet-rpc proofs"; multisig: `commit_tx` wipes `m_multisig_k` for the selected indices — "Those per-transfer signing nonces cannot be regenerated ... the cosigners would need to re-export and re-import keys."
DETECTION SIGNAL: an API that accepts a serialized INTERNAL operation structure (transaction metadata, job envelope, batch blob, draft object) from the client and commits it through the same path as self-produced operations, with only a bounds check on the blob's referenced-resource indices and no key-image/ownership/provenance cross-check; second-order tell (the report's own): a security guard added to a handler family in ONE commit with one sibling left unguarded — diff the patch's touched handlers against the full method list. Differential: `transfer` returns the restricted denial while `relay_tx` returns success.
CHAIN: a process holding restricted (`--restricted-rpc`) credentials — the documented safe mode "for giving a monitoring agent, payment integration, or CI pipeline read-only-ish access" (precondition: the mode was the boundary) -> `relay_tx` lacks the `m_restricted` guard (primitive: per-handler deny-list hole — the §17.448 family, one more instance) -> submit a valid attacker-funded `pending_tx` blob with `selected_transfers` = the VICTIM's output indices (primitive: the deserialized structure is trusted without ownership/provenance check — the NEW atom) -> `commit_tx` marks victim outputs spent + plants the attacker's `tx_key` + wipes `m_multisig_k` (terminal: cross-wallet state corruption = frozen funds on a hot wallet, forgeable payment proofs against anything trusting `get_tx_proof`, permanent multisig signing-key-nonce destruction; program rated Low — recorded as rated).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any API with a "prepared/partial operation" input later committed: (1) produce the structure on a DIFFERENT account/instance and submit it to the target's commit/relay/execute endpoint; if fields referencing local resources (self-declared indices/ids) are honored without cross-checking against the signed transaction's actual inputs (key-image/ownership match), the state-corruption primitive is live; (2) on services with a restricted/read-only mode, run §17.448's full per-method differential — one unguarded sibling in a guarded family is the entry; (3) prove impact by diffing the state APIs (`get_tx_key`, `incoming_transfers`) before/after the commit. Rule: **a deserialized prepared operation is client-asserted intent — the commit path must re-derive what it touches from the signed transaction itself, never from the blob's self-declared indices.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3687543`, `benisprlh`, `relay_tx`, `pending_tx`, `m_multisig_k`, `do_not_relay`, `commit_tx` (as this technique) = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.448 (#3620006, same reporter family — wallet RPC restricted mode enforced PER HANDLER = deny-list with holes, 18 unguarded handlers; here the guard gap is only the ENTRY, and the NEW atom is the ownership-free deserialization -> cross-wallet state corruption + payment-proof forgery); §17.629 (#3601469 — the mode not propagated to a second transport); the #3699522 entry (check_reserve_proof duplicate-count inflation — a verifier-side counting bug in a different method; its `selected_transfers` mention is a PoC variable, no deserialization, no ownership issue). New general method -> §17.750.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3619287  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3619287.json` — HTTP 200, `vulnerability_information` 2,447 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: DuckDuckGo, "RCE + Supply Chain Attack via pull_request_target in content-scope-scripts/semver-label.yml — Affects All DuckDuckGo Browsers", reporter `6r1ff1n`, created 2026-03-21, disclosed 2026-06-11, severity Critical.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; per doctrine §2(d) no fork PR was opened against any repository and nothing was sent to any target)
CLASS: RCE on a GitHub Actions runner + secret exfiltration + supply-chain release poisoning — `pull_request_target` runs in the BASE repository's context (secrets in scope) while the workflow checks out and EXECUTES the untrusted fork-PR head (`npm install` + `npm run build`), and the same job's `GITHUB_TOKEN` (`pull-requests: write`) drives the repository's own label-triggered release pipeline (CWE-862 + CWE-94 in CI)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE TRIGGER (report verbatim): `duckduckgo/content-scope-scripts` `.github/workflows/semver-label.yml` "uses `pull_request_target` trigger without any access controls, checks out and executes untrusted fork PR code via `npm install` + `npm run build`, and subsequently runs a trusted script with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment" — "zero access controls — any GitHub user can trigger this by opening a fork PR."
  2. THE EXFIL (report): "Any GitHub user can execute arbitrary code on the Actions runner by opening a fork PR. ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is directly exfiltrable, enabling API abuse at DuckDuckGo's expense." Related leg: "DAX_PAT exposure via related `pull_request_target` workflows"; the report enumerates "10 workflows using `pull_request_target` across the DuckDuckGo org".
  3. THE ESCALATION (report): "GITHUB_TOKEN with pull-requests:write can add semver labels to any PR, triggering the automated release pipeline on merge. The attacker controls when releases happen." (`semver-label.yml` -> `semver-release.yml` -> `build.yml`.)
  4. THE TERMINAL (report): "content-scope-scripts is 'shared JavaScript used within ALL DuckDuckGo browsers.' A poisoned release propagates to Android, iOS, macOS, Windows browsers, Chrome extension, Firefox extension, and the autofill component — affecting tens of millions of users" and "build.yml creates releases with no code signing or integrity verification. Downstream consumers pull from the releases branch with no signature validation."
DETECTION SIGNAL: a workflow with `on: pull_request_target` (or `issue_comment`/`workflow_run` with an untrusted-ref checkout) that checks out `github.event.pull_request.head` / `head.sha` and then RUNS anything from the workspace while secrets/job permissions are in scope; a later trusted step holding a secret after untrusted code ran; `permissions:` carrying write scopes on that job; a label/merge-triggered release workflow the job's token can feed. Tell: `pull_request_target` + a PR-head checkout in the same job = the trust boundary inverts — the trigger exists precisely to run TRUSTED code on untrusted events.
CHAIN: any GitHub user with no permission on the repo (primitive: fork + PR = the documented trigger) -> the `pull_request_target` job runs with the BASE repo's context and secrets while the checked-out code is the attacker's PR head (primitive: trust-boundary inversion) -> `npm install`/`npm run build` executes attacker code on the runner -> exfiltrate `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `DAX_PAT` / any job secret (escalation: secret theft) and use `GITHUB_TOKEN` `pull-requests: write` to add semver labels to arbitrary PRs (escalation: the repo's own `semver-release.yml` -> `build.yml` publishes a release) -> poisoned `content-scope-scripts` release pulled UNSIGNED by every DuckDuckGo browser/extension (terminal: attacker JS in tens of millions of clients = supply-chain compromise).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every org with GitHub Actions: (1) grep all workflows for `pull_request_target`/`issue_comment`/`workflow_run` and flag any that check out `github.event.pull_request.head*`/untrusted refs and then execute anything from the workspace; (2) passive-only on third-party targets (doctrine §2(d)): read the workflow files and the `permissions:` block — `pull_request_target` + PR-head checkout + any secret/write scope is the finding shape; where authorized (own repo/lab), open a fork PR whose `package.json` `postinstall` beacons out and observe runner execution + env secrets; (3) map the release automation reachable with the job's token (label/merge-triggered workflows) — an unsigned release pipeline is the supply-chain terminal. Rule: **`pull_request_target` exists to run TRUSTED workflow code on untrusted events — the moment it checks out and executes the PR head, every fork PR is RCE with the base repo's secrets and token, and a write-scoped token turns that into release poisoning.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3619287`, `6r1ff1n`, `content-scope-scripts`, `semver-label`, `semver-release`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `DAX_PAT`, `pull-requests: write` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the pwn-request PRIMITIVE is named in recorded material (§17.404's `workflow_run` fork+PR auto-fire on artifact override; §17.436's "`pull_request_target` pwn request" as one entry vector into its cache-poisoning chain; ledger ~16713 "the pwn-request primitive") but NO recorded entry carries the pwn-request RECIPE itself (trigger shape + PR-head checkout + untrusted build execution + secret exfil) nor the write-token -> label -> release-automation -> unsigned poisoned release escalation — those are the NEW atoms. Delta vs §17.436: there default-branch code execution is only a primitive and the terminal is cache poisoning; here the terminals are direct key exfiltration and release-pipeline poisoning driven by the job's own write token. Delta vs §17.404: that fires via `workflow_run` + artifact file-override; here the event is the fork PR itself. New general method -> §17.751.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/850775  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/850775.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 2,937 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Internet Bug Bounty (openssl), "Windows only: arbitrary file read vulnerability in openssl s_server", reporter `jobert`, created 2020-04-15, disclosed 2020-10-10, weakness Path Traversal, program severity Medium. The report body itself carries the full fix diff (openssl commit `0a4d6c67480a4d2fce514e08d3efe571f2ee99c9`, quoted inside the report).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Windows host stood up and no request sent to any target)
CLASS: LFI / path traversal -> internal arbitrary file read (CWE-22) — a homegrown `..` detector whose dot-segment state machine resets only on `/` (never `\`) and has no drive-letter branch, so Windows-native traversal forms pass
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SURFACE (report verbatim): run `openssl s_server -tls1 -WWW -accept 443` (the `-WWW`/`-HTTP` modes serve files from the working directory over the TLS socket); every request path is resolved against disk.
  2. THE CHECK AND ITS FLAW (report verbatim): "The root cause of this vulnerability seems to come from an incomplete check in path parsing logic: https://github.com/openssl/openssl/blob/master/apps/s_server.c#L3225. Ideally, it'd include a check for a backslash, too." The detector walks the request path counting dots to flag a `..` component — but its state machine only resets the dot-counter on `/` (`dot = (e[0] == '/') ? 1 : 0`), so a `\`-separated `..` never registers as traversal, and no branch handles `:`, so drive-letter absolute paths pass as ordinary text.
  3. THE EXPLOIT (report verbatim): run `.\curl.exe -k https://127.0.0.1/..\..\..\..\..\..\..\any-file` then "observe the contents of any-file, which could be located outside of the running directory, to be returned to the user". Vulnerable versions (report): "0.9.6, 0.9.7, 0.9.8, 1.0.0, 1.0.1, 1.0.2, 1.1.0, and 1.1.1"; tested against "OpenSSL 1.1.1c on Windows 10 (64-bit)".
  4. THE FIX CONFIRMS BOTH ATOMS (commit `0a4d6c67` diff, quoted in the report): `case 3: dot = (e[0] == '/' || e[0] == '\\') ? -1 : 0;` and `dot = (e[0] == '/') ? 1 : 0` becomes `dot = (e[0] == '/' || e[0] == '\\') ? 1 : 0;` (backslash now separates components) PLUS a new branch `if (e[0] == ':') { /* Windows drive. We treat this the same way as ".." */ dot = -1; break; }` (drive letter treated AS traversal), and the reject string becomes `'%s' contains '..' or ':'`. Two bypass shapes, one check: `..\`-separated segments and `C:` absolute paths.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a Windows-hosted file-fetching endpoint (download/export/raw/viewer/serving mode) guarded by a self-written traversal check that only understands `/`: `../` blocked/rejected but `..\..\file` or `C:\file` returns content. Tell: the reject message mentions `'..'` yet backslash traversal works. Source-level: a dot-segment state machine with `== '/'` resets and no `|| == '\\'`, and no `:` branch.
CHAIN: any caller who can reach the file-serving handler (primitive: the request path is resolved against disk — in s_server -WWW/-HTTP there is no auth model at all) -> `\`-separated `..` components, or a `C:` absolute path, pass the `..` detector (bypass: separator-asymmetric state reset + missing drive-letter branch) -> "the contents of any-file ... returned to the user" (terminal per report: "An attacker may be able to read arbitrary files from a user's file system" — e.g. `C:\Windows\win.ini`, source trees, config/credential files next to the served directory).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any Windows-target file endpoint with a homegrown traversal filter: (1) baseline `GET /../../../../windows/win.ini` (expect a `contains '..'` reject / 404); (2) swap the separator: `GET /..%5c..%5c..%5cwindows%5cwin.ini` and the raw form `/..\..\..\windows\win.ini`; (3) try the drive-letter absolute forms `GET /C:%5cwindows%5cwin.ini` and `/..%5c..%5cC:%5cwindows%5cwin.ini`; (4) the `[fonts]` header line in the response = the check is separator-asymmetric / colon-blind. Source variant: grep the path parser for `== '/'` state resets without `|| ... == '\\'` and for a missing `:` drive branch. Rule: **a traversal filter and the filesystem must agree on what a separator is — if the checker counts `..` only across `/`, Windows `\` and `C:` walk straight past it.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `850775`, `s_server`, `0a4d6c67`, `dot = -1`, `Windows drive`, `jobert` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: playbook V-12 ("WAF bypass path traversal via Unicode/UTF-8 alternate slash character mappings") lists `..%5c`/`..\\` in its encoding ladder BUT its mechanism and precondition are a WAF-vs-backend layer differential ("A blocking layer in front (WAF/proxy/CDN with `../` rules)") — here there is NO blocking layer: the application's OWN detector (the `www_body` path parser in `s_server.c`) is the thing bypassed, and V-12 carries neither the app-internal detector framing nor the `:` drive-letter atom; #1131465 (Ruby Tempfile, ledger 12748) is WRITE-side and its atom is a broken sanitizer (`String#delete` set-syntax eats the `/` but not the `\`) — here nothing is sanitized, a DETECTOR's state machine is asymmetric; ledger ~3377 (Mozilla VPN hot_reload) is write-side filename traversal; playbook V-04/`C:\Windows\win.ini` hits are canary-file CHOICE for proven reads, and playbook line 7436 ("strip absolute paths, drive letters, and `..` segments") is remediation text, not an attack test. NEW: app-internal dot-segment detector with `/`-only state reset + missing `:` drive branch = two payload forms (`..\..`, `C:`) that read arbitrary files.
FRESHNESS: created 2020-04-15, disclosed 2020-10-10 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~6.1 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The defect shape (separator-asymmetric traversal check) is re-detectable on every Windows-hosted file endpoint.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; nothing sent to any target; no Windows build exercised). Every quote and the fix diff above are verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (2,937 B) — the commit patch is quoted INSIDE the report body (the GitHub commit page itself was not separately fetched this pass).

CORE METHOD:

  4. THE ESCALATION RUNG (NOT demonstrated in the report — the reporter stops at the WSDL; this rung is documented by the report's own reference, the Acunetix class page, fetched this pass, verbatim: "Because of improper configuration an anonymous user has access to the SharePoint Web Services", classification `CVSS:3.0/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:N/A:N`, Severity Medium, CWE-200): the operations listed in the very WSDL the PoC fetches are then callable without authentication — `GetList`/`GetListItems`/`GetAttachment` read list content (the C:H leg) and the write-shaped operations modify it where exposed.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any IIS/SharePoint host answering 200 + `<definitions`/`<wsdl` XML on `/_vti_bin/<service>.asmx?WSDL` to a cookie-less anonymous request (siblings: `?disco`, `/_vti_bin/` directory response). Tell: the returned `<wsdl:operation>` list enumerates the site's lists/permissions — the admin/author API is mapped without auth.
CHAIN: anonymous internet caller (primitive: the `_vti_bin` web services are not auth-gated — demonstrated) -> anonymous `GET /_vti_bin/lists.asmx?WSDL` -> the service operation map + "full structure of the application" (demonstrated in the report) -> invoke `GetListItems`/`GetAttachment`/`GetList` as plain anonymous SOAP POSTs against a list from the WSDL (escalation: per the report's own Acunetix reference, C:H — NOT shown in the report) -> read (and, where the service exposes write ops, modify) SharePoint list content = the application's business data (terminal: BAC on list data — the concrete impact the chain reaches beyond structure disclosure).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/174632  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/174632.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 1,959 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Internet Bug Bounty (python), "Information disclosure in mmap module - python 2.7.12", reporter `aerodudrizzt`, created 2016-10-08, disclosed 2019-10-13, weakness Information Disclosure. Report states it was sent to python's security mailing list 2016-08-27 and fixed by benjamin 2016-10-05 (rev `144f10202076`).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Python build exercised, nothing sent to any target)
CLASS: internal memory read / sensitive info disclosure with PoC (CWE-125 + CWE-190) — `mmap.resize()` updates `size` but NOT `pos`, so a shrink leaves `pos > size` ("breaking the code's invariant"); `read()`'s only guard is an `assert()`, and `readline()` hands the negative `size - pos` to `memchr()` as `size_t` — the read crosses out of the mapped file into adjacent memory pages
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SURFACE (report): the `mmap` module "uses size_t pos, size for boundary checks before read/write access to the mmaped file".
  2. THE DESYNC (report verbatim): "The resize() function, in both windows and linux, updates only the size variable, and **ignores** the pos variable" — a "resize() that shrinks the mapped file can lead to a situation in which pos > size, thus breaking the code's invariant".
  3. THE read() ARM (report verbatim): "The only check is an assert() that size >= pos" (assert compiles out of release builds); then `n = self->size - self->pos;` ("n is now negative"), "n is adjusted to be: n = PY_SSIZE_T_MAX;", "n is now a poor upper bound to the read operation that follows".
  4. THE readline() ARM (report verbatim): "no size checks are done, the checks are passed to the memchr() function" — `memchr(start, '\n', self->size - self->pos)`; "memchr() receives size_t as the length parameter, thus making the size bound negligible"; the read will "steal" data from another page: `PyString_FromStringAndSize(start, (eol - start))`.
  5. THE CONSTRAINT + PROOF (report verbatim): "The exploitation will only work in case that there is an adjacent memory page, otherwise the read() / memchr() will cause a segfault while reading outside of the process memory mapped pages." — "I have exploited the vulnerability and demonstrated it's possible effects on a windows 7 computer" with the attached attack script and its output.
DETECTION SIGNAL: an object whose read APIs bound-check a cursor against a SIZE that a state-changing op (resize/truncate/compact/shrink) updates without moving the cursor; the invariant guard is an `assert()` only (gone under -O/NDEBUG); lengths computed as `size - pos` and passed to unsigned/size_t C functions; observed delta: read() after a shrink returns far more bytes than the object's current size (or segfaults — the adjacent-page miss).
CHAIN: a caller who can resize-shrink then read an attacker-reachable mapping (precondition: any code path where an untrusted party influences the shrink; the report demonstrates the library primitive directly) -> `pos > size` invariant break (primitive: state desync — one bound moved, the cursor not) -> `n = size - pos` negative -> clamped to `PY_SSIZE_T_MAX` / wrapped to a huge `size_t` in `memchr` (bypass: signedness wrap + assert-only guard) -> `read()`/`readline()` return bytes from ADJACENT memory pages (terminal: process-memory disclosure beyond the mapped file — secrets/keys/data of other objects resident in neighbouring pages; the report rates it "a major information leak").
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any library/API with resize-then-read semantics (mmap-backed caches, blob stores, image resize-then-download, byte-range/chunked readers): (1) create the object, advance the cursor/offset with a small read; (2) SHRINK it through the resize/truncate path; (3) call read()/readline()/range-read and measure the returned length against the object's current size — over-length returns = the wrap is live; a crash = the segfault branch; source variant: grep for boundary checks guarded ONLY by `assert()` and for `size - pos` values passed to `size_t` parameters. Rule: **a shrink path that moves the size but not the cursor breaks the invariant every later read trusts — and an assert() is not a check in a production build.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `174632`, `aerodudrizzt`, `PY_SSIZE_T_MAX`, `memchr`, `144f10202076`, `resize()`/`pos > size` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the mruby-engine entry (batch 291, `mmap(MAP_FIXED)` VA reclaim after a UAF) is a WRITE-side memory-corruption primitive, not a read-side bounds desync; #1131465 Ruby Tempfile and the openssl `s_server` §17.752 entries are filesystem path issues, not in-process memory reads. NEW: shrink-resize cursor desync + assert-only guard + size_t length wrap as an OOB-read recipe.
FRESHNESS: created 2016-10-08, disclosed 2019-10-13 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~7 years; Python 2.7 is EOL) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The defect shape (state-changing op updating one bound; assert-only check; signed length into an unsigned API) is re-detectable in any native-backed reader.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; nothing sent to any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (1,959 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/207013  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/207013.json` = HTTP 404 (twice) -> body recovered IN FULL (411 B) from the local parquet mirrors `/tmp/h1_reports.parquet` + `/tmp/h1_train.parquet` (duckdb on `/usr/bin/python3.12`, `visibility: full`); corroborated by the Wayback capture `20221004160902` whose `og:description` carries the same opening. The report's own reference, jira.atlassian.com/browse/CONF-39704 ("Insecure Direct Object Reference", Fixed, High, CVSS 7.7), was fetched IN FULL this pass and is labelled where used. Metadata: OLX, "Public Vulnerable Version of Confluence https://confluence.olx.com", reporter `hdbreaker`, created 2017-02-17, disclosed 2019-06-03, weakness Information Disclosure.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no request sent to olx.com or any target)
CLASS: internal file read via direct object reference on a template/decorator parameter (CWE-22/639) — `decoratorName` names a decorator template but accepts an absolute filesystem path, so any authenticated user reads any file the Confluence process can read
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SINK (report PoC verbatim): `GET: https://confluence.olx.com/spaces/viewdefaultdecorator.action?decoratorName=/WEB-INF/classes/confluence-init.properties`
  2. WHAT IT IS (report verbatim): "The public server is vulnerable to Insecure Direct Object Reference, allowing any authenticated user to read configuration files from the application such as the content of webapp directory in confluence."
  3. THE GENERAL FORM (report's own reference CONF-39704, fetched IN FULL this pass, verbatim): `http://<server>/spaces/viewdefaultdecorator.action?decoratorName=<FILE>` and `http://<server>/admin/viewdefaultdecorator.action?decoratorName=<FILE>` — "Where <FILE> any file readable by the user who runs the Confluence instance is accessible through Confluence itself." Reference PoC: `?decoratorName=/WEB-INF/web.xml`; "This has been verified in confluence 5.9.1, 5.8.15, and 5.8.14" (Affects Version/s: 5.5.7, 5.7, 5.6.6, 5.8.14, 5.8.15).
  4. THE PAYOFF FILES: `/WEB-INF/classes/confluence-init.properties` (the report's target — home dir + configuration), `/WEB-INF/web.xml` (the reference's target), "the content of webapp directory" — the app's own config/source inventory.
  5. THE FIX SHAPE (CONF-39704 workarounds, verbatim): "Do not run Confluence as root/administrator ... You can limit the impact of this bug by restricting what the app user account can access"; "Block URLs that match this pattern using proxy or load balancer rules" — the vendor's own response treats the parameter as a file-path primitive.
DETECTION SIGNAL: Confluence (self-hosted/DC): authenticated GET to `/spaces/viewdefaultdecorator.action?decoratorName=` (or `/admin/...`) returning rendered file content for a path outside the decorator directory. Generic: any template/decorator/layout/view "name" parameter that reaches a file loader and echoes the file — absolute paths (`/etc/...`, `/WEB-INF/...`) or `../` accepted.
CHAIN: any authenticated user (precondition: a low-priv account — the report's "any authenticated user"; the OLX instance was internet-facing) -> `decoratorName=<FILE>` absolute-path injection (primitive: template name = unconstrained file path) -> read `/WEB-INF/classes/confluence-init.properties`, `/WEB-INF/web.xml`, "the content of webapp directory" (terminal: internal file read = configuration/credential/source harvest from inside the app; CONF-39704 scores it CVSS 7.7 High).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every Confluence instance: authenticate as any user and `GET /spaces/viewdefaultdecorator.action?decoratorName=/WEB-INF/web.xml`, `?decoratorName=/WEB-INF/classes/confluence-init.properties`, and the `/admin/viewdefaultdecorator.action` twin — file content in the response = arbitrary file read. Generic form for any framework: find parameters named `*decorator*`, `*template*`, `*layout*`, `*view*`, `*theme*` that feed a file loader and submit absolute paths + `../`; walk up from a known webroot path to app config or `/etc/passwd`. Rule: **a template-name parameter is a file path — unless it is allow-listed to the template directory and basename-normalized, it is LFI.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `207013`, `hdbreaker`, `viewdefaultdecorator`, `decoratorName`, `CONF-39704`, `confluence-init` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.338 (Bugcrowd Atlassian/Confluence — client-supplied `mimeType` deny-list bypass -> stored XSS -> PAT minting; different parameter, class and chain) and the `WEB-INF` ledger hits (file-write sink catalog entries + the WebHelpDesk JSP hardcoded-cred harvest — write/cred classes, not renderer-parameter reads). NEW: `decoratorName` renderer-parameter LFI on Confluence + the `/spaces/` + `/admin/` twin-path probe.
FRESHNESS: created 2017-02-17, disclosed 2019-06-03 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~7.4 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The defect class (template name = file path) recurs across frameworks and the probe is one request per instance.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; nothing sent to olx.com or any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the material actually opened this pass: the 411 B body recovered from the local parquet mirrors, and the CONF-39704 page fetched via `web_extract`. Demonstrated boundary, stated plainly: the report demonstrates ONE file read (`confluence-init.properties`); the `/admin/` twin, the `web.xml` PoC and the "any file readable" wording come from the report's own reference (CONF-39704) and are labelled as such.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/576504  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/576504.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 4,456 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Revive Adserver, "Authentication Bypass by abusing Insecure crypto tokens in /lib/OA/Dal/PasswordRecovery.php", reporter `paulos__`, created 2019-05-10, disclosed 2019-05-21, weakness Improper Authentication - Generic.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Revive instance stood up; per doctrine §2(d) no token spray was run against any target)
CLASS: authentication bypass -> admin ATO via predictable (time-derived) password-reset tokens (CWE-330 -> CWE-287)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE TOKEN GENERATOR (report verbatim): `/lib/OA/Dal/PasswordRecovery.php` `generateRecoveryId($userId)`: `$recoveryId = strtoupper(md5(uniqid('', true))); $recoveryId = substr(chunk_split($recoveryId, 8, '-'), -23, 22);` — the password-reset token used to create new passwords for admins.
  2. WHY PREDICTABLE (report verbatim): `uniqid()` — "This function does not create random nor unpredictable string. This function must not be used for security purposes"; "without being passed any additional parameters the return value is little different from *microtime()*"; quoted PHP C source: `strpprintf(0, "%s%08x%05x", prefix, sec, usec)` — "The first four bytes are the current UNIX timestamp, and the last 20 bits are derived from the current time in microseconds. This gives a bit less than 2²⁰, or one million, possible results per given second." (md5 of the value adds no entropy.)
  3. THE CLOCK ORACLE (report verbatim): responses carry `Date: Thu, 09 May 2019 21:26:20 GMT` — "so we can easily know what timezone the server syncs and uses"; "locate the server's geoip and set their timezone similar to the server's timezone", then "request a password reset token simultaneously as they generate uniqid() from their side as well".
  4. THE ENUM LEG (report verbatim): "All an attacker needs is the email address of the account they reset (which can be enumurated in numerous ways, including by abusing *admin/password-recovery.php* by sending some email addresses until it says *Email Password Reset sent*)".
  5. THE SPRAY (report PoC verbatim): locally run the generator loop 10,000x — `for($i=0;$i<=10000;$i++){ $recoveryId = strtoupper(md5(uniqid('', true))); $recoveryId = substr(chunk_split($recoveryId, 8, '-'), -23, 22); print $recoveryId; }` — bracketing the generation window, then "automating this with Burp Intruder" against the token-consumption step -> set a new admin password -> "Authentication Bypass". Suggested fix (report): `openssl_random_pseudo_bytes()`.
DETECTION SIGNAL: reset/session/invite tokens whose generator derives from the CLOCK (`uniqid()`, `md5(uniqid(`, `time()`, `microtime()`, `md5(time`) — in source via grep, black-box via a local reimplementation that reproduces your own tokens from second+microsecond values; a `Date:` header (or any server timestamp) that lets the attacker bracket the generation window; a recovery endpoint that distinguishes valid from invalid emails ("Email Password Reset sent").
CHAIN: enumerate the target account via `admin/password-recovery.php`'s distinct response for valid emails (primitive: user enumeration) -> trigger a reset and bracket the generation window using the `Date:` header + timezone match (primitive: server-time oracle) -> precompute the `md5(uniqid(...))` candidate ladder for that window (~10^6/sec; the report used 10,000 candidates) and spray it at the token-consumption endpoint via Burp Intruder (bypass: time-derived token guessed offline; bounded, single-account per doctrine §2(d)) -> set the admin's password = authentication bypass -> admin panel of the ad server (terminal: admin ATO on the account-management surface; the report rates the finding "Authentication Bypass", "seem to affect all versions").
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every password-reset / magic-link / invite / verification token: (1) mint 5-10 tokens yourself and try to reproduce them with a local script fed only by the clock (`uniqid`/`md5(uniqid`/`md5(time`) — the report's loop IS the test script; (2) read the server `Date:` header, GeoIP-align your timezone, trigger the reset and generate candidates over the same window; (3) spray a bounded ladder at the consumption endpoint (one account, low volume); (4) check the recovery endpoint's response delta for valid vs invalid emails (the enum rung). Rule: **if a token can be reproduced by a script that only needs the CLOCK, hashing it does not help — the hash of the clock is still the clock; tokens must come from a CSPRNG (`openssl_random_pseudo_bytes`/`random_bytes`).**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `576504`, `paulos__`, `generateRecoveryId`, `PasswordRecovery.php`, `uniqid`, `chunk_split`, `recovery_id` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the recorded password-reset families all attack a DIFFERENT link — token returned in the reset-initiate RESPONSE (Uber, ledger ~2922), scalar->array type confusion fanning the token email to an attacker (GitLab §17.9), IDOR on the reset action itself (tag-manager family), host-header poisoning of the reset link (ledger ~1476/1489), and the checklist slot "Predictable/eternal password-reset tokens" (ledger line 635 — a hunt prompt with no recorded method); #730067 (Mail.ru am.ru, batch 36 POINTER) is "Improper Restriction of Authentication Attempts" = missing rate limit on a random token, whereas here the token is PREDICTED offline — no rate limit is bypassed, the token is precomputed. NEW: clock-derived reset-token precomputation + `Date:`-header window alignment as a recorded method.
FRESHNESS: created 2019-05-10, disclosed 2019-05-21 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~7.5 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The defect class (time-derived token) recurs in every framework's session/reset code and the test is generator-grep + one local script.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; no Revive instance stood up, no token generated or sprayed against any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (4,456 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/288955  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/288955.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 6,756 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: IRCCloud, "[IRCCloud Android] Theft of arbitrary files leading to token leakage", reporter `bagipro`, created 2017-11-09, disclosed 2017-11-15, weakness Privacy Violation. The report itself opens: "similar to my bug reported to Harvest https://hackerone.com/reports/161710" (that report is ALREADY recorded in this ledger — see DEDUPE/DELTA).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Android device/lab; no intent sent to any app)
CLASS: internal file read + arbitrary file write (mobile) — a copy handler derives BOTH the SOURCE uri and the DESTINATION filename from one caller-supplied EXTRA_STREAM uri; the filename is URL-DECODED (`getLastPathSegment()` — encoded `../` traversal escapes the cache dir) while the source read `openInputStream()` decodes the uri and FOLLOWS AN ATTACKER SYMLINK planted at the decoded path -> protected-file theft (-> token leakage -> ATO) AND arbitrary file overwrite
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SINK (report verbatim): exported `com.irccloud.android.activity.ShareChooserActivity` (manifest: `action.VIEW` + `action.SEND`, `data mimeType` application/audio/image/text/video) — `this.mUri = (Uri) getIntent().getParcelableExtra("android.intent.extra.STREAM"); // getting attacker provided uri` then `this.mUri = MainActivity.makeTempCopy(this.mUri, this); // copying file from this uri to /data/data/com.irccloud.android/cache/`.
  2. THE COPY (report verbatim): `makeTempCopy(Uri fileUri, Context context, String original_filename)` where `// original_filename = mUri.getLastPathSegment()`; source `InputStream is = ...getContentResolver().openInputStream(fileUri);` dest `Uri out = Uri.fromFile(new File(context.getCacheDir(), original_filename));`. Report verbatim: "It means that the specified file will be copied to `/data/data/com.irccloud.android/cache/` with original name. Original name is `getLastPathSegment()` from the specified uri. But there is one thing: this method decodes last path segment."
  3. THE TWO DECODE VIEWS OF ONE STRING (report PoC verbatim): attacker uri `file:///data/data/com.attacker/x/x/x/x/..%2F..%2F..%2F..%2Fsdcard%2Fprefs.xml` (`String zhk = "..%2F..%2F..%2F..%2Fsdcard%2Fprefs.xml"; // path to sdcard (encoded relative path from "/data/data/com.irccloud.android/cache/")`), plus a symlink at the DECODED canonical path: `Runtime.getRuntime().exec("ln -s /data/data/com.irccloud.android/shared_prefs/prefs.xml " + sumlinkFile.getAbsolutePath())`. Report's step 4, verbatim: "But `openInputStream(...)` automatically decodes the specified uri. So it will access my symlink file which points to `/data/data/com.irccloud.android/shared_prefs/prefs.xml`". Report's step 5, verbatim: `Uri out = Uri.fromFile(new File("/data/data/com.irccloud.android/cache/", "../../../../sdcard/prefs.xml"));` — "So it simply outputs the specified file to Sd card."
  4. TERMINAL (report verbatim): "attacker can force IRC Cloud app to copy arbitrary files to arbitrary directories. File `/data/data/com.irccloud.android/shared_prefs/prefs.xml` contains `session_key`. In normal situation this file is accessible only to IRC Cloud app. But when it's copied to e.g. Sd card it will be accessible to everyone. But Sd card is only simple example. Attacker can also force IRC Cloud app to copy a file to its internal directory." AND "BTW this vulnerability also allows to overwrite arbitrary files. So attacker also can replace any your protected files and substitute for example history."
  5. FIX SHAPE (report verbatim): "Just specify e.g. current timestamp as a file name, but don't use provided by attacker."
DETECTION SIGNAL: an exported IPC handler that copies from a caller-supplied SOURCE uri AND names the destination from the SAME uri's path segment; `getLastPathSegment()`/url-decoded filename joined to a directory (no basename()); a file copy that follows symlinks in caller-writable directories; the output path escaping the app's own dir. Tell: one request controls both ends of a copy, and each end resolves/decodes the string differently.
CHAIN: any third-party app on the device (precondition: installed, no permissions) -> explicit intent to the exported `ShareChooserActivity` with EXTRA_STREAM = `file:///data/data/<attacker>/x/x/x/x/..%2F..%2F..%2F..%2Fsdcard%2Fprefs.xml` + a symlink at the decoded path (primitive: caller controls source AND destination name) -> `openInputStream` resolves the decoded path through the symlink = READ of the victim app's protected `shared_prefs/prefs.xml`; `new File(cacheDir, "../../../../sdcard/prefs.xml")` = WRITE outside the cache dir (primitive: encoded-`../` traversal in the decoded filename) -> `session_key` lands world-readable on sdcard -> account takeover (terminal: token leakage -> ATO; demonstrated in the report with screenshots); overwrite variant (report): replace any protected file ("substitute for example history") = integrity loss inside the app dir.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every exported component that copies/attaches/stores a caller-supplied URI: (1) `adb shell am start -n <pkg>/<activity> -a android.intent.action.SEND -t text/plain --eu android.intent.extra.STREAM "file:///data/data/<attackerPkg>/x/x/x/x/..%2F..%2F..%2F..%2Fsdcard%2Fprobe.txt"` after planting a symlink at the DECODED path inside the attacker's own dir; (2) check where the copy lands — outside the app's cache dir? through the symlink to a file the attacker cannot read directly?; (3) escalate: point the symlink at `shared_prefs/*.xml`, `databases/*`, `app_webview/Cookies` and pull the copy from sdcard — grep for tokens; (4) overwrite variant: aim the destination at a victim file. Rule: **when one caller-controlled URI supplies BOTH the source and the destination name of a copy, compare how each side decodes/resolves it — a decode mismatch plus a planted symlink turns the handler into an arbitrary file READ and WRITE.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `288955`, `ShareChooserActivity`, `makeTempCopy`, `getLastPathSegment` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the #161710 Harvest entry (this ledger, same reporter — its sink copies a caller-supplied SOURCE uri into world-readable `/sdcard/.../<timestamp>.pdf` with a SERVER-chosen name; no caller-controlled filename, no traversal, no symlink — its own rule "classify the URI as SOURCE or DESTINATION" predates this entry's BOTH-AT-ONCE case); §17.728 (Reddit — exported deeplink router renders `file://` in the in-app browser); §17.603 (intent-supplied uri as the DESTINATION file NAME -> write traversal — the opposite single-direction case). NEW: the dual-view trick — one URI, decoded twice differently (source resolves the DECODED path through an attacker symlink; destination joins the DECODED last segment with `../`) — giving protected-file read AND arbitrary overwrite in one copy sink.
FRESHNESS: created 2017-11-09, disclosed 2017-11-15 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~9 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The defect shape (decoded filename + symlink-following copy) recurs in every platform's export/share/attach flows.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/200427  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/200427.json` = HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content`, `vulnerability_information` 0 B — the researcher body was withheld at disclosure and is NOT public on any route (recovery ladder tried IN ORDER: Wayback CDX captures exist from `20171220084816`; the earliest capture read = the JS shell whose `og:description` is the PROGRAM SUMMARY only; ajaysenr/HackerOne-Disclosed-Reports `reports/200427.md` = "*No vulnerability information available.*"; `web_search` = listing mirrors (ajaysenr, BugBountyHunter, h1.security.nathan.sx) + mzfr's "Exploiting Exported activities in Android apps" post which lists the title only). ROUTE THAT WORKED FOR THE METHOD: the public CLASS documentation of the exact technique the report title names — Oversecured, "Android: Access to app protected components" (https://oversecured.com/blog/android-access-to-app-protected-components, article dated 2020-08-05) fetched IN FULL this pass. The report contributes its title + team summary (quoted below); the technique text is the Oversecured class doc, labelled as such. Metadata: Slack, "Access of Android protected components via embedded intent", reporter `bagipro`, created 2017-01-22, disclosed 2017-07-17, weakness Privilege Escalation, Critical per aggregator metadata.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Android device/lab)
CLASS: privilege escalation (Android intent redirection — "embedded intent") — a proxy component takes an Intent EMBEDDED as a Parcelable extra (or parsed from an `intent://` URI via `Intent.parseUri()`) and hands it to `startActivity()`/`sendBroadcast()`/`startService()` -> a malicious unprivileged app reaches the victim app's NON-EXPORTED (protected) components with the victim app's own identity
CORE METHOD (SOURCE: Oversecured class doc, verbatim quotes — NOT the withheld report body):
  1. THE PRIMITIVE (Oversecured, verbatim): "Since class `Intent` is `Parcelable`, objects belonging to this class can be passed as extra data in another `Intent` object. Many developers make use of this feature and create proxy components (activities, broadcast receivers and services) that take an embedded Intent and pass it to dangerous methods like `startActivity()`, `sendBroadcast()`, etc."
  2. THE IMPACT (Oversecured, verbatim): "This is dangerous because an attacker can force the app to launch a non-exported component that cannot be launched directly from another app, or to grant the attacker access to its content providers." — "This leads to a violation of Android's security design, and nullifies all the access restrictions the developers have created." ("According to Oversecured statistics, more than 80% of apps contain this vulnerability.")
  3. THE WEBVIEW ARM (Oversecured, verbatim): "`WebView` also sometimes changes a URL from a string to an `Intent` object, using the `Intent.parseUri()` method, and passes it to `startActivity()`" — attacker builds an `intent://` scheme URL; exploitable remotely "including cross-site scripting on the server side or MitM on the client side", or via an exported activity/deeplink opening arbitrary links.
  4. ESCALATION (Oversecured, verbatim + the page's flag list): reach the victim app's non-exported Content Providers that have `android:grantUriPermissions` = `true` by embedding an intent with the attacker as recipient carrying `Intent.FLAG_GRANT_PERSISTABLE_URI_PERMISSION` ("permits persistent access to the provider"), `FLAG_GRANT_PREFIX_URI_PERMISSION` (prefix-wide access), `FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION` / `FLAG_GRANT_WRITE_URI_PERMISSION`; the "Attacks on Android File Provider" arm then reads "app files located in directories that the developer predetermined" via `ContentResolver`. Max class impact (Oversecured, verbatim): "theft of authentication details (the user's session), the forging of content within the app, and sometimes also to the execution of arbitrary code – for example, in situations where an attacker can acquire the ability to rewrite files and substitute for the native library."
  5. DEMONSTRATED BOUNDARY (the report's own public text — team summary, verbatim): "@bagipro found a vulnerability wherein a malicious and unprivileged app on the victim's phone could interact with any activity in the Slack Android app, allowing manipulation of the app in unintended ways." The report's exact component/extra names are NOT public — nothing Slack-specific beyond this is claimed.
DETECTION SIGNAL: exported activities/receivers/services that read an Intent out of an extra (`getParcelableExtra(...)`/`getSerializableExtra(...)`) or out of `Intent.parseUri(...)` and call `startActivity`/`sendBroadcast`/`startService` on it; WebViews launching `intent://` URLs. Tell: an exported trampoline sitting between any caller and the app's non-exported components.
CHAIN: malicious unprivileged app installed alongside the victim app (precondition: no permissions, no user interaction beyond installation) -> sends an intent to the exported proxy whose EXTRA carries an embedded Intent naming a NON-EXPORTED component (primitive: intent redirection — the app is the confused deputy) -> the victim app launches its own protected activity/receiver/service with its OWN identity (bypass of the export restriction) -> per the class doc: access auth/session activities and in-app manipulation ("interact with any activity" — team summary), non-exported `grantUriPermissions` content providers via `FLAG_GRANT_*_URI_PERMISSION` -> FileProvider read of app-private files (session theft), or write paths that "rewrite files and substitute for the native library" -> terminal per class doc: session theft / content forgery / arbitrary code execution. Slack's demonstrated boundary: interaction with any activity of the Slack Android app (team summary).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: static + dynamic pass on every Android app: (1) enumerate exported components (manifest intent-filters / `exported=true`); in each, grep the code for `getParcelableExtra`/`getSerializableExtra` feeding `startActivity`/`sendBroadcast`/`startService`, and for `Intent.parseUri` in WebViews; (2) fire an embedded intent: helper-app `Intent i = new Intent(); i.setClassName("<pkg>","<exported>"); i.putExtra("<extraName>", new Intent().setClassName("<pkg>","<NON-EXPORTED>")); startActivity(i);` — the protected component opening = the bypass; (3) escalate: embedded intent with `FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION|FLAG_GRANT_WRITE_URI_PERMISSION|FLAG_GRANT_PREFIX_URI_PERMISSION|FLAG_GRANT_PERSISTABLE_URI_PERMISSION` targeting the app's non-exported `android:grantUriPermissions="true"` provider / FileProvider, then read via `ContentResolver`; (4) remote arm: any in-app WebView/deeplink passing `intent://` URLs to `Intent.parseUri`. Rule: **an Intent inside an Intent is an open redirect for COMPONENTS — the app becomes the confused deputy for its own protected surface; allow-list embedded intent components (or don't launch caller-supplied intents at all).**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `200427`, `oversecured`, `Intent.parseUri`, `FLAG_GRANT_URI`, `embedded intent`, `Parcelable` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.728 + the #161710/#288955 exported-URI-copy family (there the extra is a URI misused to READ/WRITE FILES; here the extra is a whole INTENT used to REACH COMPONENTS — different primitive and different test); mzfr's post (blog listing `bagipro`'s exported-activity reports — listing only, no technique text). NEW: embedded-Parcelable-Intent redirection to non-exported components as a recorded method + the `FLAG_GRANT_*` content-provider/FileProvider escalation ladder.
FRESHNESS: report created 2017-01-22, disclosed 2017-07-17 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~9.8 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; the class doc is dated 2020-08-05. read because assigned. The class is platform-invariant (Android IPC design) and re-testable on every Android target.
HONESTY: the Slack report body is withheld (`visibility: no-content`) on EVERY route — the entry records the CLASS method from the Oversecured doc (named, dated, quoted verbatim), NOT invented report content; the report itself contributes only its title and team summary (quoted verbatim). Not reproduced; nothing sent to Slack or any target.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/219171  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/219171.json` = HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content`, `vulnerability_information` 0 B — researcher body withheld at disclosure (recovery ladder: Wayback CDX captures from `20170622034653`; earliest capture read = JS shell, `og:description` = VK team summary only; ajaysenr mirror = "*No vulnerability information available.*"). ROUTE THAT WORKED: the CREDITED PUBLIC WRITEUP by the reporter — Habr, `norver`, 2017-07-09, "Уязвимость ВКонтакте: отправляем сообщение с кодом восстановления страницы на чужой номер" (https://habr.com/ru/articles/332684/), fetched and read IN FULL this pass (whole article incl. the attack scheme); corroborated by the Google-indexed H1 page snippet ("Summary by norver", quoted below) and the VK team summary. Metadata: VK.com, title (ru) "Возможность взлома любого пользователя, не использующего двухфакторной аутентификации, через получения кода восстановления на чужой номер" ("Ability to take over any user not using 2FA by getting the recovery code sent to someone else's [the attacker's] number"), reporter `norver`, created 2017-04-06, disclosed 2017-05-20, weakness Privilege Escalation, Critical; writeup: $2,000 bounty, fixed within 17 hours; the same `libverify` library shipped in ICQ (same bug, +$1,000).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no request sent to VK or any target)
CLASS: authentication bypass -> ATO (business logic) — the SMS recovery-code SESSION is keyed by a client-generated `session_id` and NOT bound to the destination phone number: re-requesting the code with the SAME `session_id` for a DIFFERENT number delivers the SAME code to the attacker's phone; the request `signature` protecting the flow is client-side and defeatable by reversing the app
CORE METHOD (source: the Habr writeup by `norver`; quotes are marked translations of the Russian original):
  1. THE FLOW (writeup): MitM the VK Android app (Fiddler per Telerik's Android config); "Забыли пароль?" (Forgot password) -> after entering the phone number the app sends 2 HTTPS requests; "Особую ценность представляет второй, потому что именно он отвечает за отправку SMS с кодом восстановления" (the second one sends the recovery-code SMS). Params, verbatim: "`phone` — номер на который отправляется SMS" (the number receiving the SMS), "`session_id` — рандомно генерирующаяся сессия операции восстановления" (randomly generated recovery session). Tampering is blocked by a `signature` param (the code-check request is signed too).
  2. DEFEAT THE SIGNATURE (writeup): decompile (dex2jar + jd-gui), grep "signature" -> the obfuscated Mail.Ru `libverify` library: `localObject3 = String.format(Locale.US, "%s%s?%s&signature=%s", ... URLEncoder.encode(ru.mail.libverify.utils.m.b(f() + (String)localObject4 + ru.mail.libverify.utils.m.c(a.b())), "UTF-8"))` — `m.b()` is `MessageDigest.getInstance("MD5")` over the concatenated string (smali read). Patch the smali (`apktool.jar d vk.apk -r`, insert `Log.d("vk-research", p0)` at the head of `m.b()`), rebuild (`apktool.jar b vk -o newvk.apk`), re-sign, reinstall, `adb logcat`: the hashed string = url part + request params + a constant hash string `506e786f377863526a7558536c644968`. With the algorithm known the researcher minted his own signed requests from a C# client.
  3. RATE LIMITS HOLD (writeup): code = 4 digits (6 at time of writing), "одинаковые цифры не находились рядом" (no two identical digits adjacent) = ~6,500 variants; but the flow enforces `ATTEMPTLIMIT` (5 attempts) and `RATELIMIT`; changing IP/params/phone numbers does not help; different `session_id`s give different codes but still hit `RATELIMIT`.
  4. THE BUG (writeup, attack steps, translated verbatim): "Отправляем запрос на отправку SMS абоненту A с session_id C, ему приходит код 1234" (send the SMS request for subscriber A with session_id C -> A receives code 1234) / "Отправляем запрос на отправку SMS абоненту B с session_id C, ему приходит код 1234" (send the SMS request for subscriber B with the SAME session_id C -> B receives the SAME code 1234) / "Теперь, если абонент A знает номер телефона абонента B, он может восстановить его страницу. Восстанавливать можно было только ту страницу, на номер которой пришла последняя SMS с сходным session_id." (if A knows B's phone number, A can recover B's page; the recoverable page is the one whose number received the LAST SMS with that session_id — so A requests his own number FIRST, then the victim's).
  5. TERMINAL (writeup, translated verbatim): "Данная уязвимость позволяла взломать большинство аккаунтов в социальной сети, в безопасности были только аккаунты с двухфакторной авторизацией (у них нельзя делать восстановление по номеру телефона)." = the bug took over most accounts on the network; only 2FA accounts were safe (they cannot recover by phone). Report-side corroboration, "Summary by norver" on the H1 page (Google snippet, verbatim ru): "Из-за уязвимости можно было отправить код восстановления любой страницы на чужой номер, спасала только двухфакторная аутентификация." VK team summary (verbatim ru): "Уязвимость в библиотеке приложения VK на Android, позволяющая получить на свой номер код для восстановления некоторых страниц."
DETECTION SIGNAL: recovery flows carrying a CLIENT-generated session/correlation id next to the destination address, where the id (not the destination) keys the code/attempt state; a request-signing `signature` computed by an obfuscated client library with a static component. Oracle: request a code for YOUR OWN address with session S, re-request for the TARGET address with the SAME S — the target receiving (or the flow validating) the code you already hold = the session is unbound to the destination.
CHAIN: reverse the app's request signing (decompile -> smali log-patch of the MD5 signer -> re-implement `signature = MD5(url-part + params + constant-hash)` in a custom client) (primitive: client-side request-signing bypass — the "signature" protects nothing once reversed) -> request the recovery SMS to the attacker's OWN phone with `session_id` C and receive code 1234 (primitive: the attacker learns the code for session C) -> re-issue the SMS request for the VICTIM's phone with the SAME `session_id` C (primitive: recovery session not bound to destination — the same code is delivered to the victim; the victim's page becomes "the last SMS for session C") -> submit 1234 in the victim's recovery flow -> set a new password (terminal: ATO of any non-2FA account whose phone number is known — at scale; 2FA is the only mitigation because those accounts cannot recover by phone).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every SMS/email/voice recovery flow with a session id: (1) capture the recovery-initiate request; note the correlation/session id and destination fields; (2) initiate for YOUR address with session S and read the code; (3) re-initiate for the TARGET address reusing the SAME S — observe whether the target receives the same code or whether your known code validates in the target's flow (order matters: the recoverable account is the one that received the LAST SMS for S); (4) if client-side request signing blocks tampering, reverse the signer (apktool smali patch + logcat, or dynamic hook) and mint requests directly. Rule: **the recovery code must be bound server-side to (destination + session + attempt); a session id that can be re-pointed at another destination hands the attacker the victim's code — and a client-side signature only slows the attacker down.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `219171`, `norver` (as reporter), `libverify`, `session_id`-reuse, `506e786f377863526a7558536c644968` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the recorded password-reset families — token returned in the reset RESPONSE (Uber, ledger ~2922), scalar->array email fan-out (§17.9 GitLab), IDOR on the reset action (tag-manager family), host-header poisoning of the reset link (ledger ~1476/1489), clock-derived precomputable token (§17.756) — all attack token LEAK/PREDICTION, none re-points an existing recovery session at a second destination; the signed-request family (ledger 3126 canonicalization boundary-shift; 19939/19948 signature-not-binding-payload) — those exploit the SERVER's signature verification, here the signature is removed from the equation by CLIENT reversal and the defect is session↔destination binding; the SMS-code-bruteforce cluster (ledger 8632) — brute force is exactly what the attempt/rate limits stop here. NEW: recovery-session unbound to destination (same `session_id`, second number, same code; last-SMS rule) + the smali log-patch signature-reversal rung as a recorded method.
FRESHNESS: created 2017-04-06, disclosed 2017-05-20 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~9.5 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned. The binding defect is server-side and re-testable on any recovery flow regardless of app age.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; nothing sent to VK, ICQ or any target). The Habr comment thread (speculation about the code being "the same per session") was NOT used as source text — only the article body.
