SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/163938  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/163938.json` — HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content` (`vulnerability_information` 0 B BY DESIGN); the PUBLIC team summary + researcher summary in the same `.json` were read, and the researcher summary CREDITS the public writeup "How I snooped into your private Slack messages [Slack Bug bounty worth $2,500]" (blog.pentestnepal.tech/post/150381068912), fetched whole via `web_extract` (the credited-writeup rung of the ladder; no fallback needed). Metadata: Slack (`slack`), "Snooping into messages via email service", reporter `rijalrojan` (writeup signed "Written by uranium238"), created 2016-08-28, disclosed 2016-09-14, H1 severity None (unrated), weakness Improper Authentication - Generic, 30 upvotes, bounty yes.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Slack/email-SaaS tenant was claimed)
CLASS: authentication bypass / message interception via UNCLAIMED-TENANT CLAIM on the third-party EMAIL SaaS the target's MX records delegate to — the DNS delegation is LIVE and the service WORKS; only the per-hostname TENANT registration is missing (dangling tenant, not dangling DNS)
CORE METHOD:
  1. Recon the target's MX records for the apex AND every tenant subdomain (writeup verbatim: "all the sub-domains of slack.com like teamname.slack.com also had MX set to the same service. These team domains were not claimed, so the emails for these domains could also be intercepted").
  2. On the third-party email service, enumerate which routed hostnames are CLAIMED tenants (writeup verbatim: "slack.com was already claimed" — the apex — while the team subdomains were not).
  3. Register/claim an unclaimed routed hostname as your own tenant and set an inbound routing rule (writeup verbatim: "set the route in a way that all emails coming to @uraniumsecteam.slack.com would arrive to my inbox").
  4. Harvest: messages addressed to the product's email-integration address (`<hashed>@<team>.slack.com`, the Slack Email app's per-channel address — writeup: "The hashedtext was randomly generated to make sure they cannot be enumerated") now land in the attacker's route; the attacker can read, modify or re-forward them (writeup verbatim: "despite the team members sending a message to ... they would not receive it because the hacker just intercepted it. He could then chose to forward the message to team, modify or edit it.").
DETECTION SIGNAL: in-scope hostnames (apex + per-tenant subdomains) whose MX points at a multi-tenant email SaaS/gateway where each customer hostname = one tenant; the provider shows a routed hostname as available/unclaimed while mail for it is live; product features that accept INBOUND email (channel-by-email, ticket-by-email, upload-by-email) whose address namespace is `<random>@<tenant-hostname>`.
CHAIN: enumerate target MX hostnames (public DNS) -> find a per-tenant hostname still routed but UNCLAIMED at the email SaaS (primitive: the delegation outlives the tenant registration) -> claim it and route to attacker inbox (bypass: the provider grants the tenant by registration alone; no DNS change needed) -> intercept every message to the product's email-integration address = read/modify private channel messages (the demonstrated impact; team summary verbatim: "would allow messages in Slack email integrations to be leaked"; the writeup's "snoop into private slack messages" and Slack's tier-0 escalation) -> any credential-bearing mail into that namespace (invites, forwarded resets) is an ATO rung — flagged as the standard consequence, NOT demonstrated in-report. H1 unrated; recorded at its rated weight.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every in-scope hostname run `dig MX <host>` and `dig MX <sub>.<host>`; for each MX target that is a multi-tenant email SaaS, attempt to register/claim each routed hostname as a tenant (sign-up, "add domain", note whether DNS proof is required); where a claim succeeds WITHOUT DNS verification, set a catch-all route to your inbox and trigger the product's email-in feature — win = a message to the app's channel/ticket address lands in your inbox. Rule: **a live MX delegation implies a tenant per hostname at the provider — verify every routed hostname's TENANT is claimed, not just that DNS resolves.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `163938`, `uranium238`, `pentestnepal`, `snooped` = 0 hits across the three write targets (the single `rijalrojan` hit is a batch-2xx NOTE about Shopify #425719 — a different report). Related-but-distinct REVIEWED and NOT conflated: the takeover FAMILY is recorded with all carriers — §1.6, #154425 (dangling CNAME -> dead CDN service claim), #1342422 (NS -> unclaimed zone), §17.310 (pre-verification grace window), §17.377 (CNAME -> unregistered domain), #1406335 (S3 bucket re-create), §17.644 (unclaimed bucket in install script) — and batch-303's #474798 was DECLINED as a family instance ("method and test unchanged"); this is a CARRIER DELTA in that family per the ledger-5416 precedent (MX delegation -> unclaimed EMAIL-SERVICE TENANT; the service is LIVE, only the tenant registration is missing), and its terminal belongs to the ALREADY-RECORDED email-interception family (§17.78 cross-account config overwrite -> email interception; §17.461 SMTP-config hijack; #1067547 exposed mail catcher; the inbound-`From:`-forgery gateway entry) -> ENTRY ONLY, NO §17 slot. The Cloudflare #1419341 entry OVERWRITES a victim's live routing config; here the victim tenant does not exist at all.
FRESHNESS: created 2016-08-28, disclosed 2016-09-14 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (~10 years) — recorded as BACKFILL, flagged; read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (knowledge intake only). Every quote above is verbatim from the material actually opened this pass: the live `.json` (team summary verbatim: "discovered a vulnerability with a 3rd party email integration provider which would allow messages in Slack email integrations to be leaked. We worked with the 3rd party to get this issue resolved"; researcher summary crediting the blog) and the credited public writeup fetched whole via `web_extract`.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3648681  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3648681.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 11,019 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked; the body carries the full source of `poc_basic.js` and the walk-through of the attached `poc_full_demo.js`). Metadata: Node.js (`nodejs`), "Improper Input Validation — HTTP Response Parser Unconditionally Accepts Bare CR in Status Line", reporter `saif-01`, created 2026-04-04, disclosed 2026-07-01, severity medium, weakness HTTP Request Smuggling, no CVE on the record; affected component `deps/llhttp/src/llhttp.c` state `s_n_llhttp__internal__n_res_line_almost_done` (line 6130); report's affected range: "Node.js up to v24.14.1 (llhttp v9.3.0 / v9.3.1)"; "No Lenient Flags Required ... strict mode with `insecureHTTPParser: false` (the default)".
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced as an exploit — MEASURED NEGATIVE on this host's Node v22.23.4 / llhttp 9.4.3: the report's exact T2 payload is REJECTED with `Parse Error: Expected LF after CR`; corroborating evidence read this pass: the Node.js 2026-06-18 security release bumped llhttp to 9.4.2 on all lines, and the report itself bounds the bug to llhttp <= 9.3.1)
CLASS: HTTP response smuggling / RESPONSE QUEUE POISONING — RESPONSE-side line-terminator leniency ASYMMETRY: llhttp's response path accepts a BARE CR (a second `\r`) as the status-line terminator in STRICT mode while the request path correctly rejects it, so a strict front proxy and a Node HTTP CLIENT disagree on where the response ends (CWE-444)
CORE METHOD (the report's own payloads and tables):
  1. The defect (report verbatim): the second `\r` after the status line "is consumed and treated as a valid line ending, transitioning the parser to header processing without requiring the mandatory LF that RFC 7230 §3.1.2 demands"; at `res_line_almost_done` the `default` branch falls into the lenient-flag check and the bare CR is accepted "Despite this flag being **unset in strict mode**".
  2. Minimal repro (report's `poc_basic.js`, verbatim payload): a raw-TCP backend answers `HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\rContent-Length: 4\r\n\r\nEvil`; `http.request({..., insecureHTTPParser: false})` parses `Status: 200`, headers `{"content-length":"4"}`, body `Evil`.
  3. The ASYMMETRY (report step 2 / test T6): the REQUEST path (`req_http_complete_crlf`) accepts LF only — `GET / HTTP/1.1\r\rHost: localhost\r\n\r\n` gets `HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request`. Test table: T2 `HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\r...` ACCEPT (bug), T3 `\r\r\n` REJECT, T4 bare LF REJECT, T5 bare CR + Content-Length ACCEPT (bug).
  4. Exploitation (report's `poc_full_demo.js` walk-through): a STRICT reverse proxy reads `\r\r` as "status complete + empty line = response done (no body)", keeps `Content-Length: 6\r\n\r\nHello A` as ORPHAN DATA ("Keeping 28 bytes as ORPHAN DATA") in its connection pool, and PREPENDS it to the NEXT client's request ("Proxy prepended orphan data to Client B's request" -> backend sees `Content-Length: 6\r\n\r\nHello AGET /page2 HTTP/1.1...` -> `400 Bad Request`) — cross-client response queue poisoning.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any llhttp-based RESPONSE parser in the chain (Node `http.request`/undici fetchers, Node reverse proxies, Node-powered webhook / link-preview / import-from-URL / image-proxy fetchers) adjacent to a strict RFC-7230 response parser; the probe is any origin you control answering `HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\rContent-Length: N\r\n\r\n<body>`; the tell is one hop accepting it as a complete response while its neighbour rejects it and retains orphan bytes.
CHAIN: attacker-controlled upstream response (primitive: any server-side URL fetch, or a malicious/compromised backend) -> status line terminated `\r\r` (primitive: response-parser terminator leniency) -> strict proxy reads response-complete and stores the remainder as orphan data (bypass: the two parsers disagree on the response boundary) -> orphan bytes are prepended to the NEXT user's request on the pooled connection (escalation: cross-client desync) -> cache poisoning ("a proxy caching responses based on its (wrong) parsing boundaries could cache partial/incorrect content served to other users"), credential exposure ("orphan data containing sensitive headers/body from one user's response gets prepended to another user's request, potentially leaking into server logs or error messages"), cross-client response leakage and DoS (400s on legitimate requests) — the report's own scenario list; demonstrated in-report = the desync + the victim 400; the credential/session rungs are scenario claims, flagged per doctrine §8.5. H1 rated medium.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every server-side URL fetcher and every proxy hop, aim a URL you control that answers `HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\rContent-Length: 5\r\n\r\nHELLO` and diff (a) what the llhttp hop parses (expect: 200 / content-length 5 / body HELLO) vs (b) what a strict hop parses (expect: complete response with NO body + orphan bytes retained); then run the request-path control `GET / HTTP/1.1\r\rHost: x\r\n\r\n` (expect 400) to prove the asymmetry is response-only. Rule: **probe line-terminator leniency in BOTH parser directions of every hop — a response path that accepts a terminator the request path rejects is a response-queue-poisoning primitive.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3648681`, `saif-01`, `res_line_almost_done`, `LENIENT_OPTIONAL_LF_AFTER_CR`, `orphan data` = 0 hits across the three write targets. The recorded REQUEST-side llhttp corpus REVIEWED as related-but-distinct: CVE-2023-30589 (ledger ~8485 — bare CR as the HEADER-FIELD delimiter in inbound REQUEST parsing, the next byte eaten, TE obfuscation; the same entry's MEASURED NEGATIVE notes the request path REJECTING `\r\r` on v22.23.4, which is exactly this report's T6), CVE-2022-32213 (TE token re-match `chunkedchunked`), CVE-2022-32214/32215/35256 (lone-LF / empty-value header-line delimiters), #3723248 §17.471 (`Connection: close<TAB>` drops `CONNECTION_CLOSE`), §17.235/§17.236 (CRLF-injection desync; single-LF block-termination detector). NONE records the RESPONSE STATUS-LINE terminator (`res_line_almost_done`) nor response queue poisoning via orphan data. NEW general method -> §17.774.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-04-04, disclosed 2026-07-01 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window.
HONESTY: not reproduced as an exploit (see STATUS; the measured negative is a real local run this pass — `node v22.23.4`, loopback only). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` body read IN FULL this pass.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3780806  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3780806.json` — HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content` (`vulnerability_information` 0 B BY DESIGN); the PUBLIC team summary (author `mbeccati`) in the same `.json` was read, then the CVE record CVE-2026-50740 (cve.org/NVD, opened this pass) and the Revive Adserver CHANGELOG 6.0.8 section (github.com/revive-adserver/revive-adserver/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md, opened whole via `web_extract`) to ground the fix and the sibling surface first-hand. Metadata: Revive Adserver (`revive_adserver`), "Reflected XSS via unsanitised refresh parameter in zone invocation tag", reporter `kanon4`, created 2026-06-03, disclosed 2026-06-25, severity medium (NVD CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:L/UI:R/S:C/C:L/I:L/A:N 5.4), weakness Cross-site Scripting (XSS) - Reflected, fixed in 6.0.8 (released 2026-06-25).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Revive instance stood up; the exact payload shape is not public and is NOT invented)
CLASS: reflected XSS via a NUMERIC-TRUSTED parameter reflected raw into GENERATED INVOCATION-TAG output — the sink is the ad-tag / iFrame-embed code the generator EMITS, not a rendered page
CORE METHOD (team summary + CVE record verbatim; the payload shape is withheld):
  1. Surface (team summary verbatim): "a missing sanitisation of user input in the zone-include.php script of Revive Adserver 6.0.7 and earlier" — `zone-include.php` is the iFrame invocation-tag generator.
  2. Parameter (team summary verbatim): "A low-privileged user could exploit the refresh parameter of the iFrame invocation tag to perform reflected XSS attacks."
  3. Root cause (CVE-2026-50740 record, verbatim): "A missing sanitisation vulnerability of user input in the zone-include.php script" — a parameter treated as trusted numeric/enum data flows into the generated HTML without encoding; fixed in 6.0.8.
  4. SIBLING surface family (CHANGELOG 6.0.8, opened this pass, verbatim): "CVE-2026-34915: Reflected XSS via clientid parameter in zone-include.php", "CVE-2026-50743: CSRF in zone-include.php allows unauthorized banner and campaign linking", "CVE-2026-50745: Reflected XSS in stats-video.php via improperly encoded URL parameters" — one generator surface, several parameters.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any endpoint that EMITS code/markup for embedding (ad/invocation tags, widget builders, "copy this snippet", tracking-pixel builders, oEmbed, newsletter merge tags) and reflects query parameters into the emitted snippet; params that LOOK numeric/enum (`refresh`, `timestamp`, `cachebust`, `width`, `height`, `target`) are the prime suspects — type-trusted, so they skip string escaping.
CHAIN: a low-privileged account — or anyone who can craft the tag URL, since the invocation endpoint is the PUBLIC embed generator -> `refresh` value reflected raw into the generated iFrame/JS tag (primitive: output encoding missing on a type-trusted param) -> any victim viewing/executing the crafted tag (an admin previewing generated tags, or a visitor of a page embedding the tag URL) runs attacker JS in the ad-server origin (escalation: the CVE's S:C scope change) -> session cookie theft, CSRF against the Revive administration interface, ad-campaign manipulation (the CVE-recorded impact chain).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on every tag/invocation/embed-code generator: (1) enumerate its parameters and fire a unique marker per param into the generated output; (2) diff which params echo RAW into the emitted snippet; (3) re-test echoing params with `'"><img src=1 onerror=prompt(1)>` AND numeric/JS-context breakouts (`1-alert(1)-1`, `1;alert(1)//` — the param may be interpolated into a JS literal inside the snippet); (4) walk every SIBLING parameter and every verb of the same script (`clientid` here; `stats-video.php` params). Rule: **the sanitizer must run on the GENERATOR's OUTPUT, not only on pages — a type-trusted numeric param gets no escaping, and the generated tag executes wherever it is embedded.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3780806`, `kanon4`, `zone-include`, `revive adserver`, `invocation tag`, `CVE-2026-50740` = 0 hits across the three write targets. Related-but-distinct REVIEWED and NOT conflated: reflected XSS is an OWNED class (recorded carriers: content-type-sniffed JSONP/CSV responses, secondary render paths §17.27 + ledger 2568/3600/8649, `javascript:` redirect sinks §17.83-material, DOM `document.URL` sinks, Steam BBCode/oEmbed chat chains) — NONE records a GENERATED-CODE / invocation-tag emitter where a numeric-trusted param reflects into the emitted snippet. Per the ledger-5416 carrier-delta precedent: ENTRY ONLY, NO §17 slot. (The IBM APAR IV88400 `WebProcess.srv` `objectId`/`actionId` XSS seen during the source-5 writeup search is a different, 2015 product finding — NOT this technique, not conflated.)
FRESHNESS: created 2026-06-03, disclosed 2026-06-25 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window.
HONESTY: not reproduced. Every quote above is verbatim from the material actually opened this pass: the live `.json` team summary, the CVE-2026-50740 record (cve.org/NVD), and the Revive CHANGELOG 6.0.8 section read whole via `web_extract`. The report body is withheld by design; no payload was invented.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3692858  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3692858.json` — HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content` (`vulnerability_information` 0 B BY DESIGN); the PUBLIC team summary (author `security-release-stewards`) in the same `.json` was read, then the Node.js security-release blog (nodejs.org/en/blog/vulnerability/june-2026-security-releases, opened whole via `web_extract`) and the Red Hat CSAF/Bugzilla text for CVE-2026-48617 (opened this pass) for the mechanism statement. Metadata: Node.js (`nodejs`), "Permission Model Bypass via `process.report.writeReport()` Path Misvalidation", reporter `suul`, created 2026-04-24, disclosed 2026-06-18, severity low (H1; NVD 8.2 HIGH — divergence noted, recorded at the report's rated weight), weakness Improper Access Control - Generic, CWE-73 (External Control of File Name or Path), CVE-2026-48617, fixed in Node 22.23.0 / 24.17.0 / 26.3.1.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — the exact misvalidation shape is not public; local check this pass on the FIXED build: `node --permission --allow-fs-read=/tmp -e 'process.report.writeReport("<outside>")'` on v22.23.4 -> `ERR_ACCESS_DENIED`, which confirms the fix and the test shape)
CLASS: sandbox escape / permission-model bypass (CWE-73) — the fs allow-list is enforced INSIDE the fs subsystem, while `process.report.writeReport()` is a NON-fs builtin that accepts an output PATH and performs its own (weaker) path validation -> restricted code writes a diagnostic report OUTSIDE the granted tree
CORE METHOD (advisory-level — the report body is withheld; quotes verbatim from the sources opened this pass):
  1. Precondition (Red Hat CSAF statement, verbatim): "This flaw affects Node.js's experimental Permission Model, a security feature that restricts filesystem, network, and process access when explicitly enabled via the --permission flag. Applications running with the default Node.js configuration (Permission Model not enabled) are not affected."
  2. The flaw (same statement, verbatim): "insufficient path validation in process.report.writeReport() could allow code running under a restricted permission scope to write a diagnostic report to a location outside the configured allow-list, resulting in low-severity information disclosure." Team summary (verbatim): "A flaw in Node.js Permission Model enforcement allows Bypass via `process.report.writeReport()` Path Misvalidation. This can lead to confidentiality impact or bypass of the intended security boundary under affected configurations. This vulnerability affects all supported release lines: **Node.js 22**, **Node.js 24**, and **Node.js 26**."
  3. The test shape: run constrained code (`node --permission --allow-fs-read=<dir>`) that calls `process.report.writeReport('<path OUTSIDE the allow-list>')`; pre-fix the diagnostic report (process internals: env, stack, handles) lands outside the grant; post-fix `ERR_ACCESS_DENIED` (measured this pass on v22.23.4).
DETECTION SIGNAL: any sandbox/policy layer whose allow-list is enforced PER-SUBSYSTEM (fs module, net module) while other builtins accept the same argument class (a PATH) and validate it themselves — `process.report.writeReport()`, `process.report.getReport()`, diagnostics/report APIs, plugin exporters, "save as" functions. Tell: the sandbox audit stops at `fs.*` because "the permission model covers file access".
CHAIN: attacker-supplied code running inside a `--permission` process (plugin / template / scripting surface — the standing precondition of this family) (primitive) -> calls `process.report.writeReport()` with a path outside the fs allow-list (bypass: the API's own path validation is weaker than the central enforcement) -> the diagnostic report — process internals — is written outside the sandbox (escalation: the write boundary is crossed AND the content is an information disclosure) -> low-severity confidentiality impact per the advisory; a write primitive aimed at a location later read/executed by a privileged consumer is an escalation rung, NOT demonstrated — flagged. H1 rated LOW; recorded at its rated weight.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: in any sandboxed-code context, enumerate EVERY builtin that accepts a PATH (fs.*, but also process.report.*, diagnostics, export/save functions) and call each with (a) an absolute path outside the grant and (b) a traversal (`/tmp/../etc/x`), diffing each verdict against the central allow-list; a builtin that writes where `fs.writeFile` is denied is the escape. Rule: **the allow-list must be enforced in ONE place every path-accepting API routes through — per-API path validation is a coverage gap, and the outlier API is the escape.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3692858`, `48617`, `writeReport`, `suul` = 0 hits across the three write targets. The Node permission-model FAMILY is recorded, one distinct mechanism each: #2256167 (type-coverage gap: `Uint8Array` not normalized), §17.170 (canonicalizer-vs-opener trust split), #2434811/§17.186 (the check's own helper monkey-patched), #2225660 + #2092852 + #1952978 (fs path traversal, CVE-2023-39331/30584), §17.455 (network/UDS coverage gap, CVE-2026-21636), CVE-2025-23083 (diagnostics-channel internal-worker leak, ledger ~17463) — NONE records a NON-fs path-accepting builtin with its own weaker validation. Per the §17.455 precedent (advisory-text-only coverage-gap variant earned a slot): NEW general method -> §17.775.
FRESHNESS: created 2026-04-24, disclosed 2026-06-18 — INSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window.
HONESTY: not reproduced (see STATUS; the fix-verdict measurement on v22.23.4 is a real local run this pass). The exact misvalidation mechanism (traversal vs absolute path vs type) is NOT public and was NOT invented.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/3680680  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/3680680.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 5,734 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Metadata: curl (`curl`), "libcurl omits IPv6 zoneid from host identity and leaks credentials/cookies across scoped link-local realms", reporter `valvelvel`, created 2026-04-17, disclosed 2026-04-19, severity medium, weakness Information Disclosure; the record's `cve_ids: ['CVE-2022-27775']` is DISPUTED by the reporter in the body (verbatim: "This appears distinct from CVE-2022-27775, which was about connection reuse / conncache keying rather than redirect, cookie, and credential host identity") — noted as-is.
STATUS: VERIFIED (core mechanism reproduced first-hand this pass — the cookie-identity leg: a `Set-Cookie` fetched from `http://[fe80::250:56ff:fe67:6e0%25eth0]:18080/` on this host is jarred under the key `fe80::250:56ff:fe67:6e0` with NO zoneid (curl 8.5.0, local IPv6 listener, loopback/lab only), exactly the report's repro-2 jar line; the cross-zone redirect-auth and .netrc legs were NOT rebuilt — no two-zone same-address lab was built)
CLASS: credential/state leakage via HOST-IDENTITY-KEY vs ROUTING-KEY disagreement — the IPv6 zoneid (`%zone`) is honored by the transport to SCOPE the connection but OMITTED from the identity comparisons gating redirect-credential forwarding, cookie storage/selection and `.netrc` lookup, so two distinct link-local realms collapse into ONE identity (CWE-284 / CWE-668)
CORE METHOD (the report's own repros; the cookie leg run this pass):
  1. Lab shape (report verbatim): two IPv6 bridge networks; "Two containers are given the same MAC address so they receive the same link-local IPv6 address on different interfaces, which creates two distinct scoped destinations with the same numeric fe80:: address."
  2. Redirect-protected secret forwarding (repro 1): `%zoneA` answers `HTTP/1.0 302 Found` + `Location: http://[fe80::42:acff:fe11:2%25br-B]:18080/target`; `curl -g -v -L --oauth2-bearer s3cr3t "http://[fe80::42:acff:fe11:2%25br-A]:18080/start"` follows and STILL sends `Authorization: Bearer ***` to the %br-B realm (report verbatim: "This happens without --location-trusted / CURLOPT_UNRESTRICTED_AUTH"); the same with `-u user:secret`, `-H 'Authorization: ***'`, `-H 'Cookie: test=yes'`.
  3. Host-only cookies cross-zone (repro 2): a cookie jarred from `%zoneA` is keyed `fe80::42:acff:fe11:2` (no zone — REPRODUCED this pass on curl 8.5.0 with a local listener) and is then SENT to `%zoneB` (same numeric address).
  4. `.netrc` cross-zone (repro 3): `machine fe80::42:acff:fe11:2 login user password secret` applies to BOTH realms (`Authorization: Basic dXNlcjpzZWNyZXQ=` sent to each).
  5. Root cause (report verbatim): `lib/urlapi.c::Curl_url_same_origin()` "compares scheme/host/port but ignores CURLUPART_ZONEID"; `lib/vauth/vauth.c::Curl_auth_allowed_to_host()` "compares only host/port/protocol"; `lib/cookie.c` "key cookies on normalized host strings without zoneid"; `.netrc` lookup uses `conn->host.name`; while `lib/url.c::zonefrom_url()` "later extracts CURLUPART_ZONEID and sets conn->scope_id" for ROUTING.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any client/server library where a URL carries a ROUTING-relevant component (IPv6 zoneid, port suffix, scheme, unix-socket path, SNI) that the connect layer honors but the SECURITY-IDENTITY key (same-origin check, cookie domain key, auth-forwarding guard, credential lookup) ignores; multi-homed hosts, containers/service meshes, local automation using `fe80::`-scoped URLs. Tell: the same numeric address reachable under two scope ids.
CHAIN: attacker controls one scoped/link-local realm OR an open redirect on it (primitive) -> the victim's client follows a redirect / reuses its cookie jar / `.netrc` into the SAME NUMERIC address in a different zone (bypass: the identity key omits the zoneid, so the "same host" guard passes) -> built-in credentials, Bearer tokens, protected custom headers, host-only cookies and `.netrc` credentials are sent to the other realm (escalation: cross-realm secret flow with NO `--location-trusted` opt-in) -> confidentiality loss / cross-realm session-state confusion on multi-homed systems, automation, containers and service meshes (report self-rated Medium: "the attack surface is narrower than a generic internet-wide cross-host leak").
TRANSFERABLE TEST: for every URL-consuming client: (1) build the two-realm lab (two bridges/namespaces, cloned MAC = same fe80 address, different zone) or, short of that, verify the KEY FORMAT — jar a cookie from a `%zone`-scoped URL and check whether the stored key carries the zoneid (measured this pass: it does not); (2) replay the three legs — `curl -g -L` with `--oauth2-bearer`/`-u`/`-H 'Authorization'` across a zoneA->zoneB redirect, cookie reuse across zones, `.netrc` machine match; (3) generalize beyond IPv6: diff the IDENTITY KEY used by same-origin/auth/cookie/credential guards against the ROUTING KEY used by the transport, for every URL component. Rule: **every component the router uses to distinguish destinations must be part of the identity key — an ignored component is a cross-realm trust collapse.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `3680680`, `valvelvel`, `CURLUPART_ZONEID`, `zonefrom_url`, `CVE-2022-27775` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets. Related-but-distinct REVIEWED and NOT conflated: §17.188 ("a redirect-following client forwards credentials across host AND scheme — curl") is a DIFFERENT bug — there curl forwards across a RECOGNIZED host change; here the host change is INVISIBLE to the guard because identity omits the zoneid. The "validation and resolution are separate steps" family (ledger 4118/21529) and §17.170 are check-vs-consumer splits on a PATH/URL; this is an identity-key-vs-routing-key split in the credential-scoping layer. The fail-open family (§17.212, §17.630, #287245/§17.773) fails open on EMPTY results, not on omitted identity components. NEW general method -> §17.776.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2376909  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/2376909.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 4,074 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked; the report embeds the linked `DocumentAPIController.php` source excerpt). Metadata: Nextcloud `richdocuments` (Nextcloud Office / Collabora), "Possible to enumerate valid files in password protected shares/files drop shares as well as spam folder with files", reporter `lukasreschke`, created 2024-02-17, disclosed 2025-02-21, severity low, weakness Information Disclosure; asset `nextcloud/richdocuments` (SOURCE_CODE).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Nextcloud instance stood up; the root cause is quoted verbatim from the live body + the linked GitHub source line).
CLASS: BAC / authorization-gate bypass (CWE-284 / CWE-863) — a share's access gate (password-protected flag + read-vs-write / upload-only permission bits) is enforced on the primary file API but NOT re-applied on the share-token file-CREATE endpoint (`DocumentAPIController#create`), which resolves the share by token and acts anyway -> (a) a filename-existence ORACLE on password-protected shares and (b) unauthorized empty-file creation (spam) into shares the caller cannot write to.
CORE METHOD (report body + linked source read IN FULL this pass; quotes verbatim):
  1. Surface: `DocumentAPIController#create` in `nextcloud/richdocuments` (report links `lib/Controller/DocumentAPIController.php#L70-L149`) is a file-creation API taking an OPTIONAL `shareToken`.
  2. The missing check (report verbatim): `DocumentAPIController#create` "is not validating whether the share is writable, upload only or password protected." When `shareToken !== null` it runs `$share = $this->shareManager->getShareByToken($shareToken)` then `$rootFolder = $share->getNode()` — resolving the share WITHOUT ever consulting the share password or its read/write/upload-only permission bits.
  3. ORACLE (report step 3): "As attacker send a request to `DocumentAPIController#create` to enumerate the valid files" — the handler calls `$folder->nodeExists($fileName)` and returns `"File already exists"` (STATUS_BAD_REQUEST) if the name is taken vs proceeds to create otherwise; the error divergence distinguishes existing vs non-existing filenames inside a share the attacker cannot even read (password-protected).
  4. WRITE/SPAM (report step 4): "As attacker send a request to `DocumentAPIController#create` to spam files" — the writability/upload-only flag is never checked, so attacker-supplied `fileName` reaches `$folder->newFile($fileName)` + `putContent(...)` = attacker-named empty documents land in the share.
  5. Params that matter: `mimeType`, `fileName`, `directoryPath`, `shareToken`, `templateId`; the `shareToken` branch is the gate-skipping path.
DETECTION SIGNAL: any share-link / file-drop / document platform with a token-gated CREATE (or upload/insert/attach) endpoint that resolves the share by token (`getShareByToken`) and acts WITHOUT re-checking the share password and its read-vs-write-vs-upload-only permission bits. Tell: the same token against the primary browse/download API IS password-gated / read-only, but the create endpoint accepts it bare; and the create endpoint's error strings ("File already exists" vs "Cannot create document") diverge on name presence = a filename-existence oracle.
CHAIN: attacker holds only a share LINK (no password) for a password-protected or upload-only Files-Drop share (primitive) -> calls `DocumentAPIController#create` with that `shareToken` (primitive: create resolves the share by token) -> the share password + writability gate is never applied (bypass: the check runs on the primary file API, not on create) -> (a) iterate candidate `fileName`s and read the "File already exists"/success divergence to ENUMERATE valid filenames inside the password-protected share (escalation: filename-existence oracle past the password), and/or (b) `newFile($fileName)` writes attacker-named empty documents into a share the caller cannot write (escalation: unauthorized write). Demonstrated impact (report's own framing) = filename enumeration + empty-file spam on password-protected / Files-Drop shares. The rung to full content READ of the share's files is a DIFFERENT missing gate (the Text-app attachment download — this batch's source 6 / §17.777 read carrier) — flagged as the sibling, NOT claimed here. Program rated LOW (info disclosure); recorded at its rated weight.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any share-link / Files-Drop / password-protected file platform, for every token-gated secondary action (create, upload, attach, insert, template, preview): (1) capture the request the UI makes when the action is used WITH the password / with write rights; (2) REPLAY it holding only the bare share token (no password) and, separately, on a read-only / upload-only share; (3) a 2xx / "success" / a created object = the gate is not re-applied on that endpoint; (4) diff the endpoint's errors on an EXISTING vs non-existing filename ("File already exists" vs "Cannot create document") to turn it into a filename-existence oracle. Rule: **the share's password + permission bits must be re-checked on EVERY token-acting endpoint, not just the primary browse/download path — the create/attach endpoints that resolve the share by token but skip the gate give a filename oracle and an unauthorized write.** (Shared root with the attachment-read carrier: §17.777.)
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `2376909`, `lukasreschke`, `DocumentAPIController`, `richdocuments`, `getShareByToken`, `nodeExists`, `files drop`, `password protected share`, `enumerate valid files` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets (the sole `nodeExists` hit is an unrelated Nextcloud PHP path-traversal line at ledger 11050). The share/Nextcloud corpus REVIEWED as related-but-distinct: §17.124 / ledger 4307 (#1170024 federated-OCM `RESHARE_CHANGE_PERMISSION` — a share PERMISSION-REWRITE via an unauthenticated federation notification: different gate, different verb); §17.589 / ledger 17160 (#155618 Vimeo content-gate bypass via config-token leak + state-flag + alternate viewer — the umbrella "gate must be re-checked on every path" rule, but a single OBJECT's gate + a state-flag/viewer mechanism, not a share-collection's permission flags skipped on a create endpoint); the recorded "existence oracle" family (34 hits — the generic error-divergence oracle is owned; here it is the CONCRETE `nodeExists` carrier on a share create). Per the §17.740 / #3137895 (Nextcloud-Tables-ownership) delta precedent: DELTA carrier of the NEW §17.777 general method (share-gate not re-applied on secondary share-token endpoints) -> §17.777 (established at source 6's entry; this is the WRITE/ORACLE carrier).
FRESHNESS: created 2024-02-17, disclosed 2025-02-21 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (BACKFILL, flagged); read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no Nextcloud instance stood up this pass — knowledge intake only). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` body read IN FULL this pass (including the linked `DocumentAPIController.php` source excerpt the report reproduces). The higher-impact content-read rung is NOT claimed for this report (it is source 6's separate carrier).

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2376900  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/2376900.json` — HTTP 200 but `visibility: no-content`, `vulnerability_information` 0 B BY DESIGN; the credited public writeup = the vendor security advisory GHSA-gxph-5m4j-pfmj (team summary, author `nickvergessen`, verbatim: "Security advisory at https://github.com/nextcloud/security-advisories/security/advisories/GHSA-gxph-5m4j-pfmj"), fetched via `web_extract` this pass (Impact / Patches / CVE sections read IN FULL). Metadata: Nextcloud `text` app (Server / Nextcloud Enterprise), "Attachments folder for Text app is accessible on Files Drop/Password protected shares", reporter `lukasreschke`, created 2024-02-17, disclosed 2024-11-15, weakness Information Disclosure (CWE-200), severity low, CVE-2024-52513, patched Server 28.0.11 / 29.0.8 / 30.0.1 (Enterprise 25.0.13.13 / 26.0.13.9 / 27.1.11.9 …).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Nextcloud instance stood up; the method is the vendor advisory's own Impact statement read this pass).
CLASS: BAC / internal-file-read (CWE-200 exposure of sensitive information to an unauthorized actor) — a share's access gate (the password on a "Password protected" share; the upload-only restriction on a "Files drop" share) is enforced on the primary file-browse/download API but NOT re-applied on the Text-app ATTACHMENT sub-resource download, so attachments referenced inside Text documents are downloadable WITHOUT the password.
CORE METHOD (vendor advisory GHSA-gxph-5m4j-pfmj read IN FULL this pass; quotes verbatim):
  1. Impact (advisory verbatim): "After receiving a 'Files drop' or 'Password protected' share link a malicious user was able to download attachments that are referenced in Text files without providing the password."
  2. The gate being skipped: a Nextcloud share can be "Files drop" (upload-only — the visitor may add files but not read them) or "Password protected" (the whole share sits behind a password). Both gates are enforced on the primary browse/download path.
  3. The bypass: the Text app stores each document's embedded attachments (images/files referenced in the Text document) in a per-document attachments folder. That ATTACHMENT sub-resource has its own fetch URL which resolves the share by token but does NOT re-check the share password or the upload-only restriction -> anyone holding only the share LINK downloads the attachments.
  4. This is a READ of content (attachment file bytes) the share gate should have blocked — the confidentiality impact (unauthorized read of files inside a gated share), CWE-200. Fixed by re-applying the gate on the attachment path (upgrade 28.0.11 / 29.0.8 / 30.0.1).
DETECTION SIGNAL: any share-link / password-protected / upload-only file platform where a document/editor app stores per-object SUB-RESOURCES (attachments, embedded images, thumbnails, previews, temp/template files) in a separate folder with their own fetch URL; the tell is that these sub-resource URLs serve bytes when only the bare share token is supplied, while the primary file listing/download prompts for the password or refuses the read (upload-only).
CHAIN: attacker holds only a share LINK (no password) to a "Password protected" or "Files drop" (upload-only) share (primitive) -> opens/reads a Text document in the share (or otherwise learns its attachment references) -> requests the referenced ATTACHMENT sub-resource URL (primitive: the attachment path resolves the share by token) -> the attachment download does NOT re-check the share password / upload-only flag (bypass: the gate is enforced on the primary path, not on the sub-resource) -> the attacker downloads the attachment file bytes = unauthorized READ of content inside a password-protected / upload-only share (escalation: confidentiality breach past the gate; CVE-2024-52513, CWE-200). This is the READ half of the share-gate family; the WRITE/ORACLE half is this batch's source 4 (`DocumentAPIController#create`) — same root, different carrier. Program rated LOW (info disclosure); recorded at its rated weight.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any share-link / password-protected / upload-only file platform, for every per-object sub-resource (attachment, embedded image, thumbnail, preview, temp file): (1) obtain the share link and OPEN the primary browse/download API WITHOUT the password — confirm it gates (prompts for password, or 403 on an upload-only read); (2) enumerate the sub-resource fetch URLs (from the document/editor's network calls, the referenced attachment paths, or predictable per-document folders); (3) request each sub-resource URL with ONLY the bare share token (no password); (4) a 200 with file bytes = the sub-resource download skips the gate = unauthorized read of gated content. Rule: **the share's password and read/write/upload-only permission bits must be re-checked on EVERY sub-resource fetch (attachments, previews, embedded media), not just the primary file path — a per-document attachments folder with its own URL is a gate-bypassing read.** (Shared root with the create/write-oracle carrier: §17.777.)
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `2376900`, `lukasreschke`, `CVE-2024-52513`, `GHSA-gxph`, `text app`, `attachments folder`, `without providing the password`, `files drop` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets (the sole `text app` / `attachment.*password` hits are unrelated — the Word/XXE attachment-metadata corpus). The share/Nextcloud corpus REVIEWED as related-but-distinct: §17.124 (federated-OCM permission-rewrite — different gate/verb); §17.589 (Vimeo content-gate via config-token + state-flag + alternate viewer — the umbrella rule but a different mechanism); the recorded sub-resource / path-traversal families (a per-object attachments folder is a NEW carrier of the share-gate-bypass, not a path traversal). NEW general method (shared with source 4): a share's access gate enforced on the primary path but not re-applied on secondary / token-acting endpoints and per-object sub-resources -> §17.777.

STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no AEM instance exercised this pass; the payload and detection string are quoted verbatim from the public scanner's payload list and `scan.py`, not from the unreachable report body; the exact reflection context lives in the H1 body and was NOT invented).
CLASS: reflected XSS (CWE-79) via an AEM Sling RENDERING SELECTOR (`.childrenlist.html`) that reflects request-derived path/segment content into the HTML response without output encoding.
CORE METHOD (from the aem-xss scanner source + payload list, read IN FULL this pass; quotes verbatim):
  1. ENTRY POINT: AEM Sling selector `.childrenlist.html` (the "children list" / Coral-UI column-view renderer). URL shape: `https://target/<SEGMENT>.childrenlist.html`.
  2. PAYLOADS (the scanner's own list, verbatim): `CAPP<img src=x data'a'onerror=alert(domain)>.childrenlist.html` and `CAPP<br><br>please%20authenticate<br><br>.childrenlist.html`.
  3. DETECTION (scanner `scan.py`, verbatim): flag vulnerable when `response.status_code == 200` AND the body contains `<img src="x" data onerror="alert(domain)"/>` OR (`<br /><br />please authenticate<br /><br />` AND `data-coral-columnview-id`) — i.e. the injected `<img>` tag is reflected back with an ACTIVE `onerror` handler (attribute syntax normalized `src=x`->`src="x"`, `data'a'onerror`->`data onerror`, but the event handler SURVIVES), and the second marker identifies the Coral-UI column-view page (`data-coral-columnview-id`) keyed on the segment.
  4. MECHANISM (at the level the artifacts support): the `childrenlist` selector renders a child-listing/column-view page whose output echoes attacker-controlled path-segment content without neutralizing HTML -> a crafted `<img ... onerror=...>` executes in the AEM origin. (The precise sink template/field is in the login-gated H1 body and was not recoverable.)
DETECTION SIGNAL: an AEM / Sling / CMS "child list" / "column view" / "listing" RENDERING SELECTOR that echoes request-derived path/segment or node-name content into HTML; tell: request `.../MARKER.childrenlist.html` (and sibling rendering selectors) and grep the response for the MARKER rendered as LIVE HTML (not text) — a reflected `<img onerror>`/`<svg onload>` confirms it. Enumerate selectors: `.childrenlist`, `.list`, `.infinity`, `.tidy`, `.1.json`, `.json`, `.feed`, `.form` etc. on AEM content paths.
CHAIN: victim (AEM author / Adobe Connect user) opens the crafted `...childrenlist.html` URL (primitive: reflected XSS, requires user interaction) -> JS executes in the AEM/Adobe origin -> session-cookie / token theft of the logged-in author/admin -> account takeover / admin actions (standard XSS impact). In THIS report the host is a STAGING instance (cbconnection-stage.adobe.com) and the demonstrated impact is a reflected-XSS popup, rated LOW (CVSS 3.1) / P3-; the transferable value is the AEM selector probe, which on a production AEM AUTHOR/admin instance is admin-session XSS.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any AEM/Sling/CMS target, `GET https://target/CAPP<img src=x onerror=alert(document.domain)>.childrenlist.html` (URL-encoded) and check whether the response reflects the `<img onerror>` as live HTML; repeat across every rendering selector (`.childrenlist`, `.list`, `.infinity`, `.json`, ...) and content path. Rule: **enumerate a CMS's rendering/listing SELECTORS and test each for path/segment reflection into HTML — a `childrenlist`/column-view selector that echoes the request segment unencoded is reflected XSS.** (Carrier delta of the recorded reflected-XSS class — the new atom is the AEM Sling rendering-selector surface, NOT a new injection class.)

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2433634  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/2433634.json` -> HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 872 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked; the body quotes the vulnerable `info-message.js` source and the payload).
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — no GoCD instance exercised this pass; the sink code and payload are quoted verbatim from the live body).
CLASS: DOM XSS (CWE-79) — a query-string value is regex-extracted from `window.location.search`, `decodeURIComponent`-decoded, and injected into the DOM via jQuery `.html()` with no escaping; the `Utils.infoMessage` helper only wraps the value in `<p>` (cosmetic, NOT sanitization).
CORE METHOD (report body read IN FULL this pass; quotes verbatim):
  1. SINK: `gocd/gocd-analytics-plugin` `assets/js/pages/info-message.js#L28` (report links the source line). Verbatim code: `$(document).ready(function () { const msg = window.location.search.match(/[&?]msg=([^&]+)/); const msgText = msg ? decodeURIComponent(msg[1]) : "No data collected for this metric, cannot generate analytics."; $(document.body).html(Utils.infoMessage(msgText)); });`
  2. The `?msg=` value is pulled from `window.location.search` with a regex (`[&?]msg=([^&]+)`), `decodeURIComponent`-decoded into `msgText`, then passed to `Utils.infoMessage` which (report verbatim) "basically just wraps `msgText` in a `</p>`" — a cosmetic wrapper, NOT escaping — and the result is written with jQuery `.html()` (an HTML-injection sink).
  3. PAYLOAD (report verbatim): `?msg=%3Csvg%2Fonload%3Dalert%28%22XSS%22%29%20%3E` -> `<svg/onload=alert("XSS") >` injected into the webpage.
DETECTION SIGNAL: a client page that reads a query/hash/route value and writes it into `.html()` / `innerHTML` / `document.write`, ESPECIALLY when a "message"/"info"/"status" helper wraps the value in benign markup (`<p>`, `<div>`, `<b>`) that looks like sanitization but performs no escaping; the tell is the decoded attacker string appearing as LIVE HTML in the DOM (the rendered page shows the tag executed, not the literal text).
CHAIN: victim (GoCD admin/user) clicks a crafted analytics-plugin link `...?msg=<svg/onload=...>` (primitive: DOM XSS via query param, requires user interaction) -> JS runs in the GoCD analytics-plugin origin served by the GoCD server -> steals the victim's GoCD session / performs actions as them -> CI/CD pipeline access + secrets (the report's own impact verbatim: "An attacker can run malicious code on servers running this plugin, comprising the integrity and confidentiality of such servers"). Program rated Medium 4.3 (DOM XSS, privileges none, user-interaction required).
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any page that renders a status/info/message from a query or hash param, open `?msg=%3Csvg%2Fonload%3Dalert(document.domain)%3E`; if the marker executes or appears as live HTML (not text), it is DOM XSS. Watch for the false-sanitizer pattern: a helper that wraps the value in `<p>`/`<div>` before `.html()` — the wrapper does NOT neutralize tags. Enumerate every `decodeURIComponent(location.search/hash)` -> `.html()`/`innerHTML` path in the app's JS bundles. Rule: **a formatting/INFO helper that only wraps user input in benign markup before an HTML-injection sink (`.html()`, `innerHTML`) is not sanitization — decoded query/hash values flowing there are DOM XSS.**
DEDUPE/DELTA: `2433634`, `info-message.js`, `Utils.infoMessage`, `analytics plugin` = 0 hits across the three write targets. The 17 `gocd` hits are OTHER reports (GoCD `new.loading.page.html` `redirect_to` `javascript:` XSS #2419227 = §17.83, and the SonarSource GoCD pre-auth `/add-on/business-continuity` takeover) — NONE records the analytics-plugin `?msg=` DOM XSS. The DOM-XSS class is OWNED; per the ledger-5416 / batch-306-Revive precedent this is a CARRIER DELTA (a specific plugin sink + the cosmetic-wrapper false-sanitizer), recorded as an entry but NO §17 slot. Distinct from the §17.83 `javascript:`-URI redirect sink (#2419227) — different plugin file, different sink (HTML injection vs navigation), different payload class.
FRESHNESS: created 2024-03-25, disclosed 2024-03-27 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (BACKFILL, flagged); read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (see STATUS). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` body read IN FULL this pass. Knowledge-intake entry (carrier), not a report.

SOURCE: https://hackerone.com/reports/2112973  2026-11-13
ROUTE: live `curl https://hackerone.com/reports/2112973.json` — HTTP 200, `visibility: full`, `vulnerability_information` 781 B read IN FULL (FIRST route worked). Team summary credits the vendor advisory GHSA-8jwv-c8c8-9fr3 (Nextcloud). Metadata: Nextcloud Calendar / DAV, "Enabling Birthday Contact to any user", reporter `nvz`, created 2023-08-16, disclosed 2023-11-21, weakness IDOR (CWE-639), severity medium 4.3.
STATUS: UNVERIFIED (not reproduced — knowledge intake only; no Nextcloud instance exercised this pass; the request shape and the verb are quoted verbatim from the live body).
CLASS: IDOR / horizontal write-BAC (CWE-639) — a CalDAV custom-XML METHOD VERB that performs a state-changing WRITE, addressed to a calendar collection whose owner is taken from the path `{userId}` with NO ownership check, so a low-privilege user flips a setting on ANY user incl. Admin/SuperAdmin.
CORE METHOD (report body read IN FULL this pass; quotes verbatim):
  1. Entry point: `POST /remote.php/dav/calendars/{userId}` — the DAV calendar-collection route, where `{userId}` is the TARGET account (attacker-controlled path segment).
  2. Body = a namespaced CalDAV custom verb, verbatim: `<x3:enable-birthday-calendar xmlns:x3="http://nextcloud.com/ns"/>`.
  3. UI trigger (report verbatim): "Navigate to Calendar. At the very bottom find calendar settings. Click on `Enable Birthday Contacts`. Intercept the following request" — i.e. the UI action produces exactly the above POST, which is then replayed with a DIFFERENT `{userId}`.
  4. Missing gate: the `{userId}` path segment selects whose calendar the verb acts on, and there is NO ownership/authorization check that the caller may modify that user's calendar -> any userId (Admin/SuperAdmin included) is writable.
  5. Result (report Impact, verbatim): "Users with low privileges enable the \"Birthday Contacts\" feature for any user, including Admins and SuperAdmins."
DETECTION SIGNAL: a DAV/CalDAV/CardDAV endpoint where a state-changing action is delivered as a CUSTOM XML METHOD VERB in a POST/PROPPATCH body (a namespaced element like `<…:enable-birthday-calendar>`), and the target collection is chosen by a path segment (`/calendars/{userId}`, `/addressbooks/{userId}`, `/files/{username}`) with no ownership check; tell: replay the exact UI request with the path `{userId}` swapped to another account and a 2xx / the setting changes on the victim's resource.
CHAIN: low-privilege authenticated user (primitive: ordinary account) -> captures the "Enable Birthday Contacts" POST and swaps the path `{userId}` to an arbitrary victim (Admin/SuperAdmin) (primitive: the verb resolves the collection by path with no ownership check) -> the verb executes against the victim's calendar = a horizontal WRITE state-change on another user's account (impact: unauthorized integrity change on any account; here the action is low-value — flipping a birthday-calendar setting — so the program rated it Medium 4.3). The reusable escalation is the METHOD: the same path-segment + custom-verb probe reaches any more-sensitive DAV action (share/permission/contact writes) if present.
TRANSFERABLE TEST: on any DAV/CalDAV/CardDAV/WebDAV app, (1) trigger each calendar/contact/file action in the UI and capture the request; (2) identify any CUSTOM XML METHOD VERB (a namespaced `<ns:action>` element in a POST/PROPPATCH/MKCALENDAR body) and the path collection segment (`/calendars/{userId}`, `/addressbooks/{userId}`); (3) replay it with the path `{userId}`/`{username}` swapped to a second account you do NOT own; (4) a 2xx / the state change landing on the other account = write-IDOR. Rule: **a DAV custom-XML verb is a state-changing method whose target is the path collection — the owner of that collection must be re-checked on EVERY verb, not just the standard PROPFIND/GET; enumerate custom verbs and swap the path userId to find write-IDORs.** (Carrier delta of §3.1 / #3382343 "swap the identifier in a path parameter" — the new atom is the CalDAV custom-XML WRITE verb.)
DEDUPE/DELTA: tokens `2112973`, `nvz`, `enable-birthday-calendar`, `enable birthday`, `http://nextcloud.com/ns`, `x3:` = 0 technique hits across the three write targets (the sole `birthday` hit in Bugbountyskills.md is an unrelated member-fields line; the sole `CalDAV`/`PROPPATCH` hit is the unrelated #758978 Mail.ru WebDAV XXE). REVIEWED as related-but-distinct and NOT conflated: §3.1 / #3382343 (Nextcloud Out-of-Office API `userId` path param BOLA — a READ of another user's calendar data through a path param: the SAME path-param-swap root, but a READ verb) and the Nextcloud `remote.php/dav` corpus (§17.212/oCIS presigned-URL fail-open, #3382343 OOO, chunked-upload share-token BOLA, #1879549 WebDAV Basic-auth brute, file-lock) — none records a state-changing CalDAV CUSTOM XML VERB as the write primitive. Per the batch-306/307/308 carrier-delta precedent: DELTA carrier of the recorded "swap the path-parameter identifier -> cross-user access" family (§3.1) with a NEW atom (custom-XML write verb) -> entry recorded, NO §17 slot.
FRESHNESS: created 2023-08-16, disclosed 2023-11-21 — OUTSIDE the preferred 6-12 month window (BACKFILL, flagged); read because assigned.
HONESTY: not reproduced (no Nextcloud instance stood up this pass — knowledge intake only). Every quote above is verbatim from the live `.json` body (`vulnerability_information`, 781 B) read IN FULL this pass. The impact is the report's own (enable a birthday-calendar feature) — a low-value write; recorded at its rated weight (Medium 4.3), NOT inflated.
